# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

Please choose versions by [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

* MAJOR version when you make incompatible API changes,
* MINOR version when you add functionality in a backwards-compatible manner, and
* PATCH version when you make backwards-compatible bug fixes.

## Unreleased

- fix: **a subject asleep by design now renders `asleep-until:<instant>` at all three points that classify it, instead of `lapsed` / `unverifiable` / `waiting-on-human`.** The label differed each time because no point carried a reason for *why* a row is not moving. Measured 2026-11-26 over three subjects in one night: *Every Row That Should Be Open…* reads `SLEEPING until 2026-11-27` — which the fleet sweep already maps correctly to `sleeping` — and was rendered `unverifiable`, because the drive leg treats an empty `task_file` as unverifiable and a manager subject owns a goal page, not a task file; *Merge the Goal Manager Tier…* reads `UNARMED`, a state the sweep's `--state` row had **no cell for**, so it fell through to `lapsed`; *Manager Token Overhead Falls…* reads `OK` and was rendered `waiting-on-human` with nothing waiting. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` (`SLEEPING` → `asleep-until:<instant>` carrying the state line's own instant, plus a new `UNARMED` cell), `agents/fleet-drive.md` (a manager subject is no longer `unverifiable` for an absent `task_file`, and `CLOSERS class=none` on a date-gated subject is `asleep-until`, not `unverifiable`), `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` (a new `asleep-until:<instant>` cell evaluated before `waiting-on-human`), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.370.3

- fix: **the drive leg carries only the rows it can still nudge, and `share` counts this round's rows.** `retained_entries()` returned every ledger entry whose verdict was `blocked`/`unverifiable` — 737 of 2850 on 2026-11-26, 410 of them with no `task_file` (unprobeable by construction) and only 19 with a live session among the 323 that have one — so the round's `N candidates` header tracked the ledger's total size rather than the round's work. It now admits a row only when it has a `task_file` AND `session-liveness.py --check` returns LIVE (ABSENT and UNKNOWN both drop, because neither licenses a nudge); the ledger itself is untouched, `drive-ledger.py` staying its one writer. The second half: `drive-share.py measure()` admitted every `unverifiable` entry in the merged payload, including step 0's retained rows, so `share` swung `1/281` → `1/11` → `0/280` across three consecutive rounds; it now skips entries carrying a **non-empty** `reprobe_verdict` (a fresh row canonicalises to `""`, and `canonical()` emits the key on every entry). Change set: `scripts/drive-retained.py`, `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.370.2

- fix: **the closer lint no longer reds a day when a Monitor is only the watcher.** SC4's own clause permits the in-session watcher in the `(mechanism, id)` slot, but `session_bound_class()` read the whole `⏰ Ends:` body, so any closer naming a `Monitor` was classed `monitor` even when the awaited thing was an external observable. Measured 2026-11-25 on session `de5e925c`, `⏰ Ends: tag on \`bborbe/github-update-go-agent\` containing \`d0849b7a\`, observed by Monitor \`bkrc2l2og\``: the awaited thing is an external git tag, and the record red the day and restarted the goal's 7-day window. The classifier now strips the **watcher slot** — a `Monitor`, or a clause labelled `mechanism:` / `observed by` / `watched by` / `via` — before classifying, and keeps a slot naming a **producer** red (sub-agent, background agent, `run_in_background` shell, `CronCreate`, or a bare `(ScheduleWakeup …)` slot), because the awaited thing there IS that mechanism's output. A slot holding only filler (`⏰ Ends: nothing (Monitor x)`) falls back to the full body, so a closer waiting on the Monitor itself stays red. `scripts/closer-daily-reading.py` now re-derives each record's class from its stored `ends` with the lint's own function, so the fix reaches records already on disk; a record with no `ends` (every record before 2026-11-19, and every `blocked-retry`) keeps its stored class — the fail-closed direction. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/closer-daily-reading.py`, `docs/closer-gate-lint-reasons.md`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_daily_reading.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/` (27 rewritten to name the Monitor as the awaited thing; 55–59 new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.370.1

- fix: **the stand-down marker is honoured by ordering in both of its readers, so a row the operator re-flagged after stopping it opens again.** `/supervisor:stop` writes `stopped: true` + `stop_date`; `scripts/open-queue.py`'s `is_stopped()` and `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`'s resume decision both tested the field for truthiness and nothing else, so a deliberate re-flag could not reopen a row through the surface that actually reopens it. Measured 2026-11-24 on `Classify the Heat-Pump Penetration Seal`: flag cleared 17:10, stopped `18:05:33+01:00`, re-flagged `19:36:58.408271+01:00` — both readers still called the row stopped. The rule now lives once, in `scripts/standdown-order.py` (loaded by path, the way `worker-keepalive.py` loads `topic-keepalive.py`), and both readers delegate to it: a row is skipped only when `stop_date` is strictly newer than the row's raw `flag_set_at` — never `approval_instant()`'s `approved_at`-first fallback, which answers a different question. The re-flag must also be **operator-set** (`flag_provenance.flag_admits`): `flag_set_at` is written for an agent-set flag too, and an agent-set flag opens nothing in this repo, so an agent-set or unattributed flag reads as absent evidence and the stop stands — without that gate `worker-keepalive`, which has no admission check of its own, would resume a row the operator stopped on the strength of an agent's flag. Absent evidence never reopens a retired row: `stopped: true` with no `stop_date`, or with no parseable `flag_set_at`, keeps the presence-only skip, and a tie opens. Change set: `scripts/standdown-order.py` (new), `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_standdown_order.py` (new), `scripts/tests/fixtures/standdown-order/` (new — the two-page fixture pair), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.370.0

- fix: **a sessionless flagged row whose name a live session carries now reads LIVE, not `create`.** The SC3 hourly probe scored `private-personal/topic/Scratch Keep-Alive Fixture` as `liveness: none`, `branch: create` on 2026-11-24 while the tick reported the same row `already-live`: `liveness_of(None)` returns `none` for a row with no `claude_session_id`, and `_finish_row`'s name-based upgrade accepted only `ABSENT`, so the probe never asked and counted a working mechanism as a failure. The guard now covers `none` as well as `ABSENT` (`live in ("ABSENT", "none")`), and a new fixture class pins the new arm, the no-namesake control, the retained `ABSENT` arm, and the `--no-liveness` no-upgrade case. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- refactor: **the fleet round's keep-alive acting leg is retired; the wrapper's keeper is the sole keep-alive authority.** The router row was already gone (`0bfff171`), but `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`'s description still read *"Router row 5b of /supervisor:fleet-drive"* and `commands/steps/keepalive.md` still said *"When: every tick"* / *"one of the round's three acting legs"* — so a live Fleet Manager dispatched the actor every round and it wrote the digest's `keep-alive: unactioned:` counts. Deleted `commands/steps/keepalive.md` and `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` with the four sub-step files (`keepalive-topicless-goals.md`, `keepalive-stood-down.md`, `keepalive-stalled-tabs.md`, `keepalive-decision-parks.md`), removed the LEGS contract (`output-shape.md` item 12, the round tail's `--status`/`--check`, the sweep's seed), and dropped the actor from the two hand-back registries. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` (deleted), `commands/steps/keepalive*.md` (deleted), `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/sweep.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/check-handback-size.py`, `scripts/keeper-census.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_size.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.369.0

- feat: **a daily SC4 reading over the closer-gate-lint records, replacing an `exit 0` that certified nothing.** The goal's `# Readings` series recorded a bare `closer-gate-lint.py` run; with no payload on stdin the Stop hook lints nothing and exits 0, so every such reading certified a clean day without looking at a single closer. `scripts/closer-daily-reading.py` reads `~/.claude/state/closer-gate-lint/*.jsonl` for one calendar day and prints `<date> closers=<n> session-bound=<n> sessions=<n> coverage=<n>/<m>`: `closers=` counts records with a non-null `closer` — every line the hook found a `👤 You:` on; `no-closer` is the only reason that leaves it null, and `not-a-gate` records are counted because the session-bound check runs before the gate checks (2 672 of the day's 3 324), and `session-bound=` counts **distinct sessions** carrying a non-null `class`, distinct because `blocked-retry` re-emits the original class and a record count reports one session twice (measured 2026-11-24: 3 records, 2 sessions). Exit 2 = a session-bound observable, 3 = no records for the day, 4 = coverage below the 0.9 floor against the census `sessions`, or no usable denominator at all (absent, or zero/negative — against a zero population `sessions < 0` is never true, so that denominator would certify every day clean); only 0 certifies, so a day can never be read clean by a warning that exits 0. `--root` exists so two builds can be compared on ONE frozen snapshot: the same day's live directory grew 2 321 → 3 324 closers between two reads, so a live-vs-live comparison cannot tell a real mismatch from records added since. Measured on the closed day 2026-11-24: `closers=3324 session-bound=2 sessions=156 coverage=156/30`, exit 2 — the day is NOT clean, and the first honest reading of it says so. Change set: `scripts/closer-daily-reading.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_daily_reading.py`, `docs/closer-gate-lint-reasons.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.368.0

- feat: **the goal-manager tier is merged into the topic manager.** The four-tier tree (fleet → topic → goal → worker) becomes three (fleet → topic → worker): a topic manager now drives each goal it declares directly, so there is no goal-manager session. `scripts/owner.py` resolves a goal to its declaring topic's manager (a goal declared by a topic is owned by that topic; a goal in no topic still falls to the fleet manager); the keep-alive chain drops from 7 rows to 5; `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `commands/read-guides.md` state three tiers, and the `goal-*` commands are the topic tier's goal scope. Change set: the full PR (44 files) — `scripts/owner.py`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/topicless-goal-keepalive.py`, `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, the topic/goal/keepalive command and step files, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/subject-resolution.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `commands/read-guides.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, and the matching tests.

## v0.367.7

- fix: **`close-worker.py` now closes a session that no page carries, instead of refusing it.** The verb resolved a session by `claude_session_id`, then `metrics_sessions`, then a goal/topic anchor, and refused `task-unresolved` when none matched — which is the state of any second session working a task another session already recorded: a stray tab opened by a batch, or a headless worker spawned against an already-owned row. `reap-idle-headless.py` (since v0.303.1) and `close-idle-tab.py` already close exactly that shape as `unanchored-closed`; this verb was the last close path that refused it, so an idle finished session had no sanctioned close at all and the fleet rule forbids the only lever left. New `unanchored_verdict()` splits the two facts `task-unresolved` was carrying: an **incomplete** search (the session's own vault declares no `tasks_dir`, or no directory was searched) still refuses — without that guard an empty `vault-cli` config would read every session as unanchored and close the whole fleet in one tick — while a **complete** one closes as `unanchored-closed`, printed before the `🧹` header the same way a refusal token is. The proof is taken over exactly the directories the caller's own lookup searched — `task_search_dirs` (the root-wide task-name probe plus the configured scan) for this verb, `fleet_task_dirs` for `close-idle-tab.py` — because a proof wider than the search declares an unread task directory empty, and one narrower than it proves absences over a subset. That pairing was wrong in the first cut of this change and is what the PR review caught: `find_tasks` read only the configured set while the proof spanned `fleet_task_dirs`, so a task page in a vault `vault-cli` config does not name resolved to nothing and the wider list made the absence look proven — a live anchored session could be closed. `find_tasks` now also probes the task names root-wide (matching `find_pages`, which already probed the goal/topic names that way), and a regression test pins it. A second review round closed the remaining hole in the same proof: `_vault_config()` returned `{}` on a failed read, which made the own-vault check vacuously false while the root-wide probe kept the directory list non-empty — so a session anchored in a configured vault whose `tasks_dir` uses a non-standard name (reachable only through the config) was declared unanchored and closed. `vault_config_error()` now records why the read failed and `unanchored_verdict` refuses while it is set, before it consults the directory list. `close-idle-tab.py` now calls the same helper rather than carrying its own copy of the predicate. Every other guard is untouched and runs first: `busy-target`, `held-session`, `dirty-worktree` and `gate-held` all precede resolution, so a busy or gate-held session still refuses and an anchored session with a live task still refuses `task-not-terminal`. Measured 2026-11-24 on two sessions that had no sanctioned close: `605be6d3` (pid 59145, `sdk-cli`, idle) refused `task-unresolved` here and `wrapper-busy: wrapper pending_turns 18` from the reaper, and `e1fbf025` (pid 19266, `cli`, idle) refused `task-unresolved`. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/close-idle-tab.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.367.6

- fix: **the drive leg no longer routes an orphan row to the removed `keepalive.md` row 5b.** `agents/fleet-drive.md:48` read *"cause `orphan` — **resume it** (`keepalive.md` 5b)"* — a live instruction to the driving model to resume through a step file the round no longer dispatches, in an agent the round runs **every** round. Found 2026-11-24 by a closure probe that was itself too narrow: the probe covered `commands/fleet-drive.md` and `agents/fleet-round.md` and printed nothing, because `agents/fleet-drive.md` was outside its file list and the reference is the bare `` `keepalive.md` `` form rather than `commands/steps/keepalive.md`. The disposition now says the resume belongs to the wrapper's keep-alive tick, which owns the leg end to end, and that a round resuming it would be the second writer the tick's claim guard exists to defend against. Change set: `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.367.5

- fix: **a row carrying a registered future `park_event` is no longer a nudge candidate.** `park-event.py register` already bound the wait to an anchored task and wrote a `defer_date`, so re-asking it to park every round only burns a model turn for the whole wait. `park-nudge.py` now excludes a row whose sid is a member of a park watch whose `recheck_at` is in the FUTURE, records the skip as a `park_event_rows` entry (sid + future recheck instant) plus a `<n> park-event-skipped` count, and places the gate BEFORE the unmeasured-`watching_min` skip so a row the instrument cannot measure this round is still excluded by its park event. An EXPIRED park event is not a park — the row is due and stays a candidate, so a blanket exclusion fails the two-row fixture. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.367.4

- fix: **a reap is drafted only when the task page owns the session being reaped.** The sweep resolved a session to a task page and read the page's terminal state as the session's verdict, so a COMPLETED task whose body merely mentioned a live manager's id reaped that manager. Measured 2026-11-23, round `1795464184.784114`: `A Worker That Dies Mid-Turn Becomes Unresumable.md` (`completed · done · 0 open`, frontmatter stamp `7daf8265…`) carries the sentence `claude_session_id: c49298a8-… on [[Claim Scheduling Is Hardened]]` 18 050 bytes into its `# Tasks` prose; the join read that as a stamp, classified the live `Claim Scheduling Is Hardened Manager` (`c49298a8…`, a goal at 2/3 SC) as `finished — reap candidate`, and sent it a reap message pointing at `/exit`. `close-worker.py` refused with `task-not-terminal=1`, so no session was closed — the guard held and the message had already gone. New `session-ids.py --task <file> --owns <session-id>` answers the join from the page's FRONTMATTER id set (`OWNS` exit 0 · `NOT-OWNS` exit 3 · full id only, so a prefix fails closed), and both legs now require it: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 8 refuses the `finished` class on `NOT-OWNS` — the row is `unverifiable`, with a `NOTES` clause naming the page's own stamp — and `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 1b refuses to draft the reap. Step 4 states the rule both enforce: a `claude_session_id:` in a page's BODY is prose, never a stamp. Change set: `scripts/session-ids.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_ids.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.367.3

- fix: **the keep-alive leg's hand-back is its last emission.** `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` now names `SubagentHandback` as the terminal action and forbids anything after it — no `SendMessage`, no heading, summary or trailing note — and states that a late-landing digest is corrected by calling the tool again rather than by messaging `main` and narrating. Measured 2026-11-24 on session `893df368`: 36 of 39 keepalive delegates complied (410–779 B), and 2 of the 3 over-cap ones were exactly this shape — a correction `SendMessage` plus a prose `Final state` block naming the round, which `check-handback-size.py` counts under its `ROUND_ID` regex (1 973 B and 1 109 B). The `<output_format>` "two artifacts" framing and its separate Digest paragraph are gone: the digest is written by `fleet-legs.py` and never reported, so the hand-back is the only thing the leg emits. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.367.2

- test: **the census's goal-page attribution gains its positive control.** A goal manager opened by the flagged tick used to depend on its own `/vault-cli:work-on-goal` start turn for the page stamp, and that turn can never run — measured 2026-11-23 20:51:49 in `~/Library/Logs/claude-wrapper/stderr.log`, the start command was not sent ("the keystrokes reached the pty but the Enter did not take effect"), so the page kept no `claude_session_id` while its manager ran. The wrapper now stamps the page at open time, before its start commands run, and this test pins the census half: a goal page carrying its session id, with that session live, emits no `live session <sid> — no page records it` line and no `MISMATCH page`, **and** the same live session with the page's id removed IS reported — the control that proves the absence assertion can fail. Change set: `scripts/tests/test_keeper_census.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.367.1

- fix: **the fleet keeper no longer cards an ABSENT death for a manager it restarted itself.** `main()`'s `if live == "ABSENT":` branch reads `sid = fleet_session()` — the id the fleet manager's registration names — and a `restart fresh` retires that session and starts a new one whose registration lands a moment later. The branch also returned before `load_lapse()`, so the record proving the keeper caused the death (`fresh_consumed`, written by `act_on_fresh_answer` immediately before it runs `restart-worker.py --reason lapse --fresh-prompt /supervisor:fleet-loop`) was never read on the carding path. Measured 2026-11-16: the operator answered `restart fresh` at `15:20:21Z`, the keeper stopped the manager at `15:30:15Z` and started the replacement at `15:30:24Z`, then minted an ABSENT card at `15:30:33Z` asking the operator to act on the death they had just authorised. The guard matches the **session the keeper stopped**: `fresh_sid` is written only when the restart succeeds, and the branch suppresses only while `sid == fresh_sid`, so a death of the REPLACEMENT manager — or of any session the keeper did not stop — still cards, and a refused or failed restart arms nothing. It is bounded by `RESUME_DEFER_MAX`; `fresh_first_try` and `fresh_sid` persist in the lapse record because the ABSENT branch runs in a later process. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.367.0

- feat: **the fleet round drops its keep-alive and reap rows.** Both legs now run in the `claude-wrapper` keepalive tick, so `commands/fleet-drive.md` and `agents/fleet-round.md` no longer carry rows 5b, 6 and 16b, and the two split lines that named them are retargeted. Rows are removed by LABEL, not line number — the pins had rotted twice and pointed at rows 5d/7/17-18 at `origin/master`. ⚠️ **`agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` is NOT deleted in this change:** retiring it fails the repo's own `check-handback-keys` and `check-handback-size` guards until every one of the 8 step files that dispatch it is re-pointed, so the retirement belongs with that scrub rather than here.

## v0.366.13

- fix: **a dangling `goals:` link is reported instead of dropped.** `goal_owners()` used to `continue` past an unopenable page, so a declared link whose page was missing left the owners list empty — the row then looked goal-less and the fleet leg resumed it with nothing said, indistinguishable from a task that declares no goal at all. It now delegates to `owner.py`'s `of_subject()`, the module that already rules an established absence `not-owner` (`test_owner.py` *"absence that was ESTABLISHED stays not-owner"*) and an unreadable page `cannot-tell`, and `main()` appends every `not-owner` answer to the reason line — line 2, not a third line, because `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` slices `lines[:2]` into its TSV row and would have discarded a note printed below it — so the broken link is visible in the leg's own output. The unreadable case is upgraded as a side effect: it was dropped into `[]` — a fleet resume of a worker whose goal manager was merely unmeasurable, the double-writer the module exists to withhold — and is now `cannot-tell`. The crash-loop card gate now asks "did any goal resolve an OWNER" rather than "is the list empty", which a `not-owner` entry would otherwise have inverted for exactly the row no manager can own. Change set: `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.12

- fix: **a manager can no longer settle an operator-tier permission gate through `attention-answer.py`.** `cmd_answer_permission` posted every verdict with no tier read at all, so a hand-built `attention-answer.py answer <id> --decision allow --by manager` released a prompt the classifier rates `operator`. Measured 2026-11-23 06:37:51Z on item `aecf9478…`: `answered_by: manager`, `resolved_by 070129ad…`, a `Bash: … git -C $R show … | nl | sed` payload the classifier rates `operator` (form `secret`). `gate-answer-check.py` had read the tier since 2026-11-18, but this row never went through it — it reached `attention-answer.py` directly. The branch now classifies the card payload with the same `permission-tier.py` the placement measurement uses and refuses a non-operator arm on an operator-tier card with a named reason, before the store is written; only the operator's own surfaces (`--by operator`, `attention-board`, `supervisor:attention-next`) may answer one, and an unreadable classifier or a payload naming no tool fails closed to `operator`. Change set: `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.11

- fix: **the fleet round records the refusal token it already holds, and the freeable-tabs check reports it.** `commands/steps/drive.md` step 3b-iii now writes `refused<TAB><name><TAB><token>`, keeping `closed<TAB><name>` at two fields. `scripts/check-freeable-tabs-acted.py` `acted_names()` splits on every tab and joins on `fields[1]` alone, so a three-field line no longer collapses to `<name> <token>` through `_norm_name`'s whitespace fold and report `missing`; measured 2026-11-22 on a copy of a real record, the pre-change parse read `acted 1 · missing 1` on a three-field refusal against `acted 2 · missing 0` on the identical two-field control, and 40 such entries exist on disk. The check's line gains a `refused: <token>=<n>` tally bucketed by the token's first word and printed unconditionally (`(none)` when empty), so the token is observable rather than parsed and discarded, and `commands/steps/output-shape.md` carries the new shape, with `OUTPUT_SHAPE_MARKER` widened to the new tail so a doc that drops it fails the static contract. Change set: `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `scripts/check-freeable-tabs-acted.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_freeable_tabs_acted.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.10

- fix: **the fleet keep-alive leg no longer drops a topic page the stood-down handover just cleared.** § 1's population was filtered on `claude_session_id` — the exact field the handover clears — so a page a close had just handed over left the population and no replacement manager was ever opened for it, stranding any topic that was still re-manageable. The selection now lives in `scripts/topic-candidates.py`, which asks `scripts/owner.py` — the one ownership definition — and refuses only `loop-stopped`, so a handed-over page that is **not itself stood down** stays a candidate, while a page still carrying its `.stopped` marker reads `loop-stopped` and is still never re-opened (clearing a stand-down is the operator's act, never this leg's). The inline `python3 -c` heredoc in the actor is gone, the two docs that asserted the old behaviour are amended, and `scripts/tests/test_topic_candidates.py` pins both directions (a page carrying no session id is a candidate; a `STOPPED` page is not). Change set: `scripts/topic-candidates.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_candidates.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive-stood-down.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the keep-alive leg now prints the session id it opened, on the line that names the page.** § 1 printed the page and its pre-act verdict only, so the opened id surfaced on a *later* run — and a hand-arm with `/supervisor:open --topic` produces the identical page stamp, which made the leg's own act indistinguishable from the operator's. Each acted row now renders `<name>\t<verdict>\topened=<full session id>`, the id taken from `/supervisor:restart`'s or `/supervisor:open`'s own verdict in that round, never read off the page afterwards; `jump` / `leave` / `cannot-tell` render `opened=none — <reason>`. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.9

- docs: **`--topic-open` now has a written invocation home.** The flag is invoked by the wrapper's open endpoint (`bborbe/claude-wrapper` `pkg/open_tab.go:292-297`, `--topic-open --hand-run`), but `commands/open.md` stopped carrying it in `dcd2d991` — that commit made `open.md` a thin caller of the wrapper endpoint, and `scripts/check-open-hand-run.py` now forbids `topic-spawn.py` there — so the only reference to the *invocation* was a grep-negative. `commands/topic-spawn.md` gains a `## Opening a topic's manager — --topic-open` section: the invocation (with `--hand-run`), the `open-ok: <topic>` / refusal-token verdict, and the ≥300 s Bash-call timeout `commands/open.md:243-246` already mandates. Change set: `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.8

- docs: **the keep-alive leg may no longer state a capacity comparison it did not obtain from a cap instrument.** Measured 2026-11-09: the leg reported `52 live workers against a hard cap of 50` and skipped all 57 dead-worker resumes, while the cap denominator that round read **31** — and at 31 the wrapper's own refusal already named the soft cap correctly. The 52 was the leg's own count, drawn from a wider population (`list_agents`, `live-workers.py`, the raw registry entry count), none of which is the cap denominator. `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` §4 now licenses exactly two forms, both **read rather than composed**: a refused resume quotes the wrapper's refusal verbatim, and a reported capacity is `workers: <live>/<target>` from `worker-sessions.py --count` + `worker-target.py`, whose single home stays `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 1. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.


## v0.366.7

- fix: **a crash-loop posts no attention card, because its recommended option was the no-op the system already performs.** `post_card()` hardcoded `--recommend "keep stopped"`, and a crash-looping row is already stopped, so every card asked the operator to ratify what happens anyway — 189 `crash-loop:` cards in 24 h (11.3 % of the board), none answered, three dedup keys re-minted 62 times each at a 19-minute median gap. A new `card_is_noop()` predicate gates the mint, so a card whose recommendation equals the row's current state is not posted; the `crash-loop:` row in the fleet leg's hand-back remains the channel. This **supersedes the routing #1056 (v0.362.9) added** for a task with no resolvable goal page — that card is a no-op whatever the row's parentage — and the test that pinned it is rewritten rather than deleted. `recommend`/`current` are parameters so the guard is a filter and not a blanket suppression, and both directions are pinned. Change set: `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_keepalive.py`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.6

- docs: **the `clock` durable-observable comment now names only forms the arm actually rejects, states that the arm is prefix-bounded, and records the separator limitation.** Three residuals left by #730: the comment listed `2026-11-09T15` and `2026-11-09T15:30abc` as rejected though both CLEAR (backtracking to a `YYYY-MM-DDThh` prefix), said nothing about that prefix-bounding, and left the parser-accepted-but-blocked separator family (`2026-11-09X15:30`, `2026-11-09123` read as 23:00) unstated. The comment now names `2026-11-09abc` / `2026-11-0912` as the genuine rejections, documents the prefix-bounded looseness, and records the separator limitation as deliberately deferred; a new `test_the_clock_comment_names_only_genuinely_rejected_forms` reads the comment text so a green suite cannot pass over a lying comment, and `test_an_iso_datetime_is_a_clock_observable` pins `2026-11-09X15:30` as staying blocked. Comment and tests only — the `clock` regex is unchanged. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.5

- fix: **`close-worker.py` closes a live session when the session is bound to two tasks — one finished, one still being worked.** `_grep_task` returned on the FIRST `claude_session_id` match under `sorted(paths)`, so a session holding a `completed` row and an `in_progress`/`execution` row closed on whichever sorted first; measured 2026-11-09 on session `f8544288`, whose reap closed it off the completed row while a second task was still live. The close path now collects every task the session holds (`find_tasks` / `_grep_tasks`) and refuses `task-not-terminal` naming the live task when any is non-terminal (`held_task_gate`); a task reached only through `metrics_sessions` stays the documented hand-off case and does not refuse. A hit suppresses a failed-search error — only an empty set from a failed lookup refuses, the rule `find_task_checked` already applies — so a host without `ripgrep` does not refuse every close on the wider pass's 19.6 s timeout. The task search's failure is consulted LAST: a goal or topic page that resolves the session still decides, so a goal-only session is not refused over a task-directory error. `find_task`, `find_task_checked` and `_grep_task` keep their first-candidate contract for their five other importers (`restart-worker.py`, `reap-idle-headless.py`, `close-idle-tab.py`, `park-nudge.py`, `task-standdown.py`), and the grep invocation they share moves to `_session_grep` rather than a copied second matcher. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.4

- fix: **a dead `SITE_LIST` entry no longer names a deleted watcher script.** `scripts/transport-read-check.py` enumerated `("manager-attention-watch.py", "pane_for_session_name")`, but that script is deleted and the function exists nowhere live — `git grep pane_for_session_name` matches only that entry and a `CHANGELOG-archive.md` line. Its replacement needs no new entry: `manager-wake.py`'s `find_pane` does the join, and its own docstring states the read is `jump.py`'s `wezterm_panes`, which the list already enumerates and drives. A dead entry is not harmless here — it claims a mechanism nothing can fail, the "enumerated but never driven" gap the list exists to catch. Change set: `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.366.3

- docs: **the prose that still described `manager-attention-watch.py` as live now names what replaced it — the manager-wake job, or the delivery arm.** The script was deleted when its behaviour moved in-process (`v0.366.0`), but 22 mentions across 15 files still sent a reader following a comment to code that no longer exists. Each now names the replacement rather than a generic noun, and the last surviving mention of the deleted filename in a docstring is removed rather than wrapped in a parenthetical. Comments and docstrings only — no executable line changed, and `git diff --stat` is the 22/22 signature of an in-place substitution. ## v0.366.2

- docs: **the fleet round's INLINE rules carry the ownership fallback, not just the prohibition.** `commands/fleet-drive.md`'s INLINE list said *"Never answer another manager's workers — route to the owning manager"* without the rule that a subject with no lower-tier manager is the fleet manager's, so a manager read the prohibition and missed who owns an unmanaged subject — measured 2026-11-22, when two live topics were described as *unmanaged* and the operator corrected it (*"They are not unmanaged. Every session has a manager as long as the fleet manager runs."*). The list gains one bullet naming the fleet manager as the owner and citing `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Which manager owns a subject; `commands/steps/rules.md:28` no longer sends a no-goal worker to the operator — it routes to the owning manager per the same section, leaving the two-page and foreign-goal branches escalating. ## v0.366.1

- fix: **the hand-back stops at its last required line — stated in the contract and in the four legs that broke it.** `agents/fleet-round.md` has said *emit the fence and NOTHING else* since v0.356.x; the other legs carry only *≤ 1 KB, payload off the hand-back*, so four of them appended a prose `Notes:` block after their counts. Measured 2026-11-21 on a live Fleet Manager's first 8 closed rounds after `v0.362.4` loaded: **fleet-keepalive-actor 10 of 20 over cap (1 235–2 563 B), fleet-sweep-reader 8 of 20 (1 089–1 690 B), fleet-drive 9 of 20 (1 052–2 194 B), round-readings-reader 1 of 1 (1 811 B)** — while `fleet-round` complied 8/8 at 511–514 B. The rule is added to `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md` and `agents/round-readings-reader.md`, and stated once in `docs/handback-contract.md` § The shape for the remaining legs. `check-budget.py` governs the agent bodies: `fleet-keepalive-actor` is unbaselined and hard-capped at 30 720 B, so its addition is paid for by compressing the adjacent payload/truncation prose (final **30 701 B**); `fleet-sweep-reader` and `fleet-drive` are deliberate growth, so their `scripts/budget-baseline.json` entries move with them (final **46 727 B** and **50 006 B**, matching the shipped tree). Change set: the four agent files, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.366.0

- feat: **no manager arms a `Monitor` for the attention feed any more — the per-session watcher that fed it is deleted.** ⚠️ **"The four are gone" would overclaim:** `answered-watch.py` and `permission-answer-poll.py` were deleted earlier (#1036, #1047), `manager-attention-watch.py` and its two suites go here, and **`gate-owner-filter.py` is deliberately RETAINED** as a library for `round-readings.py` — so this PR deletes one script, not four, and the filter stays on disk with no arming site. `attention-controller`'s in-process wake job watches the store and types `attention gate: <item-id>` into the manager's own pane, so the wake is a line in the pane rather than a poller's stdout. `commands/fleet-drive.md` row 13, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` and `commands/steps/round-tail.md` no longer arm anything, and `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py` plus its two suites are deleted. ⚠️ **The ownership filtering the arm did is not lost — it moved in-process**, and every branch has its own test: peer drop and dead-manager keep (`keeps()`), the operator's own `role: human` row (`#318`), another goal's gate (`#321`, reading the vault's stamps), and the line shape both the job and `agents/gate-answerer.md` now agree on. The dispatch rule keeps `hst:` beside `attention gate:` because a manager may still hold an older feed line in flight; nothing produces `hst:` any more.

## v0.365.1

- fix: **`manager-receipt.py --start` prints the round id (`round=<stem>`), and `round-dispatch.md` names it as the only source.** Round artifacts are named by the six-decimal stem, but `--start` printed only the slug, so the manager rebuilt the id itself. Measured 2026-11-21: one round passed an ISO timestamp, another the receipt float's 7-decimal repr (`1795255389.1027682` against artifacts under `1795255389.102768`), and the tail's `fleet-legs.py --status` read `UNKNOWN`. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.365.0

- feat: **the fleet round's adopt leg is gone — the wrapper's adopt job replaces it.** Row 4 is removed from `commands/fleet-drive.md` and `agents/fleet-round.md`, `commands/steps/adopt.md` is deleted, and the render loses its Adoption item (the remaining items renumber, with every live cross-reference shifted: `item 13` → `12` for the `Legs` block, `item 12` → `11` for the round's debt). `claude-wrapper` v0.50.0 runs the same job in-process on the flagged tick's cadence, logging `job=adopt action=tick adopted=<n> skipped=<n> adopted_sessions=<ids>`, so the round no longer spends a leg on it. ⚠️ **`git grep 'adopt-orphans' -- commands/` now returns no match** — `commands/steps/readings.md`'s note about the population is repointed at the wrapper's adopt tick. The Python `scripts/adopt-orphans.py` stays: it is the reference implementation the Go port was written against and still runs by hand, but nothing in the round invokes it. Change set: `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `commands/steps/adopt.md` (deleted), `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `agents/fleet-round.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/fleet-legs.py`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/*`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.364.3

- docs: **`permission-channel.py`'s header no longer names the script it was extracted from.** The two mentions were the last retired-script references in a file created by this extraction, so rewording them removes the module from the set the deletion criterion has to clear. The behaviour is unchanged and nothing else in the file moved.

## v0.364.2

- fix: **the gate-answerer dispatch now fires on the wake job's own line shape, not only on the feed's.** `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` keyed the dispatch on `hst:<item-id>`, which is what `gate-owner-filter.py --feed` prints — while `attention-controller`'s in-process wake job types `attention gate: <item-id>` into the manager's pane (`managerWakeLinePrefix`). The rule would have gone **silently inert** the moment the feed arm is retired: the wake still arrives, and nothing answers it. Both shapes are now named, with the reason, so the arm removal cannot disable the dispatch without failing a read of this file. ⚠️ **The dispatch alone was not enough — the review caught that the agent it dispatches to still accepted only `hst:` and would have reported the new shape `skip | tab gate`**, the same silent-inert outcome one hop later; `agents/gate-answerer.md` now takes both token shapes too.

## v0.364.1

- refactor: **the permission-channel reading moves into `permission-channel.py`, so `round-readings.py` no longer imports `manager-attention-watch.py`.** The watcher and the round both need the same window, staleness bound and abort reader, and the round was reaching them by loading the whole watcher — the last live module load of a script that is being deleted, so deleting it would have broken `permission_channel_sid` on every manager round. The **policy still differs per caller** and is now stated in one place: the round applies `PERM_CHANNEL_STALE_S`, the watcher deliberately does not, because a stale-id bound would un-mark exactly the dead-channel session that cause exists for. `manager-attention-watch.py` re-exports every moved name unchanged, so no other caller had to move. Verified: `python3 -m unittest test_round_readings test_manager_attention_watch` → **261 tests, OK**.

## v0.364.0

- feat: **the fleet round reads the wrapper's page buckets as a SECOND layer, and never merges it with its session classes.** The step-8 classes answer *is this session doing anything*; the wrapper's page buckets answer *what state is this record in* — different domains, so step 8c enumerates the gate's subjects from `/api/1.0/sweep/subjects`, reads them in one `sweep-buckets.py` call, and renders a `PAGES` line in the digest plus a `pages=` key in the hand-back. Two rules are load-bearing rather than incidental: bucket names are echoed **verbatim**, so a value the round has never seen still appears as served (folding it into `other` would make the output indistinguishable from a round that re-derived its own classification — the defect the route removes), and the two layers are **never** collapsed into one key, because one field two readers interpret differently is worse than two fields. An unreachable wrapper prints `UNKNOWN (read failed)`, never `(none)`, which would read as "no pages tracked". The fixed-section allowance moves 49 → 50. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.363.0

- feat: **`sweep-buckets.py` reads the wrapper's sweep buckets, so the round can stop deriving them.** `GET /api/1.0/sweep?subject=<name>` has served the gate's rows already classified since the route landed, and **nothing in this repo called it** — so the fleet round kept re-deriving the same classification from the same inputs, and two derivations of one fact can disagree about one row. The script reads one or more subjects, merges their rows, and prints `pages=<bucket>=<count> …`. Three rules are load-bearing rather than incidental: counting is keyed on the **raw bucket string** the wrapper sent, so a value this script has never seen appears verbatim instead of being folded into `other` (a fold would make the output indistinguishable from a local re-derivation, which is the one thing it must be able to disprove); a subject that failed is reported in `failed=` and never silently dropped, because a partial read that reads as a complete one is a false-quiet — and that count is **per READ, not per distinct subject**, so `--subject X --subject X` with both reads failing reports `failed=2` and exits 1 rather than collapsing the repeat and returning success on a total failure (the defect review caught); and a row with no `bucket` fails its subject with a named reason rather than being counted under an invented key. The base URL follows the existing `SUPERVISOR_WRAPPER_URL` convention, which is what lets a probe point the caller at a second daemon instead of stubbing the live one — it must be an **origin with no path**, and a redirect is refused rather than followed, both matching `deliver-message.py` / `manager-liveness.py`. ⚠️ **What it returns is PAGE buckets, not session verdicts** — a page bucket answers *what state is this record in*, a session verdict answers *is this session doing anything* — so a caller that needs both keeps a separate `sessions=` layer rather than merging them into one key. Change set: `scripts/sweep-buckets.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_sweep_buckets.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.13

- refactor: **`gate-answer-check.py` stops loading `gate-owner-filter.py`, and `owner_of` joins the other ownership readers in `session-ownership.py`.** The filter was already a non-dependency in substance — the four readers moved in `v0.362.10` — but this loader still pointed at it, so deleting the file would have raised `ImportError` on every manager round. ⚠️ **`round-readings.py`'s three `_load` sites are deliberately NOT repointed:** it needs the filter's store and log readers (`headless_items_checked`, `log_items`, `session_for_pane`), and repointing them broke 16 tests in `test_round_readings` alone — so the file stays as a library for it and only `gate-answer-check.py`'s one site moves. ⚠️ **`live_ids` moved too, and only a real invocation found that it had to:** the repoint was opened without it and the review caught the result — `load_inputs` calls `filt.live_ids()` at `:747`, so every real run raised `AttributeError` while `make precommit` stayed green, because the suite mocks `load_inputs` wholesale. The regression is now covered by running the function itself (`load_inputs()` returns `live` as a real set) and by the symbol check in `test_session_ownership`. Verified: `make precommit` EXIT=0.

## v0.362.12

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` is a thin caller of the wrapper's open endpoint, and a hand-run spawn into the Hold window or of an agent row is refused.** The command carried its own spawn instructions — six `wezterm cli spawn` lines, `spawn_agent` shapes, the role→window jq block and the manager-launcher/topic-spawn recipes — so each session re-derived the routing and 13 `role: agent` rows were opened as tabs in the operator's Hold window on 2026-11-18. Every routing decision (role→mode, the answer-path probe, the headless resume, the tab window, the caps) now lives in the wrapper's `POST /api/1.0/open` and the `open_rows` MCP tool; this command resolves the name, applies its own pre-call holds, sends one request and renders one verdict per row. `agent → Agents` is gone from `resolve-launcher.py` and `flagged-rows.py` (the window was removed from `~/.config/wezterm` on 2026-11-12, so the mapping silently fell through). New `scripts/spawn-route-guard.py` (PreToolUse on Bash, fail-open, kill switch `SUPERVISOR_SPAWN_ROUTE_GUARD=off`) refuses a `wezterm cli spawn` into the role map's `Hold.window_id` — read live, never hard-coded — and a `--resume` of a task whose `role:` is `agent`. `check-open-hand-run` is rewritten to guard the new shape: it fails if `commands/open.md` regains any spawn path and fails if it loses the endpoint call. Six linters that pinned the removed branches (`check-open-manager-resolution`, `check-open-role-defaults`, `check-open-resume-mode`, `check-open-manager-start`, `check-open-owner-live`, `check-resume-flag-clause`) are retired — that behaviour is now the wrapper's, tested there.

## v0.362.11

- docs: **a manager offers `/supervisor:restart <sid>`, not the operator-only `/reload-plugins`, when the target session is addressable.** Four surfaces said the reload was *the* lever and one said *a restart is not the lever* — both true of how a session picks a version up, and both silent on who may pull it, so a manager handed the operator a route it could have taken itself. `docs/fleet-surface.md` now carries the route rule (restart for an addressable session; reload only where `session-liveness.py --list --json` has no live record for the id), and `docs/session-tiers.md`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md` and `commands/steps/readings.md` point at it instead of restating the lever. The composers enforce it: `scripts/attention-ask.py post` refuses a `/reload-plugins` option when the card's `--subject` resolves live (and refuses a reload card that names no subject at all, so the guard cannot be bypassed by omitting the flag), printing `no addressable session` on the one branch it accepts; `scripts/manager-menu-guard.py` denies a manager menu offering the reload with no restart beside it. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/manager-menu-guard.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reload_route.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_menu_guard.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.362.10

- refactor: **the four feed-and-ownership readers move into `session-ownership.py`, so `gate-owner-filter.py` is no longer a library dependency of two live scripts.** `feed_rows`, `headless_key`, `is_headless_key` and `is_human` — plus their two constants, `HEADLESS_PREFIX` and `REPLAY_MARK` — now live beside `load_claims`, `load_ledger_checked` and `is_manager`, and the filter re-exports all six unchanged, so every caller keeps working. `scripts/round-readings.py` and `scripts/gate-answer-check.py` were the reason the filter could not be deleted; they still import it, but nothing they use lives there any more. ⚠️ **The dependency set was six symbols, not the three the change was scoped as, and both extras were found by RUNNING the code rather than reading it:** `REPLAY_MARK` surfaced as a `NameError` on the first real `feed_rows` call, and `headless_key` — which `feed_rows` calls — surfaced as a test ERROR only after the first two moved. A signature read would have shipped two `NameError`s into the two importers this change exists to free. Verified: `python3 -m unittest test_gate_owner_filter test_round_readings` → **305 tests, OK**.
## v0.362.9

- fix: **A crash loop no longer asks the operator to resume a worker a manager owns.** `scripts/worker-keepalive.py` posted a `crash-loop:` attention card on every crash loop, including the case where the task declares a goal — which the fleet's own rules make manager-owned, since the fleet round restarts or resumes that goal's manager. The card therefore put a decision on the operator that the manager tier is already obliged to make, once per crash loop (measured 2026-11-20: 3 of the 6 unactionable cards in a 60-card SC1 sample, all inside 12 seconds). The card is now posted only when **no declared goal resolves** — no `goals:` key, or every declared goal page unopenable — where nothing owns the row; a resolvable goal keeps the existing `crash-loop:` hand-back row as the channel and the fleet round carries the action. `docs/session-tiers.md` § Keep-alive now states the manager action for that row (restart or resume the goal's manager, or reap/stand down the worker) and the operator-card boundary. Change set: `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.362.8

- test: **one fail-closed control per remaining `unverifiable` cause in `scripts/permission-tier.py`.** The brace-expansion cause was loosened narrowly in v0.349.1 and already has fixtures; the other eight share the same branch but not the same reason, so a future "fix" that widens the tier instead of the syntax would satisfy the brace cases while opening a real gate. Pinned: a here-string feeding stdin, an expansion as the command word, a `gh` subcommand decided by an expansion, a payload truncated at the store cap, `curl`'s method/body expansion, `git tag`'s operand expansion, ANSI-C/locale quoting, and an unterminated double quote — each asserted STILL `unverifiable`. Change set: `scripts/tests/test_permission_tier.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.7

- fix: **a closer carrying no operator content no longer reads as an ask, so a worker that ended its turn is not carded as parked.** `is_ask()` tested only the `nothing` prefix and `PARKED_VERBS = ("later (on ",)`, so a bare placeholder — the measured shape is `👤 You:    —` — was non-empty, matched neither, and returned `True`; `is_gated("idle", "—")` then returned `(True, "idle+closer")` and the tracked-set watcher registered a park. Measured 2026-11-20: worker `36283b05` ended on `🟢 ACTIVE · nothing running · next step is mine` / `👤 You:    —`, and the watcher re-registered the park as cards `5bfd9e90…` and `6ce09d28…` while `agent_status` reported `pending_permissions: []`, `pending_question: null`, `current_tool_call: null`. A body that is only placeholder glyphs (`—`, `–`, `-`, `…`, `...`, empty, or any repetition of them) now returns `False` via the new `NO_CONTENT_GLYPHS` set, while every body carrying text still returns `True` — the positive control covers `approve:`, `review:`, `pick — …` and `you run:` at both `is_ask` and `is_gated("idle", …)`, and a mutation test patches `is_ask` to an always-`False` lambda and asserts the positive control fails, so the cheapest wrong fix cannot pass. A `--once` fixture test drives two tracked `idle` workers — one em-dash closer, one `pick —` — and asserts exactly one `GATED … [idle+closer]` line (the `pick`) plus `gated: 1  held: 0`. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.6

- fix: **the relayed-gate closer rule reaches the last three surfaces that still documented `--closer` as the fix for a relay.** v0.247.5 established that a relayed gate is never restated on the manager's own closer — the closer is a verb-free `🟡 WAITING` handover and `--closer` never applies — but only `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` and `scripts/attention-ask.py` carried it. `docs/voice-asks.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md` and `commands/fleet-drive.md` still told a manager that `--closer` is *required whenever the closer repeats the card's ask*, which for a relay is the opposite advice and mints the duplicate the rule removes. All three now name the relay case and point at the rule's one home. Change set: `docs/voice-asks.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.5

- fix: **three changelog bullets are unfolded into the sections their tags actually own — and a fourth was left alone.** The post-merge guard had been red on `master` for several consecutive commits, and its own last line named the damage: *the releaser is stalled at v0.362.0 — unreleased work with no topmost `## Unreleased` section*. Three bullets sat under released headings whose tags do not contain their merges, and each is moved by the TAG, never by where the merge put it — the destinations changed while this repair was in flight, because the releaser cut `v0.362.1` mid-way: the answered-card watcher (`748bc6b1`) moves from `## v0.362.0` to **`## v0.362.1`**, and the two liveness fixes (`a36d751e`, `f508cd2e`) move from `## v0.344.2` to **`## v0.344.3`**. ⚠️ **The `rebase-agent` bullet stays exactly where it is, in `## v0.251.1`** — an earlier cut of this repair moved it to `## Unreleased` on the guard's advice, which would have re-announced shipped work as pending, the error `b7149fe1` already fixed on 2026-11-15. ⚠️ **The guard named the wrong commit for it:** it walks `git log --all`, and a leftover `refs/rebase-agent/pr/671` ref makes an unreachable commit (`bfbf7000`) the last match; walking `origin/master` instead gives `0c4f0591`, which is in `v0.251.1`. The lesson is that the guard's placement advice is a hypothesis and the tag is the authority — here the two disagreed because of a stray ref, not a real fold.

## v0.362.4

- fix: **`check-handback-size.py --transcript` reads the `SubagentHandback` tool call, so a compliant leg is no longer graded absent.** The fleet legs deliver their report through that tool — their own prompt says *"call SubagentHandback({message: <your full report>}) … plain text you write at the end is not delivered"* — but `emitted_reading()` read assistant **text** blocks only. Measured 2026-11-20 on round `agent-ab4dfffd5c9330c85`: a 514 B hand-back came back as *"no `**Hand-back**` fence and no round-id block … an absent hand-back is a failure"* with exit 1, so every compliant leg was a failure — the prose-only defect this mode exists to remove, one layer down. ⚠️ **Keyed on the tool NAME, never on "a `tool_use` carrying a `message`"** — `SendMessage` carries one too (five calls at 295–547 B in the same transcript), so a name-blind read would measure a message sent to a peer as the verdict, and would pass a leg that never handed back; a test pins that shape. The tool message goes through the same `fixed_bytes()`, so the contract's declared variable lines (`crash-loop:` / `cannot-tell:` / `park-nudge:` / `refused:`) stay excluded whichever shape a leg uses, and the existing max-not-last rule now picks the largest candidate across text **and** tool. A committed fixture (`scripts/tests/fixtures/subagent-handback/round-514b.jsonl`, the measured proportions) exits 1 against the pre-fix script and 0 at `514B` after. `--transcript-dir` inherits the fix — it routes through `emitted_reading()` — and the default authored-template mode is untouched. Change set: `scripts/check-handback-size.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_size.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/subagent-handback/round-514b.jsonl`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.3

- docs: **`docs/sweep-output.md` cites the gate that exists, not a `scripts/sweep-gate.py` that never did.** Three citations (lines 53, 91, 101) named a file absent from this repo's history and from every commit in it. They now name the live gate in `bborbe/claude-wrapper` `pkg/sweepgate/` — `WriteTickFile()` (`state.go`, strips the OSC 8 escapes and creates the tick file `0600`), `CheckboxCount()` (`task.go`, returns `—` only for a section with no checkboxes and `n/m` over the boxes it finds, so a mixed gate silently drops the numbered items), and no `orphaned` bucket — each verified against that repo's source rather than assumed. The rules themselves are unchanged; only the owner they point at is. Change set: `docs/sweep-output.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.2

- fix: **the `/who-needs-me` read bound is pinned to a measurement instead of inherited.** It was `10.0 s`, a number derived from nothing, and it sat above the endpoint's own tail — measured 2026-11-20 at the fleet's pinned load (concurrency 4, 20 calls), the board returned **p50 7.26-7.66 s / p95 10.71-11.58 s**, so a fifth of polls crossed the ceiling and refused. The cause was `attention-controller`'s `who-needs-me` globbing every project directory once per registry session (52 sessions × 132 dirs); fixed in `attention-controller` v0.107.3 by a single-pass transcript index. Re-measured on the fixed board at the same load: **p50 0.45-0.48 s / p95 0.63-0.65 s / max 0.73 s**, two-way burst peak 1.17 s. The bound is now `WHO_NEEDS_ME_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0` — deliberately **not** p95 + 20 % (~0.78 s), because the board's slowest internal step (`wezterm cli list`) is bounded at 3 s inside the service, and a client bound under it would turn the server's designed degraded answer (`sources.panes: "failed"`, gates still returned) into a client-side refusal. 5 s = that 3 s internal bound + 2 s of headroom, ~4× the burst peak, and half the old ceiling. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.362.1

- fix: **the feed filter's refusal is now a STDOUT event, so a slow or dead `/who-needs-me` can no longer drop gates silently.** The feed arm is a `Monitor` that fires on **stdout**; stderr carries only the per-poll `LIVENESS` heartbeat and is deliberately not merged (merging it would wake the manager every quiet poll). `refusal_exit` wrote the refusal **only** to stderr, so on a mid-run refusal the `Monitor` died with an empty stdout — **no event**, and the manager could only infer the refusal from a *missing* heartbeat, which is exactly the "gates are lost silently" shape the arm must not have. `refusal_exit` now writes the `WHO-NEEDS-ME-REFUSED` line to stdout as well, so the refusal is an event the manager receives and can render the feed `unread` and surface the refusal. Still **no `gates:` line** on this path — a marker beside the false claim is not a fix. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **no session arms an answered-card watcher any more, and its script is deleted.** `attention-controller`'s delivery arm pushes an operator's answer into the pane on the store's write, so the third `Monitor` was a second reader of the same answer — and the poll it ran was one of the four this port removes. The arm is gone from `commands/fleet-drive.md` row 13, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` and `commands/steps/round-tail.md` (which had it twice, once as a "third arm" and once as a "second, distinct `Monitor`"), and `scripts/answered-watch.py` plus its test are deleted. Reading an answer still has one home, `scripts/answered-attribution.py`, and `attention-ask.py poll <item-id>` remains for quoting one by hand. ⚠️ **`gate-owner-filter.py` is deliberately NOT deleted with it**, although it is the next one due: it is imported as a library by `scripts/round-readings.py` (`feed_rows`, `is_headless_key`) and `scripts/gate-answer-check.py` (`is_human`, `load_claims`), so those four symbols must move into `session-ownership.py` first — the same extraction #1005 did for `adopt-orphans.py`. Deleting it today would break both scripts at import.

## v0.362.0

- feat: **the Python snapshot write is retired; the wrapper is the sole owner.** `bborbe/claude-wrapper`'s sweep tick persists `~/.claude/state/fleet-snapshot.json` over the whole registry roster — verified 2026-11-20 at 2496 ids against 49 live with **0 missing**, a superset of what the round wrote. Two writers to a diff baseline is a race, so `fleet-snapshot.py` now writes only this round's `.digest` marker, which the wrapper does not write and whose absence reads as a lapse to the watcher. ⚠️ **A run with no `--digest-path` now REFUSES**: with the snapshot gone, that call had nothing left to write yet printed `snapshot written:` and exited 0 — a recovery path reporting success while doing nothing. The recovery path in `commands/fleet-drive.md` row 18 and `commands/steps/persist.md` now passes the flag. Change set: `scripts/fleet-snapshot.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_snapshot.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/persist.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.361.1

- docs: **the fleet-drive rule says what `list_agents` actually returns.** Two shipped lines — `commands/fleet-drive.md` (INLINE) and `commands/steps/rules.md` — claimed the roster is scoped to the workers this session spawned, which stopped being true when the rehydrated roster shipped: the roster lists **every** worker, including rows adopted from the ledger regardless of which process spawned them. Only this session's **parks** are this session's to answer. A manager reading the old line could answer another manager's worker, or fail to look for its own rehydrated worker, and neither error surfaces. Both lines now state that split, and `fleet-drive.md` still fits its 10 240-byte tick budget (10 142 bytes, 98 free). Change set: `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.361.0

- feat: **`scripts/mcp-version-gap.py` compares the MCP server's running binary against its repo's latest release, and the round prints the line.** The supervisor MCP server is a compiled binary (`~/.local/bin/claude-wrapper`) whose version nothing checked: measured 2026-11-20 it ran `v0.45.2-0.20261120032107-c84c80e5ee16` — a Go pseudo-version, i.e. built from a commit carrying no release tag — while `bborbe/claude-wrapper` had released `v0.48.0`, three releases behind; a stale binary was indistinguishable from a current one until someone read `go version -m` on the process's own path. ⚠️ **The binary is resolved from the port it listens on** (`lsof` → pid → `ps -o comm=` → `go version -m`), never from a config path: the MCP entry is a URL naming no program, and `git describe` in a checkout answers what the tree says rather than what the process loaded. ⚠️ **Three states, never two** — `agreement` (gap `0`, the tag named) / `divergent` (both versions quoted, the gap counted from the release list) / `unread` (a failed `lsof` / `go` / `gh`, with its reason); a probe that always reports divergence fails exactly as one that always reports agreement, so `--running-version` / `--released-tag` feed either side and the override is recorded with the reading. `round-readings.py` carries it into the readings document as `mcp_server` (so the receipt names it), and `commands/steps/round-readings.md` step 3 prints the `--line` form every tick and posts one `ack` card on a divergence, deduped **fleet-wide** on the version pair (a fixed `--producer-id`, so every manager tier that takes the round resolves to one card). ⚠️ **The latest release is the highest tag by semver, never `gh release list`'s first row** — the list is creation-ordered, so a backport cut after a newer minor would otherwise read as `agreement` while a newer release exists; and the gap is `null` outside the fetched window, with a base newer than every fetched release named `ahead` rather than reported as `0` behind. Change set: `scripts/mcp-version-gap.py`, `scripts/tests/test_mcp_version_gap.py`, `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.360.3

- fix: **`exit-observer-keeper.py` records one ledger line per death, and cards the re-arm that never restored the writer.** `record()` appended a line on every 5 s pass for a pane that stayed stale — it carried no dedup, while the `card()` it replaced deduped by `pane+beat_at`. Measured 2026-11-20: `keeper-rearms.jsonl` held 195 lines for 3 distinct `(pane, beat_at)` deaths and zero `ack` cards. The record now dedups on `(pane, beat_at)` read back from the ledger, so one death is one line and a keeper restart cannot re-record a death already written. The second half is the loss the ledger alone hid: `rearm()` returns rc 0 from `session-exit-record.py observe` when its daemonise fails, having started no observer, and `check()` read that as success and carded nothing. A re-arm now counts as restored only when `beat_at` moves within `VERIFY_SECONDS` (equal to `OBSERVER_STALE`); a pane still stale a full window after the re-arm is carded once, with `--mechanism ack` and the dedup key `exit-observer-silent-<pane>-<beat_at>`. Change set: `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_observer_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.360.2

- docs: **operator work found inside an agent row now has one rule.** New `docs/attention-and-delegation.md` defines attention, human task and agent task, the size test (one answer, ~2 min, no artifact → attention card; else its own `role: human` row), the five channels with use-when / never-use-for, evidence ownership per shape, and what a manager decides alone. `docs/role-mode-matrix.md` gains Rule 5 carrying the test and split bookkeeping (operator ruling 2026-11-20). Rules 1–4 unchanged.

## v0.360.1

- fix: **the round's hand-back gate can now actually fail.** `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md` has told the manager to run `check-handback-size.py --transcript` and branch on its exit code since v0.352.2, and the instruction is prose: measured 2026-11-20 across a full manager session transcript, the gate ran **once in 8 611 lines** and never again, and that one run read `python3 "$P/check-handback-size.py" … 2>&1 | tail -5; echo "size_exit=$?"` — where `$?` is the **pipe's last stage**, so a real `handback-size FAILED` was logged as `size_exit=0` and the manager treated the check as passing. The step now captures `gate_rc=$?` on the line immediately after an unpiped invocation, and `scripts/check-round-gate-exit-code.py` (wired into `precommit`) refuses a piped invocation, one whose exit code is not captured, and one that has been deleted outright — a rule the call site can copy in a shape that cannot carry an exit code is the same "a rule that lives only in prose" defect the size check itself exists to close. Change set: `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `scripts/check-round-gate-exit-code.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_round_gate_exit_code.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.360.0

- fix: **a `role: human` row resolves like a worker on the resume path too.** `scripts/restart-worker.py`'s `_human_launcher` is removed (operator ruling 2026-11-20), so `resolve_launcher` no longer sends a human row to the `-claude` sibling or `manager_launcher` — the worker chain (task `launcher:` → `worker_launcher` → `claude_script`) applies. `server/spawn-cwd.mjs`'s `applyHumanLauncherRule` becomes a pass-through shim because the live spawn path is the Go `claude-wrapper` daemon. Docs: matrix Rule 4, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `server/spawn-cwd.mjs`, `server/spawn-cwd.test.mjs`, `docs/role-mode-matrix.md`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.359.1

- docs: **`llms.txt` names the Go hook instead of the retired Python poller.** The tab-worker paragraph still told a reader that the plugin's `PermissionRequest` hook is `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py`, a file the entry below deletes — so the one doc a fresh agent reads first pointed at a path that no longer exists. It now names `attention-controller-permission-answer-hook` and the `make install` that places it. Found by the PR review of the deletion rather than by the deletion's own grep, because SC5's pattern covers `commands/`, `agents/`, `scripts/` and the hook manifest but not `llms.txt`.

## v0.359.0

- feat: **`scripts/reap-stale-gates.py` closes stale `awaiting-operator` gates whose session is gone.** The attention event log is append-only and nothing ever cleared an item whose session died: measured 2026-11-09, `who-needs-me.py` reported `Board card missing (31)` and 30 of those rows belonged to sessions `session-liveness.py --check` reports ABSENT (`72b2851a` and `d72dda9f` at 260 hours, `19561716` at 221 hours), so the one live uncarded gate sat under 30 dead ones. The cause had recurred 37 days with no owner. The reaper folds `*.events.jsonl` by `item_id`, selects items older than `--max-age-hours` (default 24) whose cause is `awaiting-operator` and whose session is `ABSENT`, and prints one line per item; `--apply` appends one `close` line each and prints `reaped: <id>`. ⚠️ **It never deletes or rewrites an `.events.jsonl`** — that file is the open-state source `who-needs-me.py` folds, so rewriting it would destroy history and race the hook that appends. ⚠️ **`.open.json` is not the open-state source** (measured 2026-11-19: all 984 carry `cleared_by`). ⚠️ **A failed liveness read is never ABSENT** — an unreadable probe keeps the item, so an outage cannot close live gates. The classification is imported from `who-needs-me.py` (`cause_class`, `row`, `wezterm_panes`, `read_registry`) rather than re-implemented, so the tags cannot drift from the report it cleans. ⚠️ **A failed pane read refuses (exit 2), never crashes:** `wezterm_panes()` answers `None` on a mux timeout — measured up to ~88 s under load, which is the loaded machine this targets — and `is_routable` would raise `TypeError` on `str(pane) not in None`; `open_panes` is also derived from every open item, not just the stale candidates, matching the report. Change set: `scripts/reap-stale-gates.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_stale_gates.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.358.2

- fix: **the permission relay runs the Go hook, and the worker tier no longer arms an answered-card watcher.** `hooks/hooks.json`'s `PermissionRequest` command is now `attention-controller-permission-answer-hook` (installed by `bborbe/attention-controller`'s `make install`), which holds ONE stream subscription instead of the 2 s poll the deleted Python hook ran, so an operator's answer reaches the gate on the store's write. A missing binary prints one line to stderr and exits 0, so the prompt reaches the operator rather than the hook blocking it. `commands/worker-drive.md` no longer arms an answered-card `Monitor`: `attention-controller`'s delivery arm pushes the answer into the pane, so a watcher would be a second reader of the same answer — and it was the poll this replaces. ⚠️ **`commands/fleet-drive.md` row 13 still arms it, and that is not an oversight:** `check-step-when` makes the router row normative for `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, which is held on the who-needs-me migration, so the manager tier's half moves with that file rather than here. `scripts/tests/test_hooks_manifest.py` pins the command (it must name the Go hook, must not name `python3` or a `.py`, and must carry the `exit 0` fail-open branch) because every other test in that file reads only the state script, so a revert to the poller would otherwise be silent. Removed with the hook: `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py`, its test, and `scripts/tests/worker_mode_isolation.py`, whose only purpose was pinning `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` for that script's `main()`.

## v0.358.1

- fix: **the closure step runs the `vault-cli` complete verb and branches on its exit code, so the Go verb is the single closure gate.** `commands/steps/goal-closure.md` used to run `scripts/goal-closure-read.py` to count boxes and decide a verdict, then run the verb as a second opinion — two copies of one gate. After vault-cli v0.199.0 `goal complete` and `topic complete` read Success Criteria + DoD / Completion Gate themselves (the same `checkClosureGate` the task verb runs), so the step now runs the tier's verb every round and acts on its exit: exit 0 closes (or is already terminal), non-zero reports the verb's own `cannot complete <tier>: N closure item(s) open: …` line verbatim and leaves the page open. The reader script is gone from `commands/`; the linked-tasks-only detection reads its two named sections directly, Step 0b's rationale is corrected (the verb now refuses on a non-terminal linked task), the step restates the `DOD_REQUIRED_AS_OF` cutoff a CI test pins, and it still honours the page's optional `## Final measurement` block — a recorded `NOT MET`, or a block present but unreadable (empty, unparseable, or missing a criterion), leaves the page open, because the verb reads boxes only; the empty/absent-DoD rule now states the verb refuses on it rather than asking the step to count. Change set: `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, `commands/steps/goal-under-target.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-state.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.358.0

- feat: **`scripts/pane-less-report.py` turns a pane-less manager's self-report into an operator-visible condition.** `docs/role-mode-matrix.md` Rule 2 makes `manager` → `interactive` with no exceptions, yet a manager started headless (or resumed after its tab died) prints `↪️ no branch — no pane · id non-dead` every round indefinitely while staying unreachable by every registry-keyed verb. Called once per round with `--transcript <round.jsonl> --state-dir <dir>`, it resets on a round that carries a `pane` field and otherwise increments a per-session counter, emitting `pane-less manager <sid> — remedy: /supervisor:restart <sid>` at the third consecutive pane-less round. ⚠️ The counter lives on disk (`<state-dir>/<sid>.json`, `{count, last_round_at}`) because each round is a fresh process — an in-memory count could never reach the threshold. Four regression tests. Change set: `scripts/pane-less-report.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_less_report.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`restart-precheck.py` and `close-worker.py` answer for a live session that carries no registry entry instead of refusing `unknown-session-id`.** The reach half of the unaddressable-session defect: `restart-precheck` now emits a classified report (`registry: UNREGISTERED`, liveness from the transcript age, `cwd` from the transcript's first record, `pid` from the `claude` process carrying `--resume <sid>`) and exits 0; `close-worker` names the same state as an `unregistered-live` **refusal** and points at `/supervisor:restart`, because that verb destroys and must never return a close verdict for a session it cannot prove it owns. ⚠️ Both fall back to today's `unknown-session-id` when the transcript is absent or older than `LIVE_WINDOW`, so a dead session is never reported live; an unresolvable pid prints `unknown`, never invented. Measured 2026-11-19 (session `616a52dd`): the heartbeat read LIVE while both verbs refused. Three regression tests. Change set: `scripts/restart-precheck.py`, `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_precheck.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`flagged-rows.py` no longer reads a row ABSENT when its declared session id is stale but a live session carries the same name.** Measured 2026-11-19 on `Start Day`: the row declared `a88b5438` (no live process) while four live tabs held its panes, so every open path re-opened a row already running. `ctx["managers"]` is `live_session_names(sessions)` — every live session's name, raw and glyph-stripped — so a name hit with an `ABSENT` verdict is the same subject under a stale id, and the row now reads `LIVE`. ⚠️ `None` is not `False`: an unreadable registry still leaves the row `ABSENT`, because reading a failed probe as "not live" would authorise a duplicate spawn. ⚠️ Names are not ids: a genuinely dead row with no live namesake still resumes. Three regression tests. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
