- fix: **`session-liveness --check` marks a live session that carries no identity-registry entry as `UNREGISTERED`.** The heartbeat reader answered `LIVE` while every registry-keyed verb (`restart-precheck.py`, `close-worker.py`, `send_agent_message`) refused the same session with `unknown-session-id` — measured 2026-11-19 on session `616a52dd`, alive, `busy`, pane-less, holding no `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` entry, `--check` = LIVE, both verbs refusing. The split is now visible in the verdict instead of having to be re-derived by joining the two sources. Two regression tests: the marker fires when the entry is absent, and stays silent for a registered live control.

## v0.357.0

- feat: **the fleet round's open leg is gone — the wrapper's flagged tick now opens goal and topic rows.** Row 16 and `commands/steps/open.md` are removed, `agents/fleet-open-actor.md` is retired, and its `check-open-leg-raw-name` guard is deleted; the acting-legs carve-out and `manager-reap.md`'s `When:` now read `every tick`. The wrapper (`bborbe/claude-wrapper#126`, deployed) opens a never-opened goal/topic whose manager-chain probe reads no live manager, so the model-dispatched leg this replaces is no longer the only opener. Adopt orphans (row 4) stays until the wrapper's adopt job is wired. ⚠️ **The removal reaches the hand-back, not only the router.** `agents/fleet-round.md`'s fence no longer carries the six counts the open leg produced (`open-status`, `selected`, `opened`, `open-resumed`, `jumped`, `refused`) and `check-handback-keys.py` no longer requires them — the delegate was otherwise instructed to emit six counts it could not measure. `docs/handback-contract.md`'s agent enumeration and its open-leg truncation example, `commands/steps/readings.md`'s open-leg paragraph, and the `commands/steps/open.md` → `agents/fleet-open-actor.md` mappings in `check-handback-keys.py` and its test are gone. `commands/steps/output-shape.md` loses item 3 and renumbers, with every cross-reference in the live docs and scripts re-pointed — the `item 14` → `13` and `item 13` → `12` references were left dangling by the first cut. `scripts/check-open-leg-opened.py` and its test are deleted with the leg they guarded, and the opener named in `commands/stop.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `topic-spawn.py`, `topic-keepalive.py`, `approved-not-started.py`, `open-queue.py` and `check-handback-size.py` is now the wrapper's flagged tick. ⚠️ **The every-tick step files were the last holdouts** — `commands/steps/rules.md` (row 0), `commands/steps/readings.md` (row 15) and `commands/steps/service-guard.md` each still told the reader that 'the round's open leg (row 16)' opens the ranked flagged queue, pointing at a deleted row and a deleted step; `commands/topic-spawn.md` and `commands/topic-drive.md` said the same of workers. All five now name the wrapper's flagged tick, and `open-queue.py`'s own `open leg:` print prefix is `open-queue:`.

## v0.356.4

- fix: **A card wait whose `⏰ Ends:` names a past check time reads `declared`, not `expired`.** `declared-wait.py` took the latest ISO date in the slot as a deadline, so a session parked on an unanswered attention card — whose clock is only a re-check time — read `expired` once its `Stop` record aged past `MAX_AGE_S`, dropping a live park to `resume`. A card wait is now detected by the word "card" plus a backtick-quoted hex id and exempted from the clock-expiry rule; `MAX_AGE_S` still expires every non-card wait exactly as before. The exemption is bounded structurally, not by a card-store poll: an answered card resumes its session and writes a new `Stop` (newest-wins), and `flagged-rows.py`'s `parked` branch sits after every liveness branch, so a dead session is never parked on this reader. Change set: `scripts/declared-wait.py`, `scripts/tests/test_declared_wait.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.356.3

- fix: **the exit-observer keeper records a routine re-arm on disk and cards only on failure, so a clean death no longer reaches the attention board.** `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py` posted an `ack` card for every manager-pane exit-observer death before re-arming; a death that re-arms cleanly needs no operator action, so it now appends a line to `keeper-rearms.jsonl` before the re-arm and files a card only when the record cannot be written or the re-arm fails — the card is the fallback trace, never the routine one. ⚠️ **The ordering is the point:** the record is written BEFORE the re-arm, because a re-armed pane reads `covered` again and a record written after it would be the only sign the writer ever died. Supersedes the "card is filed before the re-arm" rule (operator decision 2026-11-19: *"is useless … dont put this on the attention board"*); the 2026-11-06 `fleet-keeper` no-kill pin is unrelated and stays. Change set: `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_observer_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.356.2

- fix: **a live manager auto-adopts a dead-spawner gate, so a non-routine permission is answered at the fast path instead of the manager's next turn.** `gate-answer-check.py` escalated every gate whose spawner was not the answering session, so a gate routed away from a dead manager waited for a turn — or timed out at 15 min — which is the p90 tail this closes. Adoption is bounded to a dead spawner (a registry `live_ids()` read; an unreadable registry escalates, never adopts) and runs the full rubric, so a production-touching or `operator`-tier prompt is still refused. Change set: `scripts/gate-answer-check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py`.

## v0.356.1

- fix: **the round delegate now runs rows 7, 8, 9, 10 and 20 — the move that makes the seven step-file rewrites live.** Those seven PRs rewrote each step file for a headless delegate, but **none of them moved a row**: the router, `round-dispatch.md` and the delegate's own run table still assigned all five to the manager, so the delegate executed none of it. All four places now agree. Row 7 routes a parked worker instead of answering in the operator's voice; row 8 files the operator's asks with the `Skill` it was granted and **routes** a spawn, since `/supervisor:open` is manager-only; row 9's cause check is script-only (`cause-resolve.py`) and no longer waits on a sentence only the manager writes; row 10 renders the escalation material for row 11 to ask; row 20 writes `<round>.render` and the manager prints it **after** its row 19 tail. ⚠️ **The manager keeps 1, 11, 13, 14, 17 and 19** — the re-arm, the operator questions, the watchers, the voice and the tail: everything an `AskUserQuestion`, a `Monitor` or the session's own compaction needs stays in the session that owns the loop. ⚠️ **The two withdrawn attempts moved rows without the capability; PRs 1–7 built the capability without the move. This is both halves.** Row 8's two step files also lose their `has NOT moved yet` caveats, so a delegate reading them is told what is true; row 11 now **consumes** what rows 7 and 8 route — its trigger is `blocked` > 0, `parks-routed` > 0 **or a routed spawn** (three different populations, so none implies another — a round that only routed a spawn would otherwise drop it silently), and a routed spawn is **recorded in the round's payload** for row 20 to compose into the render — ⚠️ **row 8 must never write `<round>.render` itself**, because that file's *absence* is what makes a `--budget`-aborted round render `not-run` instead of printing a partial block as the whole output shape. Neither route ends at a row that never reads it; the tail's ordering line says plainly that the render file is already written when it runs, so a skip it finds must be printed after the file rather than assumed inside it. ⚠️ **And the split is now checked, not remembered:** `scripts/check-round-split.py` (wired into `precommit`, with `scripts/tests/test_check_round_split.py`) asserts all four statements name the same two sets **and that their union covers every row in the router's own table**, so a row dropped from all four places cannot pass either — the first cut of this very change edited three of the four and left the fourth re-owning a moved row. Change set: `agents/fleet-round.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `commands/steps/act.md`, `commands/steps/open-items.md`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `Makefile`, `scripts/check-round-split.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_round_split.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.356.0

- fix: **a manager resume arms its own loop instead of relying on a pane keystroke.** A manager's loop is one-shot per re-arm, so a resume that left `/supervisor:<branch>-loop` to the pane wake did nothing when the guard refused or the pane was slow — measured 2026-11-19: 2 of 5 resumed manager panes never passed `pane-wake-guard.py` within 90 s and their loop was never armed. `scripts/restart-worker.py` gains `manager_loop_prompt()`, `resume_command(resume_prompt=…)` and a read-only `--resume-prompt` verb; the normal resume (manager role, including the subject-less root Fleet Manager → `/supervisor:fleet-loop`) and `--absent` carry the loop command as the resume prompt, and `commands/open.md` § 3.1 calls `--resume-prompt` so it never hand-builds or hand-quotes it. `commands/restart.md` no longer types a second re-arm. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `commands/open.md`, `commands/restart.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.355.0

- feat: **the gate answerer answers a worker's closer pick in the manager's voice.** `PICK_ANSWERING_ENABLED` flips to `True` (kept as the kill switch). `attention-answer.py --answer-text` now posts `answer.answerer` (`operator` | `manager`), which attention-controller v0.104+ and claude-wrapper v0.45+ read to deliver a manager answer as `Manager answer, via supervisor: …` instead of in the operator's voice. `answer_text_refusal` stops refusing `--answerer manager`, refuses any answerer other than `operator` / `manager`, and refuses the `supervisor:gate-answerer` arm unless it answers as `manager`. `docs/permission-allowlist.md` and `agents/gate-answerer.md` no longer describe picks as disabled. Requires both carrier releases to be installed first.

## v0.354.0

- feat: **`attention-answer.py dismiss ITEM_ID` retires a card without recording an answer.** `skip` was the only way to clear a card that asks nothing, and the store records it as a DECLINE the operator never gave; `dismiss` closes the item, stores no answer, and names the arm that closed it. `closed` is terminal, so a second dismiss exits non-zero rather than reading as a silent no-op. Change set: `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`.
- docs: **`docs/closer-classes.md` gains the hand-over split.** A `you run:` closer is a hand-over, but only some hand-overs are the operator's: an agent-doable one (a command, a session action, answering a card) mints no card, an operator-only one (chore, people-communication, no-command-form action, live-pane typing) still mints `done` / `not now`. Default is mint — a stranded gate is the cost the split must never buy. Operator-specific rows stay out of this repo and are read from `CLOSER_HANDOVER_POLICY`. Change set: `docs/closer-classes.md`.

## v0.353.1

- fix: **`attention-ask.py` refuses a card that asks the operator to type into a pane.** A card carrying "you run: type anything in pane 229 and press Enter" asked for a keystroke the asking session owns — it has `wezterm cli send-text` — and the pane in the reported case had not existed for hours; the card reopened seven times. `pane_ask_refusal` now matches the instruction in the payload or the context and refuses in **both** pane states, naming `wezterm cli send-text --pane-id N` as the action the posting session must take itself: a live pane is still not the operator's to poke, and a dead one has nothing to poke at. The rule is stated once in `commands/steps/needs-input.md`. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`.

## v0.353.0

- feat: **`flagged-rows.py` reads liveness from the wrapper, not from a subprocess.** Both `session-liveness.py` call sites are gone: `live_sessions()` builds its `{sid: name}` identity map from `GET /api/1.0/liveness` (the verdict now carries `name`, from `bborbe/claude-wrapper#131`), and `liveness_of()` maps `live` → `LIVE`, `not-live` → `ABSENT`. A session the endpoint does not list, or a failed read, is `UNKNOWN` — never `ABSENT`, because `ABSENT` licenses spawning a second session and the wrapper cannot prove dead a session it never spawned. `LIVENESS_EXIT` and `PROBE_TIMEOUT` retired; the schema's `liveness` enum shrinks to `LIVE | ABSENT | UNKNOWN | none`. Measured before switching: the script's live set (68) is a strict subset of the endpoint's (69), so no live session is lost. New `FollowTheEndpoint` spec stubs the route with two sessions and flips the second's verdict, so a consumer that self-derives cannot pass. ⚠️ **Two review findings folded in:** a `liveness` value that is neither `live` nor `not-live` reads `UNKNOWN`, never `ABSENT` (malformed must not license a second spawn), and an endpoint whose verdicts carry no `name` field at all — a daemon predating `claude-wrapper#131` — returns `None` rather than an empty identity map, because an empty map empties `managers` and fires `open-manager` against live managers. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows_schema.py`, `scripts/tests/schema/flagged-rows.schema.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.14

- fix: **`commands/steps/act.md` separates the two slash commands it names — one the delegate may run, one it may not.** PR 7 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order. Row 8's act step files a task with `/vault-cli:create-task` and spawns with `/supervisor:open`; **the first is legal for the delegate now that it holds `Skill`, the second never is** — `/supervisor:open` is a manager-only verb (`docs/session-tiers.md`) and a worker must not run it. The step now says so, **conditionally on the reader**: create-task runs here either way; a spawn stays a spawn **while the manager still owns row 8** (today), and becomes a **route** — task and readiness rendered, the manager opens it — the moment the row moves to the delegate. ⚠️ **Row 8 has not moved yet** — the four-place move is its own change, landing last. ⚠️ **This is the last of the six step files (the seventh PR is the contract row); the move is what makes any of them live.** Change set: `commands/steps/act.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.13

- fix: **the closer gate counts a local liveness probe and a task-field read as durable observables.** `session_bound_class()` checks `DURABLE` first, so a session-bound word in the `(mechanism, id)` slot — which CLAUDE.md § Async State Closer *requires* — only decides the outcome when no durable arm matched; neither probe had one, so both compliant closers were blocked. Measured 2026-11-19 in the Fleet Manager session: a `session-liveness.py --check <uuid>` observable blocked as `run-in-background-shell`, and a `vault-cli task get "<task>" claude_session_id` observable blocked as `background-sub-agent`. Two arms added — the liveness one needs the probe name AND a full 36-char uuid (an 8-char prefix is not re-probeable), the task one is keyed to a known field name so `task get "<title>" prints execution` still blocks. The `(mechanism, id)` slot is **not** stripped: that was the rejected fix, and it would newly allow a closer whose only observable is a background shell. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the closer gate's retry path compares the `⏰ Ends:` line, not just `👤 You:`.** `verdict()`'s record carried `closer` and no `ends`, and `_retry_verdict()` released only on `previous.get("closer") != closer` — so a continued turn that kept `👤 You:` byte-identical and fixed ONLY its observable was re-blocked as `blocked-retry`, and the only escape left was rewording a line that was already correct. Measured 2026-11-19: with the observable corrected, the first stop blocked as `session-bound-ends`, the retry cleared the ends check and blocked on the closer's own reason, and the pair compared equal — a `2` where `0` was right. The record now carries `ends` from the same `last_closer_scan()` call `evaluate()` already makes (one reader, no second transcript read), and the retry compares the pair. A record written before this change has no `ends` key and is read as "cannot tell", never as "changed", so its behaviour is unchanged. The loop bound is untouched: three identical stops still release. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.12

- fix: **the round delegate is granted `Skill`, so row 8's step file is runnable once the row moves.** PR 6 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order. `commands/steps/open-items.md` says the open-items ledger is read and written **only** through the `supervisor:open-items` skill, and the delegate's `tools:` omitted `Skill` — so row 8 would have been **inert by construction** the moment it moved: the delegate's own `<error_handling>` says a row needing a tool outside `allowed-tools` must be reported and stopped. `Skill` is now granted and the step file records why. ⚠️ **Row 8 has NOT moved yet** — the router, `round-dispatch.md` and the agent's own run table still assign it to the manager; the move is its own PR, after all seven step files are ready. This one makes the step runnable, not live. ⚠️ **Same class as the two withdrawn attempts:** a row cannot move until its step file stops assuming the manager's capabilities — and conversely, rewriting the step file alone moves nothing. Change set: `agents/fleet-round.md`, `commands/steps/open-items.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.11

- fix: **a manager answers the gate cards of its own subject, not only those of workers it spawned.** `own-park-check.verdict()` admitted a worker only when THIS session had spawned it, so every card raised by a peer's worker escalated — measured 2026-11-19, 0 answered in 24 dispatches while the operator answered 14. The predicate now takes a second, durable input: `session-scope.Scope.admission()` resolves the producing session's vault page to a goal and admits it when that goal is the asking manager's own subject. The spawn edge still admits on its own, and `scope` is optional, so a caller with no vault walk keeps the old behaviour. ⚠️ The new method is the fail-CLOSED twin of `exclusion()`: the feed filter fails open because hiding a row on a failed read silences the fleet, while the answerer fails closed because admitting on a failed read is the blanket widening the manager contract forbids. Every `operator`-tier, `role: human`, held and unresolvable-owner card still escalates, and a manager with no page of its own — the Fleet Manager — still answers nothing.
## v0.352.10

- fix: **`commands/steps/worker-channels.md` no longer has the delegate answering in the operator's voice.** PR 5 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order. The step runs in `supervisor:fleet-round` (router row 7); its first table row told the caller to answer a headless park with `message="Operator answer, via supervisor: <option>"` — **a provenance act `agents/fleet-round.md`'s `<role>` explicitly forbids a delegate to take.** That row is now a **route**: the delegate renders the question and its options, and the manager's row 11 answers. ⚠️ **A park belonging to another manager routes to that manager** (`commands/steps/rules.md`), which is a different destination from the operator's question — the earlier lever-(b) attempt conflated the two. The delegate still answers its **own** workers (row 5d) and still holds `answer_permission` for that. Change set: `commands/steps/worker-channels.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.9

- fix: **`commands/steps/escalate.md` routes its blocked set to the manager's row 11 instead of putting its own `AskUserQuestion`.** PR 4 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order. The step runs in `supervisor:fleet-round` (router row 10), and **a headless delegate cannot put an `AskUserQuestion`** — it would park with nobody to answer, the same reason row 11 stayed with the manager. The blocked-set bullet now says so: the delegate renders the material and routes it, and row 11 owns the ONE ask (up to 4) with its `asked-ledger` claim. ⚠️ **The old wording was satisfiable only while the manager ran this row**, which is exactly the kind of seam that made the two earlier lever-(b) attempts fail after the row moved. Change set: `commands/steps/escalate.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.8

- fix: **`commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md` now runs BEFORE the manager prints the render, which is the ordering its own text used to make impossible.** PR 3 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order. Row 20 is the delegate's — it writes `<round>.render` and returns — so the tail (row 19) runs afterwards, in the manager, judging its own `re-arm` epoch and the receipt it wrote; **only then** does the manager print the render. ⚠️ **Three passages said the opposite** ("carry both to the `Legs` render at row 20", "still render row 20, then end the turn"); under the old split they were satisfiable, and after PRs 1–2 they were not — the tail's receipt check and `fleet-legs --status` verdict could never reach the operator's block, which is the exact failure this tail step exists to prevent. The file now states the order at its head and points its two carry-forward lines at the print that follows it. Change set: `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.7

- fix: **`commands/steps/output-shape.md` renders to `<round>.render` and no longer asks the operator anything.** PR 2 of the seven-PR lever-(b) order; PR 1 (`#1014`) put the contract row in place first. The step now states that `supervisor:fleet-round` executes it (router row 20), that the render is **written to a file** and printed by the manager **after** its row 19 tail, and why both alternatives fail — printed here it lands in a headless transcript nobody reads, and returned in the hand-back it breaks the ≤ 1 KB cap. ⚠️ **Two items lost their `AskUserQuestion`:** item 6 (needs-input) and item 11 (course-correction drafts) now **render the material** for a question and leave the asking to the manager's **row 11**, the only row that may put one — a headless delegate that asked would park with nobody to answer. ⚠️ **Absence is `not-run`, not an empty file:** a delegate that exits 14 on `--budget` before row 20 runs writes nothing, so a missing render and an empty one stay distinguishable. Change set: `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.6

- docs: **`<round>.render` joins the payload-path table, with why it is a file and what its absence means.** The render cannot travel in either existing channel — as hand-back text it breaks the ≤ 1 KB cap this contract enforces, and printed by the delegate it lands in a headless transcript the operator never sees — so the delegate computes it, writes it here, and the manager prints it **after** its row 19 tail. That ordering is the reason it is a file: the tail judges the manager's own `re-arm` epoch and receipt and must reach the operator's block, so it cannot run after the render. ⚠️ **Absence is `not-run`, not an empty render:** a delegate that hands back early on `--budget` exit 14 skips row 20, and an empty block would read like a quiet fleet. Change set: `docs/handback-contract.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.5

- fix: **the goal closure reader names its basis and reads the goal's own recorded measurement, so a goal whose boxes are ticked but whose measurement misses a bar no longer reads a bare `CLOSE`.** `goal-closure-read.py` decided from checkbox counts alone — measured 2026-11-19: `24 Goals/PR Reviews Return a Verdict Within Minutes, Not Hours.md` read `VERDICT: CLOSE` with seven ticked SCs while its first single-window read missed three of them, and it flipped to `OPEN` the moment a box was un-ticked, with no measurement read at all. The reader now parses an optional `## Final measurement` block (`- SC1 bar = … measured = … verdict = MET|NOT MET`) and prints `VERDICT: CLOSE|OPEN (basis: boxes|measurement)`; a recorded `NOT MET` reads `OPEN (basis: measurement)` whatever the boxes say, and a block present but unparseable, empty, or missing a criterion id the page's SCs carry fails closed to `OPEN (basis: measurement)`. ⚠️ **The reader still never measures** — it parses what the page records, and a page with no block keeps `CLOSE (basis: boxes)`. Change set: `scripts/goal-closure-read.py`, `scripts/tests/test_goal_closure_read.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.4

- fix: **a round's end is now persisted in its own hand-back, because the receipt could not carry it.** `--handback` writes `ended_at=<epoch>` as the file's first line, and a later `--handback` for the same round **keeps the first stamp** — a late delegate's rewrite must not move the end. ⚠️ **The measurement this unblocks had no data:** a 24 h overlap sample (successor-start minus predecessor-end) found **0 measurable pairs over 19 rounds**, because `manager-receipt.py` keeps only the current and previous round, so 18 of 19 had no end at all; the max-mtime fallback used instead was gameable in both directions — a rewrite landing after the successor started read as an overlap, and excluding those pairs let a genuinely overlapping gate pass. Measured 2026-11-19. The hand-back is the one artifact written once per round, so it is where the end belongs. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.3

- fix: **the fleet keeper can no longer report a lapse whose card it never posted.** `notify()` returned `None` on a failed `attention-ask.py post` without inspecting `out.returncode` and without logging anything, and the lapse branch printed `reported — … lapse reported for <who>` regardless — so a store 503 read as a successful report with no card. Measured 2026-11-19: **73 `lapse reported for` lines carrying no card** across a six-hour lapse of session `26480062`, while `~/Library/Logs/claude-wrapper/stderr.log` carried a sustained 503 storm (`01:20:45–07:20:46`, ~1 820 lines/hour). `notify()` now logs `notify failed: exit <rc>; <reason>` (the reason being the last line `attention-ask.py` printed), and the lapse branch reports `cannot-tell` — with the lapse left unmarked so the next tick retries — instead of a cardless `reported`. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.2

- fix: **the manager CHECKS the round delegate's verdict instead of trusting it.** `agents/fleet-round.md` already said *emit the fence and NOTHING else*, and the delegates ignore it: measured 2026-11-18 across eight live `supervisor:fleet-round` runs, **seven emitted no fence at all** — a 54–111 B closing remark — and the eighth emitted prose at **1 510 B** against the 1 024 B cap. A rule the delegate can re-read and override is not a bound, so `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md` now runs `check-handback-size.py --transcript <the delegate's transcript>` before reading a single count, and on exit 1 **re-asks once for the counts only**. ⚠️ **The re-ask is the mechanism**: an overrun costs the delegate a turn instead of loading the manager's context with a narrative that restates what the payload already holds. Change set: `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.352.1

- refactor: **the session-ownership predicates live in their own module, so `gate-owner-filter.py` can be deleted.** `load_ledger_checked`, `load_claims` and `is_manager` moved out of the script into `scripts/session-ownership.py`, with their docstrings verbatim, and both callers now import it — `adopt-orphans.py` previously imported the script itself, which was the only thing preventing its deletion. ⚠️ The distinction the move exists to protect is preserved: `load_ledger_checked` returns `None` for "could not read" and `{}` for "read, nobody recorded", and the two callers need opposite answers — the filter fails OPEN on `None` (keeps every gate), `adopt-orphans.py` must refuse. Collapsing them would turn a dead controller into "no spawns ever", silently. `_Unreadable` was deliberately left in the filter: it belongs to `route_to_fleet`, not to the three functions, so moving it would have broken that caller.

## v0.352.0

- feat: **`check-handback-size.py` can now measure a LIVE round's emitted hand-back, and an absent one is a failure rather than 0 B.** The gate asserted only the authored template, so every live delegate could overrun the cap on every round and nothing failed — measured 2026-11-18 on the eight `supervisor:fleet-round` delegates of one manager session: **seven emitted a 54–111 B closing remark and no verdict at all**, and the eighth emitted prose at **1 510 B**, 486 B over the 1 024 B cap. A literal "≤ 1 KB" test passes all seven. `--transcript <path>` measures one delegate and `--transcript-dir <dir>` measures a subagents directory, filtering on `.meta.json` `agentType` (default `supervisor:fleet-round`). ⚠️ **Two block shapes count, because the live delegates emit the second:** the contract's `**Hand-back**` fence, or a block naming the round id (`**Round <id> — final verdict**`) — the discriminator SC3 names. ⚠️ **A transcript with neither exits 1**, naming how many assistant text blocks it read, so the check can fail on a live round instead of only on the authored template. Change set: `scripts/check-handback-size.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_size.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.351.1

- fix: **the fleet subject's `STALE` limit covers a healthy long round.** `observed_period()` took the p90 of start-to-start arm gaps for every branch, but an arm gap is start-to-start, so a round's own duration sets a floor on it — a long healthy round therefore sat in the tail the p90 discards by construction and read `STALE`. The `fleet--` branch now takes the **maximum of its 12-arm window and a persisted high-water mark** (`.hwm` beside the `.arms`, written on every read), bounded at **2 × median** rather than `PERIOD_CAP` — a ring-recomputed maximum forgets a long gap within ~10 arms and the false `STALE` returns, which the mark prevents; every other branch keeps the p90, and a non-fleet subject's limit is byte-identical before and after. Measured 2026-11-18: one round ran **1355 s** start-to-start against a then-live **1157 s** limit (p90 977 + SLACK 180), so the false `STALE` reproduces. The fleet limit moved 1157 s → 1535 s on the ring that contained it, and the mark keeps it there once that gap ages out — synthetic proof: **1980 s with the mark, 1080 s without**, after a 3000 s gap is removed from the ring. `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Interval now states round duration as the binding constraint, not the declared interval, and the declared 900 s stays unraised. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.351.0

- fix: **a goal manager whose declaring topic manager is dead or `cannot-tell` now has a keeper.** It was nobody's population: `topicless-goal-keepalive.py`'s guard skipped any goal a topic declared, and `topic-keepalive.py` returned `cannot-tell` for the topic manager and acted on nothing, so the whole subtree was unkept indefinitely (measured 2026-11-18 on private-personal: `selected=22 actionable=0`). The fleet tier now takes it (operator ruling, Option A) — `fleet_keeps` gains `unkept_topics`, the leg probes each declaring topic and prefixes the row's reason with `declaring topic <name> reads <verdict>`. ⚠️ **The race that exception opens is the reason this ships with a guard, not after one:** the existing checks refuse a LIVE session and a spent cap, and neither refuses two writers that both read `ABSENT` for one `claude_session_id` before the first resume registers. `topic-keepalive.py` therefore gains an `O_EXCL` claim — `--claim-resume <id>` for the actor, `claim_state` (read-only) for the deciding probe, released by `--record-resume` and expiring after `CLAIM_TTL` — so exactly one writer resumes. Change set: `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/topicless-goal-keepalive.py`, `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topicless_goal_keepalive.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive-topicless-goals.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.350.3

- fix: **the decision-park leg fires for a re-approved row.** `vault-cli task approve` preserves `approved_at` on a re-approval, so a row the operator re-approved to clear a wait carried a stamp older than the worker's last turn and kind (a) could never fire — the wait was real, recent, and invisible. The leg now compares `reapproved_at` when present and keeps the preserved `approved_at` as the fallback, so a row that was never re-approved still emits nothing.

## v0.350.2

- docs: file the goal-tier feed bullet under the release that carried it — it sat under `## v0.349.3`, which `git tag --contains 94faed1c` does not list.

## v0.350.1

- fix: **the permission classifier calls every credential path `secret`, so no allowlist entry can auto-allow a read of one.** Measured 2026-11-18 on the installed classifier: `cat ~/.claude/.credentials.json`, `head ~/.teamvault.json` and `ls ~/Library/Keychains` came back `allowlist` (the `cat`/`head`/`ls` entries), and the same commands inside a compound came back `manager` — and `server/permission-tier.mjs` asks this same script, so the goal-wrangler auto-allowed them as class B with no human involved. `scripts/permission-tier.py`'s `_SECRET_PATH` now matches, in any Bash token and in a Read/Write/Edit `file_path`: `.credentials.json`, `.teamvault.json`, `.netrc`, `.git-credentials`, `.npmrc`, `.pypirc`, `id_*` keys (and `.pub`), `*.pem`, `*.key`, any `.env*` file, and the directories `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.kube`, `~/.config/gh`, `~/Library/Keychains` themselves as well as anything under them; the glob list and the heredoc text scan gain the new names too. Also `secret`: any `.claude.json` (`jq . ~/.claude.json`), `~/.claude` named as a directory argument (`ls ~/.claude` — `cd ~/.claude` stays a move, not a read), and a whole-tree read whose path is `/`, `/Users`, home, or a directory holding a credential path (`~/.claude`, `~/.config`, `~/Library`) — `grep -r`/`-R`/`-rh`, `ls -R`, `find`, `du`, `tree`, `rg`/`ag`/`fd`, and a glob read such as `cat ~/.*` — because `grep -rh token ~` and `grep -r accessToken ~/.claude` came back `allowlist` though no single path in them names a secret. Every `~`-word counts as home — `~bborbe`, `~root/…`, `~+`, `~-` — since `grep -rh token ~bborbe` was still `allowlist` once `~` alone was caught. The server wrapper needs no change of its own — it spawns this script — and the two stay in lockstep through the shared fixture: `server/permission-tier.fixtures.json` gains 59 `cred-*` cases (plain, compound and `$(…)` forms, Bash and Read/Write/Edit), run by both `scripts/tests/test_permission_tier.py` and `server/permission-tier.test.mjs`; 37 of them fail against v0.349.2, the rest pin already-caught paths, and seven negatives (`cat /work/repo/id_mapping.go`, `cat docs/README.md`, `grep -r foo /work/repo`, `ls ~`, `rg token src`, `cat ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `find . -name '*.md'`), plus the existing `cd ~/.claude && make update` case, keep ordinary reads where they were. `server/permission-tier.test.mjs` also names the exact auto-allowed commands in its own test, through the server's subprocess path; `server/permission-tier.mjs` needs no change because it is a wrapper over this script by design, not a port, which is what keeps the two in lockstep. Change set: `scripts/permission-tier.py`, `server/permission-tier.fixtures.json`, `server/permission-tier.test.mjs`, `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.350.0

- feat: **a manager answers its headless workers' permission cards within seconds, from a background sub-agent, instead of leaving them to the board.** Measured over 7 days: the operator answered 2459 permission cards, the installed classifier rates 78% of them `manager` tier, and managers answered 13 — 3005 of 3256 cards came from wrapper-backed (`sdk-cli`) workers, whose only manager answer path was the fleet round's `own-parks` leg (every 15 min, fleet tier only, and the wrangler there holds no Bash). Three changes: (1) **the gate feed `Monitor` runs continuously in every manager tier** — `timeout_ms: 1800000`, re-armed in the turn its expiry notice arrives, never only for the ≤30 min after a round (`commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`; the tail's arm gains the `--digest` the fleet arm already carried); (2) **every `hst:<item-id>` feed line dispatches the new `agents/gate-answerer.md` (haiku; `Bash`, `Read`) with `run_in_background: true` in that turn** — it runs the new `scripts/gate-answer-check.py` (open permission card, no operator hold, a worker this manager spawned via `own-park-check.py` or adopted via an ownership claim, a parked wrapper question, not `operator` tier on BOTH the wrapper's full input and the card payload, exactly one vault page stamping the session as its brief), applies the `docs/permission-allowlist.md` rubric, and answers with `attention-answer.py answer <id> --decision allow` or leaves the card; it never answers an `operator`-tier gate and never denies; (3) **docs corrected to the running system** — the store-card answer IS delivered to a wrapper child by the claude-wrapper daemon's board delivery (`pkg/board.go`, carrier `claude-wrapper:board`; 8 `delivered` reports among the last 60 answered permission cards), so the page's "a store answer releases nothing" warning is withdrawn, and the "held off the board for 10 minutes" claim is replaced: the controller registers no manager-hold producer, so gates post immediately. `own-parks.md`'s latency note now names the instant path and keeps the step as the backstop. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py`, 26 cases — every refusal branch, both answerable tiers, the stricter-reading rule and the command cap. Change set: `agents/gate-answerer.md` (new), `scripts/gate-answer-check.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py` (new), `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/own-parks.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`, `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `README.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the same background answerer can take a worker's closer `pick —` card, answering its one recommended option in the manager's own voice — shipped DISABLED (`PICK_ANSWERING_ENABLED = False`, every pick is `skip`) because every carrier renders a card answer as the operator's; measured as its own row once enabled.** A store card with `answer_mechanism: message`, `interrupt_class: pick`, `producer_kind: session` (not a hook menu) reaches the gate feed as an `hst:` line like a permission card; `scripts/gate-answer-check.py` now classifies it `kind: pick` and requires exactly one recommended option (`recommended: true`, or a `(recommended` label), the same ownership / hold / single-brief checks, and no `PICK_DENY` class anywhere on the card — prod or deploy, merge or release, delete, force push, secrets, live trade, TDR, `approve:`; `agents/gate-answerer.md` then judges the option against the task file (in scope, not scope-changing — never an abort, complete, hold or defer). ⚠️ **The answer is never hand-built:** `gate-answer-check.py --answer` re-runs every check and only on `answerable` calls `attention-answer.py`, so no label passes through a shell the model composed. `scripts/attention-answer.py answer` gains `--answer-text TEXT` (the store's `answer: {kind: text, value}`; no `TARGET`, the same bounded delivery wait as a permission answer — the attention-controller carries it, carrier `watcher:attention-watcher`, 17 `delivered` reports among the store history's 21 answered pick cards) and `--answerer manager` (the answer marker names a manager, so the operator count is not inflated). The answer text is `Manager answer, via supervisor: <label>` — the operator prefix would be a forged provenance claim. ⚠️ **Known gap, outside this repo:** the controller's headless turn still opens *"The operator answered on the attention board."* whoever answered. `docs/permission-allowlist.md` § *A worker's closer pick* is the separate row. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py` (+17: the pick branch, every deny class, label-marked recommendation, the answer argv) and `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py` (+6: content body, no TARGET, exit 3 on undelivered, manager marker, unknown answerer refused). Change set: `scripts/gate-answer-check.py`, `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `agents/gate-answerer.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the gate answerer cannot be pointed at another manager's workers, release an MCP call, or answer a card twice.** Review round 1 on #995: (1) `gate-answer-check.py` refuses unless `--self` equals `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (a missing variable refuses) and checks ownership against the environment's value, and it excludes a `role: human` session itself rather than trusting the feed filter; (2) only `Bash`, `Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Read`, `NotebookRead` are unattended-answerable — every `mcp__*`, `WebFetch` and `Agent` call escalates `tool not unattended-answerable`; (3) the pick path is gated off (`skip`, `pick answering disabled until carriers render manager voice`) because attention-controller's pane carrier, its headless `AnswerTurnText` and claude-wrapper's `messageAnswer` all render a card answer as the operator's — and a permission answer's delivery can claim the operator's voice too: of 11 answered permission cards delivered to headless sessions, 7 were carried by the controller's headless arm, whose turn opens "The operator answered on the attention board." (documented in `docs/permission-allowlist.md`); (4) a per-manager ledger (`~/.claude/state/gate-answer/<session>.json`, under an exclusive lock) makes every item a manager judged or left a `skip` for its lifetime, so a feed re-arm never re-judges it; (5) the check prints `expect` (`sha256` of the request id, tool and full input), `--answer` requires it and refuses on a mismatch, a missing prior check or a closed card (exit 4), and a card older than its park — or either timestamp missing — escalates. Also: an unknown tier reads as `operator`; a cut `Bash` command and a `Bash` call that runs a script file (`bash x.sh`, `python3 f.py`, `python3 -m …`, `./x`) escalate in the script, not only in the prompt; `PICK_DENY` gains push, comment/email/post/reply/send, kubectl/launchctl/restart/kill, abort/complete/hold/defer and a bare `approve`. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py` (67 cases; every new case fails against the pre-fix script), including `main()` end to end through the real ledger file — check, answer, second answer refused, re-arm skipped, card closed between check and answer exits 4. Change set: `scripts/gate-answer-check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py`, `agents/gate-answerer.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **review round 2 on #995 — the gate answerer's remaining bypasses are closed.** (1) `attention-answer.py --answer-text` is refused while `PICK_ANSWERING_ENABLED` is false, for `--answerer manager`, and for the `supervisor:gate-answerer` arm under any answerer — every carrier (pane `Operator answer, via attention board:`, the controller's headless `AnswerTurnText`, claude-wrapper's `messageAnswer` `Operator answer, via supervisor:`) voices answer content as the operator's — and `--answerer` no longer defaults to `operator`; (2) `runs_script_file` now catches a shell `-c` body that is not itself allowlist-tier (`bash -c './evil.sh'`, `sh -c 'sh x.sh'`, `ls; bash -c ./x.sh`), every non-shell inline program (`python3 -c`, `node -e`), an unspaced `bash<x.sh`, and the launchers `xargs <interp>`, `find -exec/-execdir`, `env -S`, `uv run`/`npx`/… and `git -c alias.*`/`!…`; `make`, package-manager scripts and `go run` stay `manager` tier on purpose, stated in `RUNNER_WORDS`; (3) `--self` and the session env must be a UUID before any path is built (`--self ../../settings` would have made the ledger overwrite `~/.claude/settings.json`), a refused identity writes nothing, and `SUPERVISOR_GATE_ANSWER_DIR` / `SUPERVISOR_SESSION_HOLDS` are honoured only under `~/.claude/state`; (4) the card's tool must equal the park's; (5) a `Write`/`Edit`/`MultiEdit` outside the worker's cwd and `/tmp`, or under `~/.claude`, escalates; (6) any input cut at the display cap escalates, not only `Bash`. ⚠️ **Documented, not fixed here:** `--expect` binds the judgment to the re-check, not the answer to the park — the store answer carries no `request_id` its carrier honours, and claude-wrapper's `answerFor` settles the producer's current park within a 5 s skew, so the end-to-end binding needs the request id carried through `board.go`. Tests: every case fails against the round-1 scripts — `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py` (+11: `ScriptBypasses`, `Confinement`, `CardAndPark`) and `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py` (`AnswerContentTest` rewritten: five refusals plus the operator relay). Change set: `scripts/gate-answer-check.py`, `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_answer_check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `agents/gate-answerer.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.349.4

- fix: **the goal/topic tier's feed arm now batches its poll, so a goal manager stops paying one turn per gate.** The batching shipped in `v0.341.0` reached only the fleet tier: `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` arms `--feed --digest --self "<sid>" --interval 60`, while `commands/steps/round-tail.md` — the goal and topic tiers' own arm — still armed `--feed --self "<this-session-id>" --interval 60`, with **zero** occurrences of `--digest` in the file. So the tier this goal is measured on kept the per-line render: measured 2026-11-18 22:15–23:00 on a live goal session, **seven `Monitor` events for three distinct sessions**, one poll emitting three lines in a single batch — and each line is a full manager turn. The arm now carries `--digest`, with `attention-watcher.md`'s rationale and the measured per-round cost (29–106 notifications) recorded beside it so the next tier added does not repeat the omission. `scripts/budget-baseline.json` raises `commands/steps/round-tail.md` **by hand** 32 424 → 33 202 — said here rather than hidden, because `--tighten` cannot raise by design. Change set: `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the park nudge no longer orders a session that is not parked to park, and no longer prescribes commands against a task it never resolved.** Three defects in `scripts/park-nudge.py`, all measured against one pusher session that received six `PARK NOW` orders across 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-18 while its registry read `busy`. (1) **An `idle` bucket was read as an idle session.** `session-activity.py::bucket_for` lets a `status: busy` row with no tool calls in its window fall through to bucket `idle`, and park-nudge admitted every `idle` row on `watching_min` alone. A pusher is exactly that between rounds, and its `watching_min` went 29 → 17 between two sends because it restarts per `🟡 WAITING` episode. An `idle` row is now a candidate only when its registry `status` reads `idle`, counted `<n> status-skipped`. This is not a role exclusion, since the rows carry no role. (2) **The order named a placeholder task.** It told every recipient to run `park-event.py register --task "<your anchored task>"` and `vault-cli task set` on the same literal, which has no target for a session with no anchor. The anchor is now resolved through `close-worker.py::find_task_checked`, and the text names the real task. An unanchored session is skipped as inapplicable, like the manager-tier skip, counted `<n> unanchored-skipped`. A failed search is `<n> anchor-unknown` and nudges nothing, because an errored search proves no absence. (3) **The handed-over paths were the sender's version.** `park-event.py` was named by this process's cache dir and `session-self-exit.py` by the newest installed cache version, so both went stale for a recipient on another version. Both are now the unversioned marketplace clones. `--json` gains `status_skipped`, `unanchored` and `anchor_unknown`. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.349.3

- fix: **`manager-predispatch.py` no longer answers CHANGE forever on a subject whose only non-terminal row is future-deferred, and a gated round now settles the stale digest that made the two answers contradict.** Two deadlock paths, both closed. (1) `--print` answered `CHANGE 1 actionable row(s) waiting` because a future `defer_date` keeps the `ready-to-start` bucket KEY by design — the sweep reader's deferred overlay repaints the cell and subtracts the row from the offer, never from the classification — and `actionable_names` now cross-checks the stored classification against the tracked set, dropping future-deferred rows, rows that are terminal on disk (`completed`/`aborted`/`hold`/`backlog`), and names no longer tracked. Measured on the live subject: the stored `ready-to-start` set still carried an `aborted` row, which kept the clause true even after the deferred row was excluded. (2) `--print` also answered `CHANGE digest differs` — `digest_moved` — which the actionable narrowing cannot touch: a gated round never reaches `--save` (`commands/steps/goal-sweep.md` exit 0 dispatches no reader and runs `--render-cached`), so a digest left stale by the last content change was never refreshed and the two answers stayed opposed for as long as the tree stayed quiet. `--predicate --persist` now settles the stored digest to the tree's current one on a FROZEN verdict — authorised by that verdict, since a frozen tree renders the same table it last rendered — and the three gated step files pass the flag. ⚠️ The verdict and the settled digest come from ONE snapshot (`gate_snapshot`), never a re-read: a settle that re-read the tracked set at a later instant baked a content change landing in that window into the stored digest, and the next `--print` then compared equal and never dispatched it — the same fail-closed suppression this change exists to remove, reintroduced by its own fix. Pinned by a test that injects the change at the verdict's own second conjunct (fails pre-fix: `NOCHANGE` where `CHANGE` is owed) plus a mechanism test asserting the tracked set is read exactly once. ⚠️ **And FROZEN gained a THIRD conjunct: the stored sweep's `live_digest` must be unchanged.** The digest hashes liveness death, stuck, hold and membership, none of which moves an mtime, so a quiet tree can hold a worker that died or a hold that landed — and settling the digest past one replayed the stored table over a changed row and never dispatched it, the same suppression this change exists to remove. `live_digest_of` is stored beside the digest by `--save`; when the fresh half differs the verdict is NOT-FROZEN and the legs run, so `--print` and `--predicate` agree on a death instead of contradicting. A record written before the field is "cannot say" → NOT-FROZEN, one sweep, self-healed by its own `--save`. Pinned by a death test and a hold test that both fail pre-fix (`FROZEN` where `NOT-FROZEN` is owed) and by a record-without-the-field case. ⚠️ **And FROZEN gained a FOURTH conjunct: the stored render must postdate every tracked write.** `quiet` only says no mtime moved for ≥2 intervals — not that the table is current: a content write landing while the manager was down is older than the window but newer than the render, so settling it kept the pre-change table and `--print` replayed it forever. `render_postdates` answers NOT-FROZEN in that case, so the sweep runs. The parked set also joined `live_digest_of`: `save_stored` rewrites `parked` from the tracked set it is handed, so a live↔parked flip would otherwise be stored against the old table and defeat `parked_replay_reason`. The settle now reads the record once, through `gate_snapshot`, instead of re-reading it. Pinned by a render-predates-the-write test and a parked-flip test, both failing pre-fix. Measured 2026-11-17 on goal `Manager Token Overhead Falls and the Worker Share Rises` (session `5b29b81a-e7c2-4cca-bd66-2b1b216151aa`, interval 900s): `--print` -> `CHANGE digest differs` (exit 10) against `--predicate` -> `FROZEN` (exit 0). `read_task` carries `defer_date` and `deferred_names` reads it through the shared `defer-date.py`; an unparseable value stays actionable (a replay, never a suppressed dispatch) and a value that has come due is actionable again on the first tick. Tests: deferred-only -> NOCHANGE, due -> CHANGE, unparseable -> CHANGE, terminal entry -> NOCHANGE, untracked entry -> NOCHANGE, a frozen `--predicate --persist` settles a stale digest (the plain verdict leaves it — the negative control), plus a tier-independence case pinning that a `--tier topic` readings gate is honoured by a goal-tier `--check`. `commands/steps/round-readings.md:33` no longer passes `--tier` on the gate call, and `round-quiet.md` no longer claims a `defer_date` moves no input. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md`, `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md`, `commands/steps/round-quiet.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.349.2

- fix: **the round delegate's wait is now bounded by an exit code, not by prose.** `manager-receipt.py --budget --branch fleet` prints the time left in the round and exits **14** once it is spent (`period + SLACK`, the same limit the manager's `--check` uses, imported from `manager-liveness.py`). On 14 the delegate dispatches no further leg and hands back with every unreturned leg `not-run`. ⚠️ **The prose it replaces had already failed:** `agents/fleet-round.md` said "a leg still in flight is `not-run`" and `CHANGELOG v0.344.2` recorded *waiting is not a verdict*, yet the delegate polled five minutes and then five more — which is what pushed round `1795010135` past its 450 s limit and produced the 14-minute overlap the in-flight gate exists to remove. A rule a model can re-read and override is not a bound; a non-zero exit is. The brake cannot interrupt a leg already running — nothing can — but it is what stops the SECOND wait, and the second wait is what made the round long. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `agents/fleet-round.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.349.1

- fix: permission-tier treats only unquoted, unescaped `{a,b}` as brace expansion; quoted braces (`--jq '{a: .x, b: .y}'`, JSON literals) no longer classify `unverifiable`

## v0.349.0

- fix: **a round whose delegate is still running is IN FLIGHT, and neither `--start` nor `--finish` will pretend otherwise.** Measured 2026-11-18 on the Fleet Manager: round `1795010135` was dispatched 14:56:01, `manager-receipt.py --check` answered `INCOMPLETE ROUND — skipped: receipt — round started 707s ago, limit 450s` (exit 11) at 15:07:22, the manager ran `--finish` at 15:07:41 and dispatched round `1795010883` at 15:08:37 — while round 1's delegate ran until **15:22**. Two rounds swept, kept-alive, opened and drove the same fleet for 14 minutes, and the receipt recorded a window 15 minutes shorter than the round it named. ⚠️ **The root cause is an instrument, not a reading:** `commands/steps/round-readings.md` already says a mid-round firing is ABSORBED, but the only signal the manager consulted was the receipt, which reports an over-budget round as a FAILURE verdict and never as *in flight*. Three pieces close it. (1) **The head gate** — `--start` exits **13** and prints the pinned `ABSORBED  <subject>  in flight: <sweep reader|drive leg>  re-armed <n>s` line when the previous receipt is open (`round_ended_at` absent) and its hand-back is absent, and opens no new round; the line is built in the script so its pinned shape cannot drift into prose. (2) **The producer** — `--handback --branch fleet --round <id>` writes `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/<round>.round`, named from the round id the delegate was **dispatched with** (never the current receipt's: a delegate that outlives a forced close would otherwise write its successor's file and let that successor's tail close early). `agents/fleet-round.md` § 5 requires it on every ending, including the `not-run` one. (3) **The tail gate** — `--finish` refuses (exit 13, naming the path) while that file is absent, so an over-budget `--check` can no longer be converted into a closed round; it stamps `round_ended_at` when the file is present. ⚠️ **The wedge is deliberate and bounded, but AGE IS NOT DEATH.** A round past its own limit gets a `LAPSED` line naming the recovery — and that line states the condition rather than issuing the command, because the incident's own round ran 1 586 s against a 450 s limit with its delegate **alive**: force-closing on age would reproduce the overlap this gate exists to prevent. The delegate's turn must be shown to be gone first; a slow delegate is absorbed, not closed. ⚠️ **Fleet-only, deliberately:** goal/topic rounds have no hand-back producer, so the gate there would fire on every round and wedge with no legal close; extending it needs their producer first. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/re-arm.md`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `agents/fleet-round.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` raises its budget entry **by hand** 44 887 → 44 898: `*.round` joins the prune clause, because a file per round with nothing deleting it is exactly the unbounded store that clause exists to bound.

## v0.348.2

- fix: **`round-context-delta.py --receipt` no longer charges untimestamped records to every round.** `window_bytes` counted any record whose `timestamp` was absent or unparsable — *"an unreadable clock is not evidence that the record was outside the window"* — but the untimestamped population is session-wide harness state, not round content: measured 2026-11-18 on the live Fleet Manager, 320 `api-request-blob` records carried **726.7 KB**, and that constant was added to **every** window. Three rounds whose true costs were **228.4 / 184.7 / 155.8 KB** read as **955.1 / 911.4 / 882.5 KB**, and the newest round on `0.345.0` read 774.1 KB against a true **47.4 KB**, under the bound. The median reported at the time, 758.7 KB against the 70 KB bound, was an apparent 10.8× failure of a criterion the newest round met. A record that cannot be placed in a window is now skipped, and `unattributed_bytes` reports the excluded total on stderr (and as `unattributed_bytes` in `--json`) so the omission is visible rather than silent. Change set: `scripts/round-context-delta.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_context_delta.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.348.1

- fix: **the open leg hands `/supervisor:open` the row's raw `name`, not its shell-quoted `action` — an apostrophe in a task title no longer reads as `SESSION UNREADABLE`.** `scripts/open-queue.py` composes `action` with `shlex.quote`, which is correct for a consumer that runs a shell; `Skill` runs none, so a title containing an apostrophe reached `commands/open.md:419` as `sweep-gate.py'"'"'s Home …` — the escape never collapsed, `vault-cli` was asked for a task by that literal name, and Step 2A reported its exit 1 as `❓ SESSION UNREADABLE`. Measured 2026-11-18: the bare title exits 0 and returns its session id, the same title carrying `'"'"'` exits 1, and the three sibling rows the same round selected (no apostrophe) exit 0 — round `1795018507` selected 5 and refused exactly the one row with an apostrophe in its title. `agents/fleet-open-actor.md` § 3 now builds `args` from `selected[].name` verbatim plus the two literal flags and the leg's own `<vault>`, and states why the producer must **not** be unquoted: that quoting protects a `"`, a `$(…)` or a backtick for any consumer that really does pass the string through a shell, so the shell-safe form stays intact for those and this consumer simply stops using it. ⚠️ **This does not explain the 2026-11-18 14:40 round's 7-of-7 refusals** — none of those seven titles contains an apostrophe — so that episode is a second, separate cause and is not claimed as fixed here. **`scripts/check-open-leg-raw-name.py` (new, wired into `precommit`) pins both directions:** it fails when the actor stops naming `selected[].name` or stops warning against `selected[].action`, fails when the "do not unquote the producer" guard is dropped, and carries a positive control that `shlex.quote` still escapes the apostrophe — so the check cannot pass by being stale. Demonstrated on 2026-11-18 against the pre-fix copy (`git show HEAD~1:agents/fleet-open-actor.md`): **exit 1**, `never mentions 'selected[].name'`; against this tree: **exit 0**. Change set: `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `scripts/check-open-leg-raw-name.py` (new), `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a round whose open leg refuses EVERY selected row now fails loudly instead of rendering `opened 0`.** The leg's block reads the same whether it refused all rows or found nothing to open, and the round's tally prints `opened: 0 of N` in the same shape as a round that legitimately resumed rows — measured 2026-11-18, round `1795012825` refused all 7 selected rows with `SESSION UNREADABLE` and the round rendered `opened 0`, a silent fleet-wide stop-start. `scripts/check-open-leg-opened.py` (new) is handed the round's five hand-back counts and answers three ways: `0` rows reached live sessions, `1` **every** selected row was refused, `2` not a verdict. ⚠️ **It takes the hand-back's numbers, never the `.open` payload** — the payload is the block `open-queue.py` printed and carries no tally (measured 2026-11-18: **1 of 90** payloads has an `opened: …` line at all), so a payload-parsing check answers "not a verdict" on every real round; a review caught exactly that in the first cut of this file. ⚠️ **`opened` alone is not the test** — a `♻️ resumed` or `↪️ jumped` row re-enters a live session, so `opened 0 · resumed 3 · refused 1` (round `1795020429`) is healthy and must pass; the failure condition is `opened + resumed + jumped == 0` with `selected > 0`. ⚠️ **`2` is never a pass**: a count missing or unparseable makes the check refuse to conclude rather than guess, and the round then treats the leg as `unknown`. `commands/steps/open.md` item 2 runs it on the hand-back's counts and prints its line as **the round's own** line, not a per-row note. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_check_open_leg_opened.py`, seven cases — all-refused fails, opened and resumed-only pass, nothing-selected passes, and a missing, unparseable or negative count is `2`. Change set: `scripts/check-open-leg-opened.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_check_open_leg_opened.py` (new), `commands/steps/open.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.348.0

- feat: **`goal-loop` reads the who-needs-me endpoint — the last per-tick spawn site in the narrowed SC4(a).** `commands/goal-loop.md` now `curl`s `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me` and formats the answer itself, with the same five guards as `goal-gates` and `goal-status`: `-f` + the `if` + `[ -n "$feed" ]` (a dead port and a zero-length 200 both print nothing and exit 0 otherwise), the `PARTIAL READ` marker for an endpoint answering 200 from the event log, `(.text // "")`/`(.items // [])` so jq cannot abort mid-list, the `((.session_id // "") != $me)` self-exclusion so a manager does not surface its own card as a peer's, and the `(.state // "open") == "open"` filter without which answered rows inflate the count. ⚠️ **The file was already 20 bytes under the 30 KB hard body budget**, so the guards could not fit: `scripts/budget-baseline.json` now carries `commands/goal-loop.md` at **32 251** by hand, and this bullet is the statement of why — the alternative was deleting prose that still describes live behaviour. Verified against the live board: `Needs you (3)` with one row per open gate, and the extracted command exits 0. Change set: `commands/goal-loop.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ **Review round 1 found the row format had drifted from its own prose** — the descriptive tail promised “pane id, age, session label and question text” while the jq printed only `[pane] kind · text`. The format now restores all four (`[pane] ageS session_name kind · text`) **and the 60-char text bound `who-needs-me.py`'s `row()` applied** — without it a fleet-wide gate text flowed into the manager's context unbounded. The duplicated `select(...)` predicate is now a single `def open_gate`, so the header count and the rows cannot drift apart, and the jq-dependency/object-shape caveat the sibling sites carry is stated here too.

## v0.347.0

- fix: **`close-idle-tab.py` closes an unanchored finished tab session instead of refusing `task-unresolved` forever.** A tab session whose id is no page's `claude_session_id` in any configured vault had no automated exit: `close-worker.py` refused `gate-held` then `task-unresolved`, `close-idle-tab.py` refused `task-unresolved`, `pane-wake.py` refused — so it held a spawn slot until a human closed the tab. `reap-idle-headless.py` has closed exactly this shape as `unanchored-closed` since `v0.303.1`, but its chain requires the session be headless, so the fleet's own ruling reached one population and not the other. `close-idle-tab.py` now splits the no-page state the way the reaper does (`reap-idle-headless.py:783-805`): a proven absence of anchor across every searched `tasks_dir` is `unanchored-closed`, while `vault-not-searched` and an empty dir list keep the `task-unresolved` refusal — an absent search is not an absent anchor, and without that guard an empty `vault-cli` config would close every tab in one tick. ⚠️ **A failed SEARCH is not an absent anchor**, and that is the whole risk of the change: `find_task` returns `None` on a grep timeout or a grep exit ≥ 2 exactly as it does on a genuine no-match, so a transient read would have closed a live anchored tab. `close-worker.py` gains `find_task_checked`, returning `(task, error)`, and `_grep_task` now carries the failure out as a third element; `find_task` keeps its signature for the callers whose next step is a refusal, where collapsing to `None` fails closed. ⚠️ **And the absence must be proven over the FLEET's directory set, not the configured one.** The first cut searched only `vault-cli`-configured `tasks_dir`s, while the sweep that decides what may be closed probes every vault under the Obsidian root, falling back to `PROBE_DIRS` for unconfigured ones — so a task in an unconfigured vault read as absent and its live anchored pane was killed. `close-worker.py` gains `fleet_task_dirs`, unioning the root-wide probe with the configured scan (which also reaches a configured vault outside the root), and both `find_task_checked` and the task gate use it. ⚠️ The four upstream guards are untouched and still refuse (`held-session`, `gate-held`, `busy-target`, `not-tab`), pinned by `TestTabGate`. Change set: `scripts/close-idle-tab.py`, `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_idle_tab.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the goal-status step reads the who-needs-me endpoint, with all five silent-clear and self-relay guards.** `commands/goal-status.md` now `curl`s `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me` and formats the answer itself, so the per-round feed verdict costs no interpreter spawn. ⚠️ **Five guards, each load-bearing:** (1) the `if`, `-f` and `[ -n "$feed" ]` — a bare `curl … | jq …` reports **jq's** status, so an unreachable store prints nothing and exits 0 (measured against a dead port: **exit 0 with no output**), and a 200 with a zero-length body passes `-f` and prints nothing too; (2) the **`PARTIAL READ`** line — the endpoint answers HTTP 200 **from the event log** when the store is unreadable (`source: "event-log"`, `sources.store: "unreadable"`), so a degraded answer otherwise renders identically to a healthy one, and every `⟳replay` row is not live; (3) `(.text // "")` and `(.items // [])` — either unguarded form makes jq abort after printing part of the list; (4) the **self-exclusion** `((.session_id // "") != $me)` — the endpoint is fleet-wide, so without it a manager surfaces **its own** card as a peer gate and can relay the answer into its own pane; (5) the **`(.state // "open") == "open"` filter** — without it answered and closed rows inflate `Needs you (<n>)` and render as live gates. Also drops the now-dead `CAP = 20` / *"N more — pass --all"* truncation warning (the endpoint has no row cap) and states that `jq` is a runtime dependency of the step. ⚠️ The guards cost bytes, so `scripts/budget-baseline.json` raises `commands/goal-status.md` **by hand** 46 997 → 48 645 — said here rather than hidden, because `--tighten` cannot raise by design. Change set: `commands/goal-status.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.346.0

- fix: **the covered-subject invariant runs the daemon's Go gate instead of the retired vault script.** `sweep-gate.py` was ported into `bborbe/claude-wrapper` and deleted from the vault on 2026-11-18, so `commands/steps/covered-subject-invariant.md` now invokes `claude-wrapper -sweep-gate-mode invariant -sweep-gate-topic '<subject>' -sweep-gate-vault '<vault>'`. ⚠️ **The exit contract is the port's, and it differs from the script's:** `Invariant` returns `0` on every path and prints a payload only when it fires, so a non-zero exit now means the binary is missing or unrunnable. **The port never emits `3`** — `pkg/sweepgate/mode_invariant.go` says so in its own doc comment, because `3` is reserved for the caller — so the old "another layer holds the ask" arm no longer describes anything this command can receive and is retired rather than folded into "unavailable". The `python3` exit-`2` and `sweep-gate.py --tick` references below the edited block are corrected in the same pass. Change set: `commands/steps/covered-subject-invariant.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.345.0

- fix: `scripts/session-liveness.py` no longer licenses ABSENT for a registry record whose `alive` is `False` while the session's transcript was written within 120 s (`TRANSCRIPT_RECENT_SECONDS`). `alive: False` is a statement about the RECORD's pid — gone, or the number recycled onto another process — so a record that outlived its session read as death for a session still writing: measured 2026-11-17, two live sessions read ABSENT 11 s and 34 s after a transcript write, both held `busy` on a recycled pid. The arm reuses `_transcript_idle_seconds`, answers UNKNOWN inside the window, and keeps today's ABSENT when the transcript resolves to nothing or is older — so a genuinely dead session still reads ABSENT and the reaper keeps the one verdict it reclaims on. ⚠️ **Both ABSENT licences carry the guard**: `_absent_licensed` (the per-id 404 path) and `_local_verdict` (the endpoint-down disk fallback added by #977), on one shared `TRANSCRIPT_RECENT_SECONDS` constant and one shared resolver, each asserted by its own test class so neither can regress behind the other. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness_absent_licensed.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the goal-gates step reads the who-needs-me endpoint instead of spawning the script every round, with all five silent-clear and self-relay guards.** `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` now `curl`s `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me` and formats the answer itself, so the `waiting-on-human` sweep spawns no interpreter. ⚠️ **Two review rounds each found real gaps in the first cut, all now closed.** (1) **`-f`, the `if` and `[ -n "$feed" ]`** — a bare `curl … | jq …` reports **jq's** status, so an unreachable store printed nothing and exited 0 (measured against a dead port: **exit 0 with no output**); a 200 with a zero-length body passes `-f` and also prints nothing. (2) **The partial-read marker** — the endpoint answers **HTTP 200 from the event log** when the store is unreadable (`source: "event-log"`, `sources.store: "unreadable"`), so a degraded answer renders identically to a healthy one; the read now names the source and stamps `⟳replay` on every replayed row. (3) **`(.text // "")` and `(.items // [])`** — either unguarded form makes jq abort after printing part of the list, so the header count stops matching the rows. (4) **The self-exclusion `((.session_id // "") != $me)`** — the endpoint is fleet-wide, so without it a manager that posted a card surfaces **its own** card as a peer gate and can relay the answer into its own pane; `who-needs-me.py`'s `load()` removes the caller for exactly this reason. (5) **The `(.state // "open") == "open"` filter** — without it answered and closed rows inflate `Needs you (<n>)` and render as live gates; measured live, it moves the count 13 → 4. Also filters to the `permission`/`question` kinds, normalizes a trailing slash on `ATTENTION_STORE_URL`, renders `hst` only for a headless row (`?` otherwise), and states that `jq` is a runtime dependency of the step. Change set: `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.5

- fix: **a completed round's window now survives the next round's start.** `--start` builds the receipt FRESH, so it silently dropped the `round_ended_at` that `--finish` had just written: the completed window existed only from one round's tail to the next round's head, which nothing can read. Measured 2026-11-18 — `--finish` had run, the very next `--start` wiped it, `round_ended_at` read `None` on every inspection, and the round stayed unmeasurable **with the fix already deployed**. `--start` now carries `previous_round_ended_at` beside `previous_round_started_at` — the same reasoning that already carried the start — and `round-context-delta.py --receipt` prefers that pair, falling back to the current one for receipts written before the end was carried. `previous_round_started_at → previous_round_ended_at` is the one window that IS a completed round. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/round-context-delta.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_context_delta.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.4

- fix: **`restart-worker.py --absent` resumes a manager whose registration's `vault` value is a path or a stale name, not only a configured vault name.** `resume_absent` resolved the vault by exact name equality, so the supported resume path refused exactly the sessions it exists to recover. Measured 2026-11-18 across all 198 registrations in `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/`: **42 (21 %) could not be resumed at all** — 6 carried a path and 36 a stale or renamed name (`personal` 19, `Personal` 9, `Brogrammers` 3, `brogrammers` 3, `Obsidian` 1, `25 Tasks` 1) — and the workaround was hand-spawning eight manager tabs in one session. The values come from three writers that do not validate against the configured names: `fleet-register.py` defaults `--vault` to the cwd's basename, `docs/subject-resolution.md` § Recording lowercases whatever it is handed, and records written before 2026-09-23 may carry a display-case name. `resolve_registered_vault()` now resolves first-match-wins: exact configured name → a path-shaped value whose directory is a configured vault's path (compared by `samefile`, so a case-only difference resolves on APFS) → a case-insensitive name match → the legacy alias table (`personal`/`Personal` → `private-personal`, `Brogrammers`/`brogrammers` → `seibert-brogrammers`). A value matching none of these is **refused rather than guessed** — resuming a manager into the wrong vault is worse than refusing it. The refusal message is corrected in the same change: it now says the value **matches no configured vault**, naming that value and the configured names it was compared against, instead of claiming the named directory does not exist (which was never the condition — `24 Goals` named no directory at all). A configured vault whose own path is missing keeps a separate message that names the path. Tests: seven CLI cases in `test_restart_worker.py::RegisteredVaultShapes` — one per resolvable shape plus both no-target values — asserting the resolved `cwd` for each, since a wrong-but-plausible resolution is the failure mode that matters. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.3

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py --check` reports a deliberately stood-down subject, so a stand-down reaches the notification path and not only `--state`.** `classify()` already returned `STOPPED` for a `.stopped` marker, but `check()` acted only on `STALE` / `INVALID`, so a stood-down manager was observable on demand and invisible to the report — the reason every forced-lapse probe was voided. A `STOPPED` subject is now emitted once its marker is older than `2 x` its own observed period (declared interval as the fallback), carries the `STOPPED` token with `age=`/`threshold=`, and re-emits with `retry=1` until delivered. A stand-down is not a lapse: the pending entry carries `kind="stopped"`, so a resume drops the episode rather than re-emitting `RECOVERED-PENDING`. Pinned by three new tests (silent before the bound, the line plus retry after it, and STOPPED-vs-STALE tokens).
- feat: **`manager-liveness.py --state` reports `EXITED` instead of `OK` when the arm record names a session that is gone.** A `.cadence` file is written by `--arm` and nothing deletes it when the session exits, so a manager that died minutes ago kept a FRESH record and read `OK`: measured 2026-11-18, five flagged managers had exited while `--state` reported `OK`, so the board showed them closed and the fleet showed them healthy, and they were found only by reading `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` by hand. ⚠️ **`EXITED` requires agreement, not a single negative channel** — Claude Code deletes the registry row on exit, so "no entry" also covers a session spawned moments ago; the verdict needs no non-dead registry entry for the recorded id **and** a transcript that has stopped moving past the subject's own limit, the same agreement `_absent_licensed` applies on the liveness path. ⚠️ **A gate-watched goal manager is never `EXITED`**: the goal tier's liveness is its sweep gate, not a live model session, so an unregistered `.session` id with a still transcript is its normal state — without that guard every gate-driven manager reads `EXITED` and the `IDLE` branch becomes unreachable. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
