- fix: **`session-liveness.py` no longer goes blind when the attention store restarts — the on-disk channels answer in its place, as a union rather than a priority flip.** Measured 2026-11-18: `attention-controller` was down ~70 minutes, `--list --json` exited 2 with `ECONNREFUSED`, `--check` answered `UNKNOWN` for every id, 15 of 25 flagged rows read `degraded`, and nothing could be opened — while the identity registry sat readable on disk the whole time. `--check` now falls back to the identity registry (a pid that is running answers `LIVE`) and then the headless heartbeat store, and `--list --json` exits 0 built from those two channels with **every row `degraded: true`**. ⚠️ **`ABSENT` is still never licensed from a missing registry entry** — the registry deletes an entry on exit, so "no entry" covers a session that died, one spawned moments ago, and (with the endpoint down) every cluster and a2a session; the fallback licenses `ABSENT` only from an entry the registry HOLDS and computes dead, the same `alive is not False` predicate `_absent_licensed` already uses. ⚠️ **The endpoint-down fallback omits cluster and a2a sessions**, so `commands/open.md` Step 2B item 1 and Step 2C now stop on the `WARNING — the session-heartbeat endpoint … is unreadable` line instead of reading a no-match as "none is running now" — that fall-through was the duplicate-spawn hazard. ⚠️ **The stop keys on that warning, never on a `degraded: true` row**: `_list_payload` marks a row degraded for every registry-live session with no heartbeat row, the ordinary stale-plugin population, so a row-keyed stop would refuse to open managers in normal operation. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/session-identity.py`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.2

- fix: **the `fleet-round` delegate must never end a turn without its hand-back — a leg that has not returned is `not-run`, not a reason to wait.** Measured 2026-11-18: delegate `agent-a6499e16a04a576f1` dispatched `supervisor:fleet-sweep-reader`, found it still running, said *"The sweep is still running. Waiting for its hand-back before the drive leg can be gated."* and **terminated with `finished: True` and no hand-back at all**. The manager then had no verdict, could not render, never reached its tail, and the round never closed — **two rounds hung exactly this way** (09:57 and 13:33), both leaving `round_ended_at` unset. ⚠️ **Waiting is not a verdict**: an unreturned leg's counts are `not-run` with the reason, every row depending on it is `not-run` too, the rest of the round proceeds on what it has, and the hand-back says so. Change set: `agents/fleet-round.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.1

- fix: **an unreadable vault no longer kills the reader that walks it, so the exit-observer keeper can sweep at all.** `fleet-sessions.py`'s `_walk_task_stamps()` called `root.iterdir()` unguarded, and a launchd job holds no TCC grant for `~/Documents`: measured 2026-11-18, every coverage sweep the keeper ran died there with `PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted` **before resolving a single pane**, so the keeper's watch set was always empty and it could never re-arm an observer. ⚠️ **The keeper's own log hid the cause**: it truncates the child's stderr to 400 characters, which cut the traceback before the exception line — 630 `sweep failed` against 72 successes in one log, all this one error. `vault_index()` in `fleet-board.py` already degrades on the same condition ("a missing `~/Documents/Obsidian` degrades to an empty index rather than aborting the board"); this walk sits on that very call path and now degrades identically — an unreadable vault costs the board its titles, not the sweep its life. ⚠️ **A `launchctl submit` run with the default PATH does NOT reproduce it** — with no `wezterm` on PATH the same script takes a different branch and reports every manager `unknown` with no crash, which is why this looked like two separate defects. Change set: `scripts/fleet-sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_sessions.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.344.0

- feat: `scripts/flagged-rows.py` emits a new `parked` branch for a row whose last closer declares an external wait (`⏰ Ends:`), read once per run through `declared-wait.py` so there is one definition of "waiting" — the branch sits after every LIVE branch and before `resume`/`create`, so an open session is never counted as waiting and a parked row is never counted absent

## v0.343.2

- fix: **the exit-observer keeper now watches the fleet instead of watching nothing.** Three defects stacked. (1) `coverage()` resolved a manager's pane by TITLE only, so a retitled tab made the row unresolvable: measured 2026-11-18, **all** live manager rows resolved to `None` and each landed in `unknown` carrying no `pane` — and `sweep()` skips rows with no pane, so the keeper's watch set was **empty** while twelve panes carried live writers. `unrecorded: 0` therefore read as "all healthy" with nothing being checked. The store already holds that join — every record line carries `session=` — so `resolve_pane_by_session()` is the fallback, preferring a pane with a live spawn, refusing a tie rather than guessing, and refusing a missing stamp rather than letting it lose to a stamped stale pane. ⚠️ **The fallback alone was a no-op in production, and that is worth stating rather than burying**: `live_coverage()` narrowed the store read to the panes the TITLE join resolved *before* `coverage()` ran, so a retitled tab's log was never opened and the fallback scanned a dict that could not contain the answer. The narrowing is now conditional — a full read when any manager fails the title join, the cheap narrowed read when all resolve — and `TestLiveCoverage` drives the wrapper so the regression cannot return green. (2) `sweep()` built its watch set from `unrecorded` + `unknown` alone, i.e. only the panes ALREADY broken, so the first death of a healthy writer stayed invisible until the next full sweep (up to `REFRESH_SECONDS` = 600 s) — SC2's 60 s bound was unreachable by construction. `coverage()` now also reports `recorded` (the covered manager panes) and the keeper watches those too. **Measured through the production path after all three: watch set `0 → 17` panes, `unknown` `5 → 0`.** Change set: `scripts/exit-record-coverage.py`, `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_record_coverage.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_observer_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.343.1

- fix: **`--state` measures the liveness period from a gap-free tail, so an overnight stop no longer stretches the OK window to most of a day.** `observed_period()` took the p90 and the median over the last 12 arms, and a manager that stopped overnight wrote its ~17 h gap into both — measured 2026-11-18: `period=23997s`, `limit=24177s` (~6.7 h), so a genuinely stopped loop read `OK` for hours. The ring is now cut at its last span past `IDLE_GAP_SECONDS = 7200` and the period is measured from the tail; a tail under three spans keeps the old capped reading rather than dropping to `None`, since `None` moves every caller onto the declared interval. The break test is absolute rather than a multiple of the observed cadence — a relative threshold has to estimate the cadence first, and that estimate is exactly what the gap corrupts. Four fixtures carrying real idle gaps move accordingly (1740→1615, 1740→1552, 3937→3930 twice, and the historic-outage test now asserts the real 900 s period instead of the 2250 s the 2.5× cap used to bound it to).

## v0.343.0

- feat: **a round now records where it ENDED, so a round can be measured instead of inferred.** `manager-receipt.py --finish` stamps `round_ended_at` on the receipt, and the fleet round's tail runs it last; `round-context-delta.py --receipt` then measures `round_started_at -> round_ended_at` exactly. ⚠️ **The absence of an end was not a detail — it was the whole defect.** With only a start, every consumer invents the end: measured 2026-11-18 on ONE transcript, bounding a round by the next fire gave **75.4 KB**, by the first closer **8.3 KB**, and after the delegate under the same closer rule **233.1 KB** — three answers from one dataset, none of them the round. Fire→fire charges every unrelated turn between rounds; fire→first-closer charges everything up to whichever closer comes first. ⚠️ **`--finish` refuses when no round is open** rather than inventing an end, and `--receipt` exits 2 on a receipt with no `round_ended_at`, naming `--finish` — a missing end is reported, never approximated. The inferred modes stay for transcripts recorded before this change, and their docstrings now say they are inference. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/round-context-delta.py`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `scripts/tests/test_round_context_delta.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.342.0

- feat: **`commands/steps/round-readings.md` reads the attention feed from the service instead of spawning `who-needs-me.py`.** It ran `python3 …/who-needs-me.py --section needs-you` — an interpreter plus 2 788 lines of parsing per read — for data the `attention-controller` already serves at `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me`. It now uses a `curl -f` + `jq` filter that prints the `Needs you (<n>)` header and one row per open gate carrying the pane/`hst` key, the kind and the text, so `manager-receipt.py`'s `needs_you_shape()` still matches and the prose around the call still describes what it sees. ⚠️ **`--fail` is load-bearing**: without it a 404/500 whose body is the store's JSON error envelope reaches `jq`, which prints `Needs you (0)` and satisfies the receipt check on a feed that was never read. ⚠️ **The header line is load-bearing** — a rows-only artifact can never satisfy that check, and every goal/topic round's tail check would report INCOMPLETE and exit 11. ⚠️ **`commands/goal-status.md` and `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` were migrated and then REVERTED**: a bare `curl | jq` yields empty stdout and exit 0 both for an empty feed and for an unreachable service, so the `unread` vs `clear` distinction their prose requires could no longer be made — a fail-open regression the script's refusal marker prevented. They keep the spawn until a client that fails loudly on an unreachable store exists. Change set: `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.341.2

- fix: **the `fleet-round` delegate now emits its hand-back fence and nothing else, because it was returning roughly twice the contract.** Measured 2026-11-18 from the six `supervisor:fleet-round` subagent transcripts under a live manager: the returned text was **1 612 / 1 969 / 2 086 / 2 525 / 2 627 bytes** — median **2 086 B against the 1 024 B cap**, every measurable run over. The cause is not the fence, which is 542 B: the delegate wrapped it in a "Final state of this round" table, a per-leg narrative, and a trailing `note:` line ("keepalive (5b) not-run — round hit the 15-min bound"). ⚠️ **`check-handback-size.py` cannot catch this and never claimed to** — it asserts the *authored template*, and its own docstring says it pins the authored shape, not a live round. The contract now states the rule the measurement implies: the hand-back stops at its last count, a leg's `not-run` reason is payload detail, and no prose surrounds the fence. ⚠️ **The fix is behavioural and is verified by re-measuring from the subagent transcripts, not by a static check** — a static check can only assert that the instruction is present. Change set: `agents/fleet-round.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.341.1

- fix: **`round-context-delta.py` measures only CLOSED rounds, so a transcript with one marker no longer reports an 8.5 MB "round".** A round is measured from its fire to the next fire; the trailing segment has no closing fire, so everything after it — a later unrelated turn, a whole session's tail — was charged to it. Measured 2026-11-18 on `cfaf1455`: one marker in the whole transcript produced a single round of **8 508 KB**, and the same defect inflated `8605c0bb`'s last round to 377 KB. The trailing segment is now flagged `open` and dropped by default; a transcript whose only markers are unclosed exits **2** naming the reason, because an unclosed segment is not a measurement and a number there is worse than no number. `--include-open` reports it for a live session mid-round. Corrected reading on `8605c0bb`: **20 closed rounds, median 72.0 KB, max 1087.1 KB** (was 21 rounds / 75.4 KB). Change set: `scripts/round-context-delta.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_context_delta.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.341.0

- feat: **`gate-owner-filter.py --feed --digest` collapses a multi-pane poll into ONE line, so a busy poll costs the manager one watcher wake instead of one per gate.** Every line the feed prints becomes a separate `Monitor` event, and each wake costs the manager a notification framing plus a deliberation — measured 2026-11-18 on a live Fleet Manager round: **29–106 such notifications per round**, up to 25% of a quiet round's bytes and the trigger for its thinking. `--digest` prints `FEED  new: <n>  <pane> | <pane> …` when a poll emits more than one pane; a **single** pane still renders bare, so a quiet poll is byte-identical to the old output and a consumer expecting a pane id keeps working on the common path. The flag is **opt-in**: `--feed` without it is unchanged, because the per-pane shape is a contract other consumers read. The fleet round's row 13 now arms with `--digest`. ⚠️ **This reduces framing, not judgement** — the manager still acts on each gate, so it is an improvement to the round's cost, not a route to a 3 KB round. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the last two per-tick `who-needs-me.py` spawns are gone: `box-table.py` and `drive-unverifiable.py` resolve a session's pane from the service.** Both called `who-needs-me.py --pane-for <sid8>` — a fresh interpreter per row per render, for an answer the `attention-controller` already holds in its `sessions[]` (`session_id` + `pane`). Each now asks `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me` in-process. ⚠️ **The ambiguity rule is preserved, not lost with the spawn**: two sessions sharing a prefix resolve to `""`, never the first match — a guessed pane links the operator to the wrong tab. A failed read is `""` too, so a broken service degrades to the plain-text cell it was before links existed, never to a dead link. `box-table` keeps exactly one spawn — `jump-link.py`, a different concern — and its now-unused `WHO_NEEDS_ME` constant is removed. `drive-unverifiable.resolve_pane` keeps its `script` parameter, accepted and ignored, because callers and tests still pass it. Change set: `scripts/box-table.py`, `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_box_table.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.340.1

- fix: **`scripts/deliver-message.py` calls the MCP tool the wrapper actually serves — `send_agent_message`, not `send_message` — and resolves the agent handle instead of passing the session uuid through.** `tools/list` on the live daemon returns `spawn_agent, send_agent_message, list_agents, agent_status, stop_agent, answer_permission, …`, so every default delivery since `v0.335.0` failed `unknown tool "send_message"` while `--nudge` (REST, 202) kept working; measured 2026-11-17 against a live headless worker whose registry row was moved aside. The arguments are `agent_id` and `text`, read from the live `tools/list` inputSchema rather than from prose — that schema marks exactly those two required. ⚠️ `agent_id` is a **roster handle, not always the session uuid**: measured 2026-11-18 over 74 live rows, 58 carried `agent_id == session_id` and 16 carried `<uuid>~<sid8>`, so the bare uuid reaches a daemon-spawned worker and answers `agent "<uuid>" is not one this daemon created` for the rest. `resolve_agent_handle` now reads the handle from `list_agents` and refuses, naming the id, when no row matches. ⚠️ A successful call is still not proof of a delivered turn: on a headless worker the call returns no result and the transcript keeps only its spawn prompt, which the docstring now says rather than implying the route carries one. **`delivered` is therefore read from the tool's own hand-back, not from "the call returned"** — `_mcp_delivery_verdict` reads `delivered` before `sent` (a pane hand-back carries `sent: true` beside `delivered: false` when the turn stranded), takes an explicit `true` as delivered, an explicit `false` as a refusal, and a hand-back carrying neither as unknown, so the script can no longer print `delivered: true` for a message that reached nobody. `scripts/tests/test_deliver_message.py` pins the tool name, both argument keys, the four handle-resolution outcomes and the five delivery-verdict shapes — the defect that shipped silently had no test at all. The same change lands the instrument precedence rule in `docs/fleet-surface.md` — one line per instrument stating its question and what may not be concluded from it. Change set: `scripts/deliver-message.py`, `scripts/tests/test_deliver_message.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.340.0

- feat: **`round-readings.py` reads the who-needs-me answer from the service instead of spawning the script.** The second of the per-tick spawn sites: a manager round re-ran `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you --all` through `subprocess.run`, paying a fresh interpreter plus 2 788 lines of parsing to re-derive data the `attention-controller` already holds. It now reads `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me` and spawns nothing. ⚠️ **`--all` disappears with the render.** The 20-row cap was the reason that flag was mandatory; the payload carries every open item, so a gated row can no longer fall past a cap and read as a false `clear`. ⚠️ **The retry semantics and the failure shape are unchanged**: one retry on a failed read, one on an event-log answer, then `state: unread` / `partial` with the source NAMED — a fallback read still cannot certify a row clear. `parse_feed` stays as the render-side reference the parity tests hold the JSON mapping against; it is no longer on the production path. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.339.1

- fix: **a failed sweep now says why it failed.** The failure path caught `TimeoutExpired / ValueError / OSError` and printed one generic line, so the reason was thrown away — and four separate theories about the same failure (a blocking read, the enumeration's cost, host load, the interpreter) were all plausible and all wrong for want of the actual error. It now logs the return code and the subprocess's stderr on a non-zero exit, the timeout value on a timeout, the exception on a start failure, and the first bytes of stdout when it is not JSON. No behaviour change beyond the message.

## v0.339.0

- feat: **`scripts/round-context-delta.py` measures a fleet round's context cost from the transcript, so "≤ 3 KB per round" is a reading rather than an estimate.** The 2026-11-17 breakdown was computed once by hand and never reproduced; the task that depends on it could only name "the same breakdown script" and nothing could run it. The script segments a transcript at the scheduler's own markers (`scheduledFireId` / `scheduledTaskId` on a fire whose prompt starts with the round name) and reports each round's byte delta — main-thread text only, with `isSidechain` records excluded because a sub-agent's text lives in its own transcript and reaches the manager as one hand-back message, which *does* count. ⚠️ **Three shapes would make the measurement lie, and each is pinned by a test:** text before the first marker is setup and never charged to round 1; a transcript with no markers exits **2**, never a zero dressed as a clean reading; and a fire whose prompt names something else (`/supervisor:goal-drive`) is not a round boundary. Measured on the predecessor `fbad5082`: **7 rounds, median 40.0 KB, max 531.5 KB** — the baseline the ≤ 3 KB target is set against. Change set: `scripts/round-context-delta.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_context_delta.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.338.2

- fix: **`session-liveness.py` answers ABSENT for a session whose heartbeat row has expired**, so an old session can be resumed. With no identity-registry entry and no usable heartbeat row (404, or a row with `live` and `age_seconds` null), the verdict stayed UNKNOWN forever — `flagged-rows.py` mapped it to `degraded`, which never resumes — because the stale-row rule of #954 needs a row to exist and the store drops rows after a retention window. ABSENT is now licensed when the transcript (`<config dir>/projects/*/<id>.jsonl`, newest match) has been idle over 15 minutes (`TRANSCRIPT_IDLE_SECONDS`), which disproves "spawned moments ago"; no transcript, a fresh one, or an unreadable endpoint keeps UNKNOWN, and the message names the evidence. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.338.1

- fix: **the keeper backs off a full refresh after a failed sweep, and its sweep timeout sits below its own staleness threshold.** A failed sweep was retried after 60 s, and a sweep costs ~55 s (`build_task_index` 21 s + `build_grouping` 33 s, measured 2026-11-17), so the retry re-scanned the vault almost continuously — which kept the vault hot and every sweep slow enough to time out again. A failure now waits `REFRESH_SECONDS`. `SWEEP_TIMEOUT` drops 300 → 120, below `exit-record-coverage.py`'s `KEEPER_STALE_SECONDS` (300), because a sweep that blocks longer than that threshold makes the board report the keeper itself as stale — a false alarm the old value allowed by construction.

## v0.338.0

- feat: **`gate-owner-filter.py` reads the who-needs-me answer from `attention-controller` instead of spawning `who-needs-me.py` on every poll.** In `--interval` mode the filter re-ran `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you` through `subprocess.run` once per manager per minute — a fresh Python interpreter plus 2 788 lines of parsing, measured 2026-11-16 at ~1.3–2.7 cores across the fleet and the largest single consumer on the machine. The three inputs that work re-derives (the attention store, the WezTerm pane list and the session registry) are all already held by the long-running `attention-controller`, which now serves the same answer as JSON at `GET /api/1.0/who-needs-me`; the filter reads that route and spawns nothing. ⚠️ **The feed and the headless records come from ONE request.** `endpoint_snapshot()` returns both, because the service's payload already carries each headless row's `session_id` and `text` — the second per-poll `/api/1.0/attention` read `headless_items_checked` would otherwise make is exactly the load this change exists to keep off the board. `--producer` is retained and still wins when given: it is the test seam every fixture in `tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py` drives, and the escape hatch on a host without the board — but it is no longer the default, because a default that shells out re-buys the cost for every manager that never passes the flag. ⚠️ **A failed read ends the loop non-zero**, exactly as a refused pipe does: a quiet fleet and an unreachable board leave the same zero rows on the wire, and only one of them is a measurement. The emitted row keeps the shape every downstream hop reads — `(pane, replayed)` for a pane-bearing item, `hst:<store item id>` for a headless one, and `replayed` taken from the row's own flag rather than parsed off a render. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/endpoint_fixture.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.337.3

- docs: the README tool table lists `run_slash_command(agent_id, command)`, the roster tool claude-wrapper v0.32.0 serves (`bborbe/claude-wrapper#91`): one allowlisted `/compact` or `/reload-plugins` run in a local worker as a real command — a headless worker receives it on stdin, where `send_agent_message` can only hand back a `SendMessage` name and a peer message runs no command, and a tab worker has it typed into its pane. Anything else is refused with `not allowlisted`; A2A and cluster workers are refused by name.

## v0.337.2

- fix: **`flagged-rows.py` probes session liveness concurrently, so a daemon tick no longer spends minutes spawning ~68 interpreters one after another.** Rows are now prepared first, then every distinct session id the run needs (row, owner manager, fleet manager) is probed once through `session-liveness.py --check` on a `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`PROBE_WORKERS = 8`) and read back from one shared result; verdicts are unchanged. A new total deadline (`PROBE_DEADLINE = 60` s) turns any sid still unanswered into `UNKNOWN`, never `ABSENT`, because `ABSENT` licenses a second session; the per-probe `PROBE_TIMEOUT` stays. Measured 2026-11-17: ~68 sequential spawns took ~26 s by hand and 3m24s-12m30s under daemon load (load 120-300), skipping ticks while a run was in flight; a hand run now takes ~5 s against ~19 s before on the same machine. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.337.1

- fix: **the goal drive leg structurally holds a future-deferred row, so a parked row is never handed over as `To open`.** `agents/goal-drive.md` clause (6) held only on terminal, parked and unflagged-`todo` rows; a `planning` row with a future `defer_date` still classified `ready-to-start`, passed the readiness bar and was handed to the caller as `To open`, so the round printed `🚀 READY TO START: … start a session` for work deliberately parked. The hold is now the fourth hold reason, `held-on-defer`: a `defer_date` strictly in the future — both stored shapes, the quoted `"YYYY-MM-DD"` and the unquoted RFC3339 — emits no action line and opens nothing, while a value at or before the current instant is due and still offers the row (the instant comparison `scripts/defer-date.py`'s `defer_state` makes). It is the same predicate the vault gate's `is_deferred()` applies, and the drive leg's half of the rule `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` § step 4 already states in prose. Measured 2026-11-15: the round improvised a prose `held-on-defer` because nothing enforced it. Change set: `agents/goal-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.337.0

- feat: **the fleet round's machine half now runs in ONE delegate, so a round costs the manager a verdict instead of a rulebook.** `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md` (new) carries the manager's round, pointed at from `commands/fleet-drive.md`: the manager reads the receipt and re-arms (row 1), then dispatches `supervisor:fleet-round` once with the round id, its session id, the vault and the tasks dir — the digest path is the delegate's to derive. The delegate (`agents/fleet-round.md`) reads the step files, runs rows 0, 2–6, 12, 15, 16, 16b and 18 — dispatching the existing sweep, keep-alive, readings and open legs — persists the round, and returns the ≤ 1 KB verdict plus the artifact paths. The manager keeps rows 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 13, 14, 17, 19 and 20: the provenance acts, the cause check, the operator questions, the watchers, the voice, the round tail and the render. Measured 2026-11-17 on the predecessor `fbad5082`: ≈ 210 KB a round went on step-file rules and ≈ 145 KB on raw script output; at 419,584 tokens it self-diagnosed exhaustion at 42% of its 967k window and stopped dispatching the drive leg from 10:13. ⚠️ **Arming stays in the manager and is unchanged** — a delegate's lifetime ends with its turn, so an arm written inside it would expire with it; and the round now states that **a compaction never leaves the loop armed-but-not-driven**, since a wakeup that re-arms and returns has lost the round. Change set: `agents/fleet-round.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/round-dispatch.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`context-usage.py --self-pct` prints a manager's own fill level, the one reading every other view hides.** `is_candidate` excludes a self row by design — compacting yourself loses the loop state mid-round — so the `--compactable` view, which is what the sweep's `context70` hand-back is built on, drops the self row from `shown` entirely. A manager reading only that view saw no row for itself and inferred exhaustion from nothing: measured 2026-11-17, a Fleet Manager declared "out of context" at 419,584 tokens, 42% of its 967k window. The flag prints this session's `used_percentage` and exits, before any filter; `UNKNOWN` when the row or the session id is absent, never `0` — a `0%` is the same inference error pointing the other way. The round's hand-back carries it as `self-context=`. Change set: `scripts/context-usage.py`, `scripts/tests/test_context_usage.py`, `agents/fleet-round.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- test: **the hand-back contract's ≤ 1 KB cap is asserted, not merely described.** `docs/handback-contract.md` § The shape has said ≤ 1 KB since it was written, and nothing measured it — every leg could exceed the cap on every round and no check failed, the same "a rule that lives only in prose" defect `check-handback-keys.py` catches one layer down for keys. `scripts/check-handback-size.py` measures each registered leg's own `<output_format>` fence against 1024 bytes, excluding the lines the contract declares **variable** (the truncation exception: `crash-loop:` / `cannot-tell:` / `park-nudge:` rows, `refused:` lines) so a correctly truncating leg does not fail. It reads `check-handback-keys.LEGS` rather than a second registry, so a new leg is guarded by both checks and the two cannot drift. Wired as `make check-handback-size` into `precommit`. Current readings: keepalive 516 B, open 180 B, fleet-round 479 B, goal-spawn 619 B, readings 355 B. Change set: `scripts/check-handback-size.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_size.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.336.1

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --check` answers ABSENT again for an exited session whose registry entry is gone, while still answering UNKNOWN for one spawned moments ago (a regression from #952, v0.336.0).** #952 made an empty registry match set UNKNOWN, but Claude Code DELETES the registry entry when a session exits, so every exited worker read UNKNOWN forever. Measured 2026-11-17 ~19:00: 21 of 21 flagged task rows with a recorded session read `degraded/UNKNOWN` and 0 read `resume`; ticks that opened 7-26 rows before the release opened 1-2 after it; 16 rows sat idle 20-66 min. The rule now: no registry entry AND the endpoint serves a record that is `live: false` with a numeric `age_seconds` (the endpoint's own freshness window, no new constant) -> ABSENT; no heartbeat record at all (404), a fresh record (LIVE), a not-live record with no measurable age, or an unreachable endpoint -> unchanged. Tests: stale-record -> ABSENT, no record -> UNKNOWN, fresh -> LIVE, unmeasurable age -> UNKNOWN, and a flagged-rows-level case that an exited session resolves to `resume` through the real probe. Residual risk: a resumed session id inherits its old stale record until its registry entry is rewritten, so that window still reads ABSENT. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.336.0

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --check` no longer answers ABSENT for a session the identity registry has not written yet.** `_absent_licensed` computed `non_dead` from the registry's match set, and with **no matching entry** `matched` was empty, the `if non_dead:` guard was skipped, and the function fell through to `return ABSENT` — so a freshly spawned session, which carries no registry entry for seconds to minutes, read ABSENT for as long as that write lagged. Measured 2026-11-17: four live sessions read ABSENT in one night, and a sweep or a drive leg acted on the bare verdict before corroboration in at least two of them. An empty match set now answers UNKNOWN with its own message: "the registry has not heard of it" is a statement about the registry's coverage, exactly like the endpoint's, and it cannot license the one verdict that authorises a resume. ⚠️ **This inverts a deliberate, tested contract, and the trade-off is stated rather than hidden:** an id matching no registry entry can no longer be called ABSENT, so a reaper relying on that verdict must corroborate with the registry or a process check. A registry entry the reader computes as `alive: False` is still death and still ABSENT, pinned by its own test — the fix narrows the verdict, it does not erase it. Seven tests pinned the old rule and now either plant the genuinely-dead shape (`pid=4194303`) or assert UNKNOWN; one new test is red on the pre-fix build (`AssertionError: 1 != 2`) and green after. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.335.0

- feat: add `scripts/deliver-message.py`, a session-id-addressed delivery path for a live session that the Claude Code registry omits; it resolves liveness and the message control without reading `~/.claude/sessions/*.json`, then delivers a real turn over the wrapper's MCP `send_message`, releases a park over `…/answer`, or — with `--nudge` — uses the fire-and-forget REST route, which answers 202 without carrying a turn

## v0.334.3

- fix: **the exit-observer keeper is one long-lived process, not a per-minute launchd tick.** The tick re-ran the whole sweep every 60 s, and a sweep costs ~24 s on an idle host — ~40 % of a core, and worse, it cannot hold a sub-minute detection bound: the moment one sweep runs long, detection is one sweep plus a tick. It now sweeps once at start, refreshes the pane list every `REFRESH_SECONDS` (600), and between refreshes only `stat`s each watched `pane-<N>.log`, whose mtime is the writer's last sign of life because the log is appended on every heartbeat. `POLL_SECONDS` is 5, so detection is the 45 s staleness threshold plus one poll, and the loop costs nothing between sweeps. ⚠️ **The refresh is driven by elapsed time alone** — an earlier revision also re-swept when the pane set came back empty, and an empty pane set is the HEALTHY fleet, so it swept continuously instead of every 600 s; a test now pins that (it failed `2 != 1` against the old predicate). A failed sweep retries after `RETRY_SECONDS` (60) rather than a full interval, so a transient failure does not cost ten minutes of blindness. The plist swaps `StartInterval` for `KeepAlive` + `RunAtLoad`, which is only safe because the job rewrites `keeper-heartbeat` every pass and `fleet-board.py` renders `keeper ok|stale|absent` from it — a long-lived watcher whose own death is watched. The card-before-re-arm ordering, the `unknown` count, and the `SUPERVISOR_SESSION_EXIT_DIR` override are unchanged. Change set: `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_observer_keeper.py`, `launchd/com.bborbe.exit-observer-keeper.plist`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.334.2

- fix: **the stalled-tabs keepalive leg no longer cards a live goal or topic manager.** Its population test was `stall_count >= 2` with no manager filter, and its own prose called the resulting superset safe *"because the act is a card, not a restart"* — which does not hold for a live manager: the loop already passes `--dir os.path.dirname(tf)` with the comment *"the PAGE's dir: goals tier, not just tasks"*, so it knowingly reached goal-page rows, and a card for a manager that `restart-worker.py` refuses `busy-target` is noise. The loop now resolves each row's page kind — its own `page_type: goal`/`topic` (only those two literals; any other value falls through), else the folder it lives in, accepting the caller vault's configured dirs and the repo's canonical `23 Goals`/`24 Goals` and `22 Topics`/`23 Topics`, because the snapshot spans every configured vault and most goal pages carry no `page_type` — and skips those rows before invoking the script; the prose restates the safety argument for the filtered population. Measured 2026-11-17 on a staged snapshot copy: the unfiltered build emits a goal-page manager (`5bedea87`) and a topic-page manager (`6f14c290`), and the filtered build emits neither while a real stalled tab worker (`b09b5f33`, a `25 Tasks/` page) survives both. Change set: `commands/steps/keepalive-stalled-tabs.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.334.1

- fix: **the fleet round prints its two count lines even when the `Waiting on your keystroke` list fails.** `commands/steps/drive.md` led that list with the board and store lines, and the whole list is skipped when the round's pane re-read fails — so the round's only measure of operator load vanished with it. Measured 2026-11-17: rounds `00-50` and `02-07` each printed `Waiting on your keystroke — re-read failed: …` and neither `.drive` carried a single count line. Both lines read the store over HTTP and need no pane, so they now print as their own step before the list, with the prose stating they must print on every round whatever the pane read does. Change set: `commands/steps/drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.334.0

- feat: **a dead exit writer is now re-armed and carded, and the coverage sweep reports whether the keeper that does it is itself alive.** `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py` runs the coverage sweep and, for each manager pane reading `unrecorded` with a silent writer, files one `ack` card and then re-arms `session-exit-record.py observe --pane … --pid … --session …`; the dedup key carries the pane's final heartbeat time, so one death gets one card and a second death gets its own. ⚠️ **The card is filed first, and the re-arm runs only when it lands** — the other order hides the death, because a re-armed pane reads `covered` again and a failed card would leave no trace. It is a separate job, not a step in `fleet-keeper.py`, because that script is pinned by `NoKillPathTest` to hold no kill, resume, re-arm or spawn path (operator decision 2026-11-06, card `7653b7b6`); the re-arm was tried there first and the pin caught it. Its own liveness is `~/.claude/state/session-exit/keeper-heartbeat`, which `exit-record-coverage.py` now reads and reports — `keeper <state>` on the text line, a `keeper` object under `--json` — and **`absent` is never `ok`**, because a keeper that never ran looks exactly like one that stopped. Five tests for the keeper (including the failed-card path, which must not re-arm, and a failed re-arm, which must not read as success) and three for the heartbeat. It ships as `launchd/com.bborbe.exit-observer-keeper.plist` — a one-minute `StartInterval`, so a tick is a fresh process and there is no loop to die — and `fleet-board.py` renders the `keeper` line beside its coverage warnings, so the instrument has a consumer. The keeper reads the store's own override, `SUPERVISOR_SESSION_EXIT_DIR`, not a second name for it. ⚠️ **The plist PATH carries `/Applications/WezTerm.app/Contents/MacOS`, and the sweep's `unknown` count is reported rather than folded into "nothing to do".** Without `wezterm` the sweep resolves no pane, returns an EMPTY `unrecorded` list, and the keeper would read that as a healthy fleet — the same false-clean this row exists to remove; measured on review of #945, where the job was a functional no-op under the system-only PATH. An unresolvable pane now exits 1 and says it was not counted as healthy. Change set: `scripts/exit-observer-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_observer_keeper.py`, `scripts/exit-record-coverage.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_record_coverage.py`, `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `launchd/com.bborbe.exit-observer-keeper.plist`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.333.1

- perf: **`flagged-rows.py` resolves panes in-process instead of spawning an interpreter per session id.** `pane_of` ran `python -c …` once per session — 17 rows at 2.4 s each, 40.7 s of a 51 s run — to importlib-load `who-needs-me.py` only to call `pane_for`, which itself re-reads the session registry and runs `wezterm cli list`. The resolution rules now live in `pane_for_maps(session_id, registry, pmap, records)`, which `pane_for` is a thin wrapper over, so every refusal (unknown id, ambiguous prefix, unreadable registry, unreadable pane list, nameless session with its recorded pane gone, no title match, two panes sharing a title) is the CLI's own and the two paths cannot drift. `flagged-rows.py` loads the module once per run and reads the registry, the pane list and the store once, whatever the row count. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`manager-liveness.py` no longer reads `STOPPED` for a stood-down subject whose `defer_date` has passed.** `classify()` returned `STOPPED` on the `<slug>.stopped` marker before consulting any date gate, so the documented "defer to the <date> and close this session" pair (`vault-cli goal set … defer_date` then `/supervisor:stop`) left the subject reading `STOPPED` for ever — before the instant because the stand-down masked the date, after it because nothing cleared the marker and `topic-keepalive.py` maps `STOPPED` to `leave`. `state()` now reads the subject page's `defer_date` (through `scripts/defer-date.py`'s parser and `close-worker.py`'s frontmatter reader) and passes it to `classify()` as a keyword argument defaulting to `None`, so the marker-only callers (`inbox.py`, `park-nudge.py`, the `--check` sweep) are unchanged. A **pending** date gate — a future `.sleeping` marker or a future `defer_date` — now outranks the stand-down and reads `SLEEPING`. Once the **`defer_date`** has passed, the stand-down stops masking and the normal age arithmetic decides (`STALE` / `OK` / `WORKING` / `UNARMED`, never `STOPPED`). ⚠️ **A `.sleeping` marker never releases a stand-down:** `--arm` is what clears it, so an expired one is leftover state rather than a gate that passed, and treating it as one would let a stale marker resurrect a session the operator deliberately stopped and make `--check` — which passes no page data — emit a false lapse. The marker-only callers are therefore genuinely unchanged. The marker is never deleted by a reading — `--arm --resume` stays the only verb that clears it — and an unreadable `defer_date` fails closed, leaving the stand-down standing. Measured 2026-11-16 on `goal--review-and-merge-stop-binding` (`.stopped` + future `defer_date`, no `.sleeping`). Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `docs/subject-resolution.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.333.0

- feat: **the fleet digest carries `expired-defer=<n>`.** A task at `status: in_progress` with a `defer_date` earlier than today is a dead park — the date has passed, nothing moves it, and `close-worker.py` refuses to close it `task-not-terminal` — so it reads as live work while no rotation can reach it, and nothing reported it. `scripts/expired-defer.py` counts those rows across the three task roots and prints one line; `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` § `<output_format>` pastes it into the round's digest. The script keeps one definition of the state with `vault-cli`'s `EXPIRED_DEFER_DATE` lint rule (`pkg/ops/lint.go`, landing in `bborbe/vault-cli#381`): date-only in local time, and the same two exemptions `HOLD_WITH_DEFER_DATE` carries (`created_by: recurring-task-creator`, non-empty `recurring:`), whose `defer_date` is a schedule date rather than a snooze. A failed read renders `UNKNOWN (read failed)` and exits non-zero, never `0`. Change set: `scripts/expired-defer.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/steps/sweep.md`, `docs/closer-classes.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.332.1

- fix: **a registry-alive, heartbeat-degraded flagged row resolves to a named `degraded` branch instead of the permanent `unknown` dead end.** The two sources answer different questions — the identity registry proves the *process* exists, the heartbeat proves the *session* is working — so folding their disagreement into `unknown` discarded the owner the same run had already resolved. `resolve_branch` now takes `registry_ok` (the registry read succeeded) and returns `degraded` for an `UNKNOWN` / `AMBIGUOUS` / `MALFORMED` row with a known owner; `unknown` survives only when the registry itself is unreadable, where no owner is known. `degraded` never spawns (no duplicate beside an existing manager) and never resumes (no second writer on a live process). Measured 2026-11-16 on `private-personal`: 8 goals + 1 topic read `unknown`; the cleanest, `No Session Lingers Without Working`, is pid 95579 alive 2d+ with its last heartbeat 6.5 h old. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `scripts/tests/schema/flagged-rows.schema.json` (the `branch` enum gains `degraded`), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.332.0

## v0.331.2

- fix: **`scripts/round-readings.py`'s `frozen` verdict is read from the newer of the task file and the session's transcript.** It was derived from the task-file mtime alone, so a live session that spent a whole tick interval reasoning, reading or writing to its transcript without touching its task file read `frozen` — a claim about the session's working state taken from the wrong file, and one `scripts/stuck-heal.py` rejects, leaving every such row to be re-verified by hand. Measured 2026-11-16, twice in one day: `A Park Wake That Finds No Session…` read `frozen 68m` with its transcript written 0.0 min before the read, and `Night and Weekend Window…` read `frozen 134.9m` beside its own `in_flight` limb `transcript 18s` — same document, same row. `stuck_verdict()` now takes the same `transcript_age` callable `in_flight()` reads (no second definition of transcript age) and compares the threshold against `min(task_file_age, transcript_age)`; a genuinely quiet session — task file *and* transcript both stale — still fires, an absent transcript (`inf`) leaves the task-file reading untouched, and the `unread` / no-transcript population is unchanged. Covered by `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py::TheFrozenTranscriptLeg`, whose `compute()` case reproduces the false `frozen` on the pre-change code.

## v0.331.1

- fix: **`exit-record-coverage.py` no longer reads a pane covered when its exit recorder has died.** The test was a `kind=spawn` line whose pid still answers `kill(pid, 0)`, which proves the writer *started*, not that it is still running: measured 2026-11-16, pane 59's observer died at `2026-11-15T19:14:17Z` while its wrapper stayed alive another 14 h 32 m, and the sweep reported that pane clean for the whole gap. A live spawn is now corroborated by a **fresh heartbeat** — the store carries no observer pid (every line's `pid=` is the *wrapped* pid), so heartbeat recency is the only liveness signal it has. `HEARTBEAT_STALE_SECONDS` (45 s, 1.5 x the observer's 30 s interval, which leaves the response inside a 60 s bound) marks a pane UNRECORDED once its newest heartbeat is older than that, or — with no heartbeat at all — once its newest live spawn line is; heartbeats older than the newest live spawn are ignored, so a reused pane id cannot age out its new occupant; the rendered line says which of the two failures it is. `live_coverage()` takes an injectable `now`, so the threshold is testable without a live store. Four tests: a stale heartbeat reads unrecorded (it reads **covered** against the old rule), a fresh one reads recorded, a live spawn with no heartbeat ages out, and a reused pane's older beats do not age out a new spawn. Change set: `scripts/exit-record-coverage.py`, `scripts/tests/test_exit_record_coverage.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.331.0

- fix: **a park wake that resolves no session now says which fact holds — the session is dead, or the wake could not see it.** Measured 2026-11-15 on `Nine Sessions Sit Parked Past the Fifteen-Minute Bound and Nothing Exits Them`: its `sample-15-window-opens` watch fired at 16:25, the wake resolved nothing, and the goal manager recorded session `ebd76359` as dead — while `session-liveness.py --check` read `LIVE — heartbeat age 27s` and that same session had taken its own reading seven minutes earlier. The cause was not a bad verdict but the absence of one: `scripts/park-event.py due` carried no liveness dimension at all, so an absent store (`all_watches` returns `[], []` when the directory is missing), a due watch with no members (`resume: none recorded`), and a due watch whose member was dead all printed the same line and exited 0 — and `no park watches due` was byte-identical for an unreadable store and an honest empty one. `due` now prints `store=readable` on a real negative, `store=absent` when the store directory does not exist, exits **4** with `UNREADABLE store=<path> reason=…` when the store is present but unlistable, tags every member `live=yes|no|unknown` from `session-liveness.py --check` (`live=none` for a watch carrying no member), and carries the same verdicts in `--json`. `live=no` is licensed only by a probe that positively reports the id absent; exit 2 (UNKNOWN) and 3 (AMBIGUOUS) are `live=unknown`, never `no` — folding a failed read into `no` is the defect the field exists to remove. The vocabulary is `manager-wake.py`'s `live=` on purpose: one word for the same question everywhere. `commands/steps/keepalive.md` item 2 renders `parked-due: unknown — store unreadable` on exit 4 rather than `0`, and takes no act on `live=unknown`; `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` § 2 — the file whose agent actually runs `due` — carries the same rule, since the actor otherwise reduces the output to a line count and an unreadable store still renders as `parked-due=0`. `--json` emits `{store, reason, due}` rather than a bare list, so an unreadable store cannot render as `[]`. Pinned by 10 tests in `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py` (absent store vs empty store, absent/live/unknown member, a memberless watch, the JSON store token on every branch), each of which fails on the previous code. Change set: `scripts/park-event.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.330.2

- fix: **the covered-subject invariant stops being a second asker for a card the cron poster already owns, and reports a stale poster instead.** The open-card card is raised model-free by the attention-controller `sweep-gate-notify-job` (producer `sweep-gate`, kind `cron`); two posters on one dedup key do not collapse, because the store's suppression is a point read of `(producer_id, dedup_key)`, so a manager-posted twin sits beside the cron card as a second open row for one ask — measured 2026-11-16, 21 open `open-card-invariant:*` items, all `sweep-gate`/`cron`, none `manager`. The fleet round's own card could never fire anyway: `invariant_card()` returns `None` before producing a payload while any card is attributed to the subject, so its card and its `--row` claim never existed. The step now posts nothing and claims nothing; `--invariant` stays as a diagnostic whose payload means the cron poster missed a subject (reported once per round), and a new read of `last-pass.json` (`SWEEP_GATE_LOOP_STATE_DIR`, bar `max(3 × duration_s, 1800 s)`, derived from the record's own measured pass) emits one stale-poster line when the asker has stopped. Change set: `commands/steps/covered-subject-invariant.md`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.330.1

- fix: **the goal tier's liveness bound follows its observed period, not its declared interval.** `manager-liveness.py` bounded every tier by `DECLARED_K x declared` (4500 s for a manager declaring 900 s), but the goal loop arms no `ScheduleWakeup` — the detached sweep gate is its sole wake source — so a goal manager's real cadence is its observed period, and a live one lapsed roughly six times early (measured 2026-11-16: `limit=4680s` against `observed=30105s` on a manager that had missed no round). The goal tier is now bounded by `GOAL_BOUND_K (2) x observed` with the limit at one observed period; the model-cadence tiers keep `DECLARED_K x declared`. A gate-watched goal manager still reads `IDLE`, and a moving transcript past the cap still reads `LAPSED`.

## v0.330.0

- feat: **the fleet round prints the attention store's open count and per-producer split beside the board line, and the board line stops reading like the store's.** `scripts/attention-store.py` reads `GET /api/1.0/attention?state=open&limit=500` and prints `Store: 9 open — attention-unforwarded-closers 8, 5b29b81a 1 · cron 8/9 (89%)` — total, split sorted desc, and the cron share computed from the store's own `producer_kind`, so a new cron producer is counted without editing the script; a full page prints `(truncated at <limit>)` rather than undercounting, and an unreachable store prints `store unavailable` with exit 3, the same branch shape as the board's. `commands/steps/drive.md` step 5 now leads the round's operator to-do list with BOTH lines and names why neither may stand in for the other: `attention-board.py` counts the cards the page RENDERED (measured 2026-10-01: 2 rendered against 4 store items), so `Board page: 0 undimmed cards` beside a store of 52 is the shape the store line makes visible. `attention-board.py`'s line is renamed `Board: <n> open` → `Board page: <n> undimmed cards` — its board-scoped count can no longer be quoted as the store's open count — and every command file quoting it (`commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`) is updated with it. Change set: `scripts/attention-store.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_store.py`, `scripts/attention-board.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_board.py`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.329.0

- fix: open-queue lets an operator-flagged task outrank its subject's stand-down; add `manager-liveness.py --clear-standdown`, which clears a marker without arming

## v0.328.0

- perf: **`flagged-rows.py` no longer re-parses every page on every run.** An mtime page cache (`flagged-rows-cache.json` in the Claude state dir, overridable with `--cache`, keyed on `(mtime_ns, size)`) serves the skip verdict of a page whose bytes have not moved, for the two verdicts that depend on nothing else (`not flagged`, `status is …`). Eligible, deferred and unreadable pages are re-read every run, so a defer that comes due and a liveness change surface without the file changing. Measured on the live corpus with `--no-liveness`: cold run 47,482 pages parsed / 11 s, warm run **116 parsed / 47,366 cache hits / 3 s**, against a 14 s baseline; the row set and `skipped` counts are identical across all three. A deleted or renamed page leaves no row and no cache key (`pruned` counts them). `--no-cache` restores the old behaviour; `--json` gains a `cache` counter object.

## v0.327.0

- feat: **`scripts/manager-quiet.py` never offers `sleep-until` to a subject whose page is wake-counted.** For some subjects the check's own remedy is the defect: a goal whose success criterion is a count of wakable ticks loses one for every hour slept, because `pane-wake-guard.py` returns `skip:sleeping` for a `SLEEPING` subject and nothing types into a parked manager — the sleep suppresses the very signal the criterion counts, and the only defence was a language model remembering to decline the offer every interval. A new frontmatter marker on the **subject page**, `quiet_check_exempt`, whose value is the reason, turns the date-gated branch into `armed`; it is reached through the tracked rows' `goals:` (`read_task` returns `goals` but no criterion — `status` / `phase` / `session(s)` / `progress_hash` / `met`, with `defer_date` and `blocked_by` derived later by `classify`), and an absent or unreadable page is not an exemption. A new `Exempt: quiet_check_exempt — <reason>` line names it, echoed from the marker's own value so the criterion is named by construction; it prints LAST, after any action line, so `commands/steps/round-quiet.md`'s "second line is the action line" stays true. It blocks `sleep-until` only — a subject that reaches `stood-down` has no row a wake could advance. Change set: `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `commands/steps/round-quiet.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.326.2

- fix: **the flagged-row predicate no longer hangs the daemon's tick on a pane lookup.** Each LIVE row spawned a fresh interpreter to `importlib`-load `who-needs-me.py` (2,300 lines) for `pane_for`, with an unbounded `subprocess.run`; measured 2026-11-16, one such helper held a daemon-owned `flagged-rows.py` in `poll()` for 11m49s until the 25-minute read bound SIGKILLed it and the tick opened nothing. Two changes: `--no-pane` skips the lookup entirely (the daemon's tick never reads a pane — it skips every non-ABSENT row before it looks at `jump`, so a LIVE row resolving to `skip` instead is identical to it), cutting a full run from 88 s to 19 s; and every helper subprocess is now bounded by `PROBE_TIMEOUT` (15 s), so a slow probe costs one row's field (`UNKNOWN` liveness, no pane) rather than the tick. The default is unchanged — `/supervisor:open` still resolves panes to jump.

## v0.326.1

- fix: `manager-wake.py` retries a transient pane-lookup miss up to `PANE_ATTEMPTS` (4) times instead of once. The gate logged 20 `NO-PANE` wake results over 24 h, every one `reason=wezterm-unreadable`, while wakes minutes either side of each failure succeeded — so `wezterm cli list` fails per call, flakily, and one 2 s retry did not cover it. Each affected session resolved to a real pane on a later read (`0dbb4336`→12, `6f14c290`→47, `c49298a8`→72). Four attempts span ~6 s inside a tick that already takes seconds.

## v0.326.0

- feat: **`Next focus` reserves one of its five slots for the objective furthest below its stated share, and caps non-urgent proposals per objective.** Tier 1 (due or operator-flagged) was concatenated ahead of every plan tier, so with 63 `priority: 1` tasks open it consumed all five slots and no plan tier was ever reached — the ranking could not pull work however good it was. Two bounds, both config constants beside `CAP`: `RESERVED_SLOTS = 1` fills one slot from the plan tier of the objective with the largest positive `stated_share - actual_share` deficit (objectives with no `stated_share` are never eligible — there is no share to fall below), and a due-today row can never take it; `CAP_PER_OBJECTIVE = 2` stops an objective already carrying its cap of non-urgent proposals from asking again, with the withheld rows **named** in the output so a withheld row and a never-found row do not look the same. The block now prints the computed deficit for every ranked objective, which is what tells a computed ranking from a hardcoded one. `stated_share` is read off each `22 Objectives/` page and the actual share from the vault's own `task-lineage.py --json` over the current week — a failed read renders `deficits: unread|unavailable|empty`, never "no deficits". Change set: `scripts/next-focus.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus.py` (7 new cases, including a swapped-share pair that fails a hardcoded pick), `commands/steps/readings.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.325.3

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` refuses an explicit `mode: headless` on a manager page at the two sites that actually resume one.** v0.321.0 put the refusal in `docs/fleet-surface.md`'s manager-resume block and `scripts/check-open-resume-mode.py` pinned it there — but the block is a **document**, and the resume happens in `commands/open.md` § Step 2B item 3 and § Step 2C item 3, neither of which read `mode:` at all. A lapsed topic manager carrying an explicit `mode: headless` was therefore resumed anyway and the refusal branch never executed. Measured 2026-11-16: the keep-alive leg returned `resume` for such a page, `open.md` resumed the id, and every check still passed — a rule documented, linted in the document, and applied nowhere. Both sites now refuse first, printing `role=manager, mode=headless — refused` and taking neither path, and `check_open` gains a structural assertion over each step (with a missing-step branch, so a rename cannot pass unread) plus three negative controls. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/check-open-resume-mode.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_open_resume_mode.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.325.2

- fix: **`scripts/context-usage.py` withholds a compaction candidate whose session is no longer registered.** A pane id is a lease — WezTerm renumbers and reuses them — so a record whose pane still exists can be worn by a different, live session, and pane existence alone offered that stranger's session for a **no-ask** compaction. Measured 2026-11-16: `--compactable --threshold-tokens 500000` returned 14 candidates whose recorded `session_id` was absent from `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` (14 of 14 dead) while every recorded pane existed and was occupied by another live session — a send would have compacted 11 unrelated live sessions, the Fleet Manager's among them. Liveness is read from that registry through `who-needs-me.py`'s `live_session_ids()` (the authority `pane_for()` already uses), `is_candidate()` gains an `alive` term, a dead row's pane is no longer printed, and the over-bound table names it `session gone — not compactable`. An unreadable registry is UNKNOWN, never "nothing is live": every record is kept, the rule `docs/pane-reads.md` states. Replay against the live state dir with the measured condition reproduced (every recorded pane declared live): 403 candidates / 402 dead-session before, 0 / 0 after. Change set: `scripts/context-usage.py`, `scripts/tests/test_context_usage.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.325.1

- docs: **`scripts/cause-resolve.py` records which half of its own verdict is observable.** The negative half — a cause it cannot find in the session's own sources is reported `unverified` — is proven on real fleet rounds. The positive half — `asserted` — is not observable there at all, and the docstring now says why: the reaper writes `job=idle-reaper action=closed sid=<sid>` to the **daemon log** when it closes a session, so a session's own `events.jsonl` can carry that line only if it read the daemon log **after** it was reaped, which cannot happen. Measured 2026-11-15 over every `*.jsonl` under the Claude projects and state trees (56,739 files): 173 `action=closed` lines, every one naming a **foreign** session, none naming its own file — the borrowing the resolver refuses is the only shape that exists. The paragraph names `scripts/tests/test_cause_resolve.py` as the positive half's **only** proof, because a change that breaks the `asserted` path turns that suite's positive control red while no production observation would notice — a round of all-`unverified` causes satisfies the negative half and reads as a working round.

## v0.325.0

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --coverage` names WHY a registry-live session has no live row, and `--check` reports the same cause on the verdict path.** `missing` folded three unlike facts into one number, so a fleet reading `missing=12` could not tell a registration outage from twelve idle sessions whose stamp had lapsed — measured 2026-11-15: all twelve held a row reading `live: false` with `source: mcp-timer` and an `age_seconds` of ~14 900 s, i.e. the store could see every one of them and was answering about them. The closed set is `not-registered` (no row at all, under this id or a case/hyphen variant — the only genuine coverage gap), `expired` (a row the store holds and reports not live, carrying its `age_seconds`) and `key-mismatch` (the same id under a different key shape, carrying `found_as`). `--coverage` adds a per-cause count to its existing line, and `--coverage --json` emits `{verdict, line, misses}` so a consumer needs no line parsing. ⚠️ **The verdicts are unchanged on purpose.** A residual miss still exits 1, and `--check` still returns UNKNOWN with `(cause: …)` appended to the message — a new exit code would change what every caller maps to "not live, resume is authorised", the one verdict this file exists to protect. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`manager-liveness.py --state` reports `LAPSED` for a manager whose arm file is past the hard cap while its `work=` is still fresh.** `WORKING` was returned for any moving transcript, so a manager that had stopped arming but was still writing read as healthy — measured 2026-11-15, seven goal-manager loops sat dead for up to 15 hours while every keep-alive round counted a fresh `work=` as fine. Past `DECLARED_K x declared` the next wake is not coming, so the reading is now `LAPSED`: named, counted, and deliberately absent from every restart set (`topic-keepalive.py` acts on STALE / INVALID only), so the mid-turn restart `WORKING` exists to prevent is not reintroduced. `LAPSED` joins `owner.py`'s loop states and its owner set — a demonstrably live session must not be given a second writer — and `crashed-managers.py` / `fleet-keeper.py`'s state vocabularies. ⚠️ **A new state is also a new entry in every ALLOW-LIST, and the one that mattered was the arming one.** `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` arms on an explicit allow-list, so leaving `LAPSED` off it would have made the state that most needs an arm the one that never got one — the loop would have stayed past the cap for ever. It is added there, and to the "never restart" rows in `commands/steps/fleet-triage-state.md` / `commands/topic-drive.md` and the state enumerations in `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md` / `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, so no reader treats the word as unknown. Tests: the WORKING case is split into a LAPSED case past the cap plus a WORKING negative control inside it, and `test_round_liveness_step.py` gains `test_lapsed_arms` beside `test_working_arms`. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/owner.py`, `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `commands/steps/round-liveness.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-state.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_liveness_step.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.324.0

- feat: **`scripts/census-manager-mode.py` names every topic or goal manager running headless.** A headless manager has no tab, no pane and no jump link, while the Vault UI still renders its card as Live — so the board says it is running and offers nothing to click, and the condition is only found when an operator notices the missing link (measured 2026-11-14 on `23 Topics/Development Pipeline Throughput.md`, session `6f14c290`). The reading joins three sources, and the join is the point: `session-liveness.py --list --json` for the live set, the identity registry for `entrypoint` (the liveness record carries **no** `entrypoint`, so a reading built on it alone would match nothing and print a clean fleet forever), and the vault for the page carrying the session. It prints `<session-id> <subject> DEFECT manager-running-headless` and exits `0` clean / `1` at least one defect / `2` a probe or the vault could not be read. ⚠️ **Only a topic or goal page counts** — a task page's session is a worker, and a worker is headless by design (`docs/role-mode-matrix.md` Rule 1), so flagging those would make every healthy fleet red. Wired as § The manager-mode reading in `commands/steps/readings.md`, beside the keeper census it mirrors. Change set: `scripts/census-manager-mode.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_census_manager_mode.py` (new), `commands/steps/readings.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.323.1

- test: **the `held-owner-live` hold's two consumer obligations are pinned, not asserted.** `scripts/check-open-owner-live.py` (wired into `precommit` as `check-open-owner-live`) pins what a prose command file cannot otherwise hold: the `⛔ HOLD — owner <kind> "<name>" has a live manager` render and that it names the owning subject, so the hold stays distinguishable from the pre-existing `blocked=True` hold; the `opened-recently.py --subject` spawn-ledger leg that catches a manager opened earlier in the same run; the footer split `held K (blocked · owner live)`; the `LIVE HERE` / `LIVE ELSEWHERE` distinctness clause; and producer/consumer agreement on `held-owner-live` between `scripts/flagged-rows.py` and the row schema. Each guard is proven to fire on its own one-line mutation of the real `commands/open.md` in `scripts/tests/test_check_open_owner_live.py` — a guard that cannot fail turns that file red, the defect a first revision of the sibling `check-open-hand-run.py` had. Change set: `scripts/check-open-owner-live.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_check_open_owner_live.py` (new), `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.323.0

- feat: **a flagged task whose owning subject already has a live manager is held, not opened.** `scripts/flagged-rows.py`'s `resolve_branch()` gains `held-owner-live`: a task that would otherwise `create` a worker, whose resolved owner is a `goal` or `topic` (never the `fleet` sentinel) and whose `owner_live` probe answers `true`, is held instead of spawned — two sessions on one subject is the collision. `owner_live: null` (an unanswerable probe) still creates, for the same reason `unknown` outranks `create`; the fleet sentinel never holds, or the whole batch would stop. `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5 now reads the resolver's `owner_live` (it carried 0 references while the resolver carried 10) and renders `⛔ HOLD — owner <kind> "<name>" has a live manager`, distinct from the row-own `LIVE HERE` / `LIVE ELSEWHERE` verdicts; a manager opened earlier in the same run counts via the spawn ledger. Tests: `HeldOwnerLive` in `test_flagged_rows.py` and the branch enum in the row schema.

## v0.322.1

- fix: **`manager-quiet.py` names a `phase: todo` row's future `defer_date` on the stand-down line.** `classify()` returned `{"kind": "quiet", "defer": None}` on the unapproved-phase branch **before** it read `defer_date`, so a subject whose only quiet row was deferred printed `Stood down: zero runnable tasks; reopen on no named trigger` — the one case `round-quiet.md` says a reader cannot act on (measured 2026-11-07 on `Automatic Weekly AI Knowledge Sharing Task in Seibert Personal`, row `Verify the Second Weekly AI Knowledge Sharing Task Materializes`, `defer_date: "2026-11-14"`). The defer read now precedes the phase branch, and `classify()` carries an `unapproved` flag **separately** from `defer`: `reopen_trigger()` names the date, while `verdict_for()` still stands the subject down rather than flipping it to `sleep-until`, because an approval is not something a clock delivers. A due or absent date still prints `no named trigger`, and an unapproved row never becomes runnable. Tests: `TestDeferredTodoRowNamesItsDate` (future date named on stdout, past date still unnamed, no-defer still unnamed, the row never runnable) and `TestDeferParsersAreNotTheFault` (the quoted-scalar form parses and displays, so the parser was never at fault). Change set: `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.322.0

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` no longer refuses the operator's own open of an unflagged topic that has no recorded manager.** Operator ruling 2026-11-15: an unflagged task, goal or topic never opens *automatically*, but a hand-run `/supervisor:open` opens what the operator names. Every automatic fresh open is already flag-gated upstream of the command — `topic-keepalive.py` prints `open-unflagged` instead of `open`, and `topic-spawn.py --goal` refuses `goal-unflagged` — so the topic branch's in-command check (`commands/open.md` § Step 2C item 4, `topic-spawn.py --topic-open` → `topic-unflagged`) only ever fired on the hand-run, refusing the one open the rule allows. `topic-spawn.py` gains `--hand-run`, which qualifies `--topic-open` only: it skips the flag check and keeps the fleet manager ceiling, because the operator's intent does not create a slot; `--hand-run` without `--topic-open` is a usage error, so it can never loosen the `--goal` path the rounds use. Step 2C passes it only on item 4's `None` arm (no recorded id), which no round reaches for an unflagged topic; its `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS` arm — a keep-alive resume whose liveness could not be read — drops it and still STOPs on `topic-unflagged`, so an undecidable resume never opens an unflagged topic fresh. The goal branch (§ Step 2B) is unchanged. `scripts/check-open-hand-run.py` pins it — no goal-flag STOP, `--hand-run` only on the `None` arm, the flag check kept on the `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS` arm, and both upstream gates still present — and is wired into `precommit`; run against master's `commands/open.md` it fails, and so does this PR's earlier revision that passed `--hand-run` on both arms. Tests: four `--hand-run` cases in `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py` (unflagged topic opens, agent-flagged topic opens, the ceiling still refuses, `--goal --hand-run` exits 2). Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/check-open-hand-run.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_check_open_hand_run.py` (new — each guard fires on its own one-line mutation of the real command), `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py`, `docs/session-tiers.md` (Rule 4 names the operator's own open), `Makefile`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json` (`commands/open.md` raised 85624 → 85699, +75 B for the two-arm step), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.321.2

- fix: name an expired `defer_date` as the close refusal reason — a task at `in_progress` with a lapsed defer now reports the expiry and the three exits instead of a message identical to one that was never deferred

## v0.321.1

- fix: **`restart-worker.py --absent` reports a missing `vault-cli` as `environment-fault` / exit 3 instead of blaming the vault registration.** `resume_absent` resolved the cwd through `vault_configs()` before `resolve_launcher` was ever reached, and `vault_configs()` returns `None` when `find_vault_cli()` is `None` — so on a host with no `vault-cli` the run printed "the registration names vault 'X', which resolves to no existing directory" and exited **1**, sending the reader to inspect vault configuration when the real fault was the toolchain, and leaving exit 3 unreachable on the path whose whole job is bringing back a manager whose process is gone. The probe now runs at the top of `resume_absent`, ahead of the `not-absent` / `held-session` / `unknown-session-id` refusals, which is what the exit-code table already promised ("checked before every refusal"); those refusal tokens are unchanged, and `environment-fault` now outranks them on a degraded host. Guarded on `not os.environ.get("SUPERVISOR_VAULT_CONFIG")` exactly as `resolve_launcher` is — that override makes `vault_configs()` bypass `find_vault_cli()` entirely, so an unconditional probe would turn every drill and test run on a `vault-cli`-less host into exit 3. `docs/restart-worker.md` § `--absent` loses the sentence claiming exit 3 is unreachable there, and `resume_absent`'s docstring no longer says `held-session` outranks everything. Tests: four environment-fault cases plus the override-suppression case in `test_restart_worker.py::AbsentMode`.

## v0.321.0

- fix: **the keep-alive leg never resumes a topic or goal manager headless.** `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` chose its path by the row's `mode:` alone, so a topic page carrying no `mode:` took **path A** — the headless resume — and the manager came back with no tab, no pane and no jump link: invisible to the operator while the Vault UI still rendered its card as Live. Measured 2026-11-14 on `23 Topics/Development Pipeline Throughput.md`, session `6f14c290` — four `kind=spawn` records through 2026-11-13, then a headless process with none. The open path resolves `role` → `mode` at Step 0.6 (`docs/fleet-surface.md` item 6, matrix Rule 1); the leg had no equivalent step, so `role:` was never consulted. The branch now reads `role:` first — a `manager` row takes **path B** (`/supervisor:open`), or, when the page carries an explicit `mode: headless`, refuses with `role=manager, mode=headless — refused` (operator ruling 2026-11-15). ⚠️ The refusal is deliberate rather than a fallback: a manager page carrying `headless` is a misconfiguration, so the manager stays down **visibly** until the page is fixed instead of returning reachable while the wrong field stays on disk to mislead the next reader. `scripts/check-open-resume-mode.py` gained the leg as a second target — role-first selector, the three classifications, the path-A ban, the refusal literal — with a pre-change negative control in `scripts/tests/test_check_open_resume_mode.py`. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `scripts/check-open-resume-mode.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_open_resume_mode.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.320.1

- fix: **the open leg no longer holds a row whose `blocked_by` names only completed tasks.** `scripts/open-queue.py::guards()` held on `has_blocked_by(block)`, a presence read of the raw array, so a row whose every named blocker had since completed was reported to the operator as blocked on an outstanding dependency and stayed closed — the report actively misleads, so the operator neither opens the row nor investigates. Measured 2026-11-15: `Rebuild Nuke Cluster - 2026W46-sun` was held by the fleet round for an hour while both of its blockers read `completed` and the CLI already resolved the row as unblocked. `guards()` now holds only when the COMPUTED verdict says so, read from `manager-predispatch.blocked_by_verdict()` — the resolver `manager-quiet.py` already holds on, which evaluates every entry and counts an unreadable or statusless blocker as unmet. `tasks_dir` is threaded through `collect()` with one `task_index()` per run, so per-blocker resolution does not become a directory scan per row; a caller that omits `tasks_dir` (a test of `is_stopped`) keeps the declaration read, which fails toward holding. New `scripts/check-open-hold-agrees-with-cli.py` pins the agreement between the open leg's held set and `vault-cli task list`'s `blocked` field, with a `--fixture` negative control that reconstructs the defect's shape and exits 1 to prove the detector fires before a live run is trusted. ⚠️ The check reads `task list`, never `task get <name> blocked`: `task get` answers an unsupported key with an EMPTY VALUE rather than an error, so that probe reads empty for every row on every vault and can never fail.

## v0.320.0

- feat: **the keep-alive leg now reports a stalled tab worker's refusal and hands its restart to the operator through one card, instead of leaving the row for a person to notice.** The sweep already classified a tab worker `stalled` (`agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md:115`), but the act — `scripts/restart-worker.py` — refuses `busy-target`, and a stalled session is `busy`/`shell` **by definition**, so the default invocation can never restart one. The only lift is `--allow-busy-lapse`, which the operator ruled on 2026-11-12 must stay their own judgement.
