The new `scripts/stalled-tab-restart.py` does both halves: it invokes `restart-worker.py --dry-run` and prints the token that invocation actually returned, and on `busy-target` it posts ONE card (dedup `stuck-tab:<slug>:<restarts>`, so each stall episode is asked once and a repeat stall after a restart is asked again) whose `ANSWERED:` runs the real restart exactly once, consumed-first, with `--allow-busy-lapse --reason lapse --fresh-prompt-task`. ⚠️ **`--dry-run` on the unattended path is a control, not caution:** without it the script proceeds on a row that flipped to `idle` between the sweep and the call, which is the unattended restart the ruling forbids, reachable through a timing window. ⚠️ **There is no `stuck-tab:` card to extend** — `CLAUDE.md:135` says a stall is a duration, not an event, and issues no attention card, so this creates it. A per-task cap (2 restarts, one escalation card) keeps its own store under `~/.claude/state/stalled-tab/` and resets when the task page's mtime advances — deliberately NOT `worker-keepalive.py`'s rule, which resets on `made_progress()` (an open box ticked, a new `# Progress` entry, a new commit). ⚠️ The leg's reading is **not** a `fleet-legs.py` key: that is a separate contract change (its `KEYS`, the sweep's seed, `check-handback-keys.py` and six `test_fleet_legs.py` fixtures all encode the four-key shape), so the count is printed and deliberately not written into the digest under another leg's key. Change set: `scripts/stalled-tab-restart.py` (new), `commands/steps/keepalive-stalled-tabs.md` (new), `scripts/tests/test_stalled_tab_restart.py` (new), `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.319.3

- docs: `docs/session-tiers.md` § Owner names `WORKING` and `IDLE` as owner loop states, matching `owner.py` since v0.319.0. `scripts/tests/test_owner.py` pins `WORKING` in the live-armed and exhaustive ownership tests, and pins `WORKING` / `IDLE` in the every-documented-state mapping, so a word dropped from `LOOP_STATES` fails a test instead of reading as a probe failure.

## v0.319.2

- fix: **`flagged-rows.py` drops a `blocked_by` entry whose task is completed.** `blocked_by_of()` returned every listed name without checking it, so a flagged task stayed blocked forever once its prerequisites finished — `flagged-tick` skips any non-empty list (2026-11-15: Rebuild Nuke Cluster and Turn off fire, all four blockers completed hours earlier). A missing or unreadable blocker still counts as blocking, matching vault-cli's rule.

## v0.319.1

- fix: `/supervisor:fleet-triage` § 3.6 gives `WORKING` and `IDLE` their own row (not a finding, never a re-arm drive or restart). Before, neither word had a row, so a mid-turn manager could be read as a lapse and driven to re-arm. The keep-alive actor and `topic-keepalive.py` now name `WORKING` as an owner state.

## v0.319.0

- fix: `manager-liveness.py --state` no longer reads `OK` for a manager with no round inside 5 × its declared cadence. The observed period still sets the limit, but it is capped at `DECLARED_K` (5) × declared, so a manager that stopped rounding can no longer stretch its own limit: The Attention Board goal (declared 900 s) had a 127 755 s limit and read `OK` after 18 h without a round. K was measured on 2026-11-15 as the largest p90-gap ÷ declared ratio among subjects ticking at their cadence (Fleet Manager 5.04). The cap only ever lowers a limit. A capped detail reads `period=<K×declared>s(bounded 5x declared) observed=<p90>s`.
- feat: a manager past its limit whose transcript moved reads `WORKING`, not `OK`, and a goal manager past its limit while the sweep gate is down reads `IDLE`, not `OK`. No restart path acts on either. `owner.py` `LOOP_STATES` (which also lacked `IDLE`, so an `IDLE` owner used to read as an unreadable probe), `fleet-keeper.py`, `crashed-managers.py`, `inbox.py`, `park-nudge.py`, `propose-managers.py` and `answer-path-check.py` read `WORKING` and `IDLE` as live owners, and the round's arm allow-list arms on `WORKING`.

## v0.318.0

- feat: `scripts/wake-misses.py` reads the sweep gate's per-subject ledgers and lists every subject whose last actionable wake did not reach a manager (`NO-MANAGER`, `NO-PANE`, `HEADLESS`, or a failed wake run), with the resolved session and its `live=` value. A ledger written before the gate recorded wake outcomes counts as `unmeasured`, never as clean, and an unreadable state directory exits 2, never "0 misses".
- feat: `/supervisor:fleet-triage` § 3.6 counts each `MISS` as `NO-LOOP`, so a subject whose manager is dead or headless is no longer missed by a check that starts from live manager sessions. The verify block prints the measured and unmeasured wake counts. The fleet keep-alive leg reports the same reading in its hand-back (`wake-misses=` and one `wake-miss:` line per subject), report-only.

## v0.317.0

- fix: `manager-wake.py` resolves a subject's manager from the union of `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<sid>.json` and the `sweep-gate/<branch>--<slug>.session` marker `--arm` writes, and keeps only sessions live in `~/.claude/sessions/` (registry entry with a running pid). A dead registration never outranks a live one; when every registration is dead the result is `NO-MANAGER`, not `NO-PANE`. Previously the newest `worker-manager` record won regardless of liveness, so a subject whose live manager never wrote one (Development Pipeline Throughput) had every wake aimed at a session dead for 135 h.
- fix: `manager-wake.py`'s pane lookup joins from the target session (registry → pid → tty → pane) instead of `jump.py`'s one-session-per-tty map, retries once on a transient read failure, and names the miss: `NO-PANE reason=wezterm-unreadable|no-registry-entry|tty-unreadable|no-tty|tty-not-a-pane:<tty>`. A live manager running headless (`sdk-cli` entrypoint and no controlling tty) reads `HEADLESS`, since no pane-wake can reach it.
- feat: every `manager-wake.py` result line ends `session=<sid8> live=<yes|no|unknown>` (`session=-` when nothing is registered), so the gate's ledger can record which session a wake resolved and whether it was alive.

## v0.316.0

- feat: **the rebase agent's repo coverage is configuration, not code.** `scripts/rebase-agent.py` carried the set as the `DEFAULT_REPOS` constant, so adding a repo meant editing the script and shipping a release. It now reads `repos.txt` — one `owner/name` per line, `#` comments and blank lines skipped — via a new `--repos-file` flag (default `/config/repos.txt`), deriving each clone path with `expanduser` and keeping the park thresholds as code defaults. The cluster CronJob's clone loop reads the same file, so a repo added there is cloned before the sweep runs; a repo named only to the script has no clone, `fetch_master` fails, and its row renders `UNKNOWN — queue could not be read`. A missing, empty or malformed set is exit `2` and never the clean-queue line — the same false negative the `--repo` guard already refuses, since an empty list used to read as "nothing is behind" when the truth is "nothing was examined". `docs/rebase-agent.md` § *The repo set* now describes the file rather than a constant, and `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py` gains `LoadReposTest` pinning the ways the file can be wrong. The Mac's launchd path keeps working: `launchd/com.bborbe.rebase-agent.plist` passes `--repos-file` as an absolute path, because the default is container-only and launchd expands nothing.

## v0.315.1
- fix: a goal whose only open box is the template DoD *"All linked tasks completed or moved out with a recorded reason"* now closes. Before this fix, that box stayed `[ ]` while any task was linked, so the goal never read `CLOSE` and closure Step 0b never ran. Step 0b now also runs when this box is the only open one, then ticks it as a `linked-task scan` (a new Step 0 evidence type) citing its own moved-out line. In `private-personal` on 2026-11-15, 6 in-progress goals carried the box.

## v0.315.0

- feat: an operator-set flag counts as approval at every reader — `flag_provenance.is_approved()` (`approved_at` or an `operator`/`legacy` flag) drives `approved-not-started.py`, the open leg's `waiting approval` guard, `/supervisor:open` Gate 1, the goal tier's todo hold (`goal-drive`, `goal-spawn-reader`, `goal-sweep-reader`), `next-focus.py`'s `needs_approval`, `inbox.py`'s inbox set and `undriven-plan.py`'s manager approve; nothing writes `approved_*` from a flag any more, so the `--flagged` batch drops its approve pass and the obsolete `check-open-approve-ordering.py` checker is retired
- docs: workers and managers are told flagged rows open and reopen automatically, so a session with nothing doable before a later date sets `defer_date` and closes to free its slot

## v0.314.0

- feat: goal and topic loops tick Success Criteria / Definition of Done / Completion Gate boxes on an inline citation they resolved this tick (merged PR, release tag, completed task on the goal tier, completed goal on the topic tier). `commands/steps/goal-closure.md` § Step 0 is the single home, replacing the "close, never certify" rule that left goal and topic boxes with no owner. Each tier ticks only pages it owns: a goal loop ticks its goal and only those tasks whose liveness reads `ABSENT`; a topic loop ticks its topic page. Once all tracked work is terminal, unevidenced boxes raise one `ack` notice per page, recorded in `# Progress`, instead of a silent `OPEN` every tick.
- feat: closure Step 0b (goal branch, subject only) unlinks the goal's non-terminal linked tasks before it closes, after the tracked set is examined, and records them in `# Progress`. It uses each task's raw `goals:` entry, because `vault-cli goal complete` compares raw entries and never matches `'[[<goal>]]'`. A started task whose liveness reads `LIVE`/`UNKNOWN`, a failed unlink or an unsafe task name holds the close.
- fix: the topic tier no longer runs `vault-cli goal complete` on member goals. A member goal goes to its goal manager, which moves its open tasks out (Step 0b) before closing it, so a goal is no longer closed with its open tasks still linked.

## v0.313.5

- fix: **a `CLOSERS` row names its store item id.** The row carried title, pane, link and class, so an answered item could not be traced from the digest: the store's key is the 32-char item id that `attention-ask.py poll` and `attention-answer.py` both take, and the pane is not it. `row()` gains a `store_ref` flag used only by the `last-closers` section; the id rides the **provenance** line (`… · item=<id>`), never `what` — `what` is capped at 60 chars and carries the closer's own words, which the routine-continue table reads verbatim. The first line's columns and the row's three-line height are unchanged, so the `[<pane>]` join and the digest's 40-line payload cap are unaffected, and a synthetic pane-free row (which owns no store item) renders exactly as before. Pinned by `test_who_needs_me.py::LastClosersSection::test_a_store_row_names_its_item_id`.

## v0.313.4

- fix: **an unowned gate no longer wakes every manager when no Fleet Manager is live.** `route_to_fleet()` returned the emit path whenever no live Fleet Manager resolved, so a `producer_kind: cron` gate with no session id — or any `unowned` gate — was handed to every manager's arm. It now distinguishes the two cases the old `if not self_id or not fleet` collapsed: an **unreadable** registry (`UNREADABLE`) still fails open, but a **readable** registry with no Fleet Manager suppresses the `unowned` class and counts it in a new `dropped(unowned-no-fleet)` bucket on the `LIVENESS` line, so the fallback is never silent. `dead-manager` and `claim-dead` are a separate residual and keep their fail-open emit.

## v0.313.3

- fix: **a session's cause is resolved from its own log, or reported unverified — never borrowed from a sibling's.** `scripts/cause-resolve.py` takes a session id and reads exactly two sources, `sessions/<sid>/events.jsonl` and the state ledger, and `commands/steps/cause-verify.md` calls it instead of leaving the round to search free-form. A line licenses a cause only when it **names the session AND states the action**: `idle-reaper-tick session=<sid> verb=reap outcome=<o>` with a non-refusal outcome, or `job=idle-reaper action=closed sid=<sid>`. `drain: session <sid> at turn boundary` names the session and asserts nothing — the drain observes a boundary and never kills — so it resolves `unverified` with `names-but-does-not-assert`. Measured 2026-11-14, round 60: a round read `reaper` off a sibling session's reap tick (`9b08cde6`'s file names `80288603`, `61c6c69b`, `f8052f51`, not the target), and the manager then read `drain` off a turn-boundary line — two false causes for one session in one day, both in a confident register. The ledger is read for context only: it records `absent-from-sources`, never a mechanism, so no cause is derived from it. `scripts/tests/test_cause_resolve.py` pins the four failure modes — the positive control (own log names and asserts → `reaper`), the sibling-borrow decoy (a sibling file naming the target → still `unverified`), the turn-boundary line, and a refusal outcome — plus the ledger-context, absent-directory and nested-`content`-block shapes, and a direct assertion that no path outside `sessions/<sid>/` is opened. Both falsifiers are mutation-proven: globbing the sessions root fails 2 tests, and an always-`unverified` stub fails the positive control.

## v0.313.2

- refactor: **the fleet round no longer opens flagged rows — the wrapper's tick does.** Row 5c, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` and `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md` are deleted, and every cross-reference is reworded in the same change (`fleet-drive.md`'s router and carve-out, `read-guides.md`, `open.md`, `fleet-round-tail.md`, `keepalive-topicless-goals.md`, `keepalive.md`, `output-shape.md` items 13 and 14, `rules.md`, `fleet-sweep-reader.md`), together with the two checkers that asserted the leg's presence. The reopen is `claude-wrapper`'s model-free `flagged-tick`: measured 2026-11-14 over 24 h, 83 close→reopen pairs, median 36.4 min, every opened row live afterwards. Both probes return 0 lines: `grep -rn "flagged-open" commands agents` and `grep -n "5c" commands/fleet-drive.md`.

## v0.313.1

- fix: **a session id claimed by two pids is resolved instead of refused.** Every close verb returned `ambiguous-pid` whenever more than one registry entry carried a session id, so a finished worker could not be closed at all — `dd5d9944` was refused on every tick on 2026-11-13 and stayed idle until the wrapper closed it by id instead. Measured shape: a shell wrapper and the child it launched, both live, both carrying the id.
  **Several entries are not automatically a refusal.** `restart-worker.py` gains `pid_alive`, `parent_pid`, `is_ancestor` and `resolve_owned_target`, and the three close verbs (`close-worker.py`, `close-idle-tab.py`, `reap-idle-headless.py`) call it. Order, each step refusing rather than guessing: one claimant wins; several with exactly one live pid takes the live one; several live with exactly one descendant of the others takes that descendant; anything else stays `ambiguous`, because picking between unrelated live processes is a guess that closes the wrong session.
  **The ancestor case resolves to the DESCENDANT, and that is not a preference between two pids:** a wrapper and its child are one logical process — the wrapper exists to start and supervise the child, and the child is the work. Refusing there would blind every close verb to the ordinary shape of a supervised session, which is the shape that actually collides.
  `find_target` is deliberately untouched, so `restart-worker.py`'s own resume path is not silently re-pointed; adopting the rule there is a deliberate one-line follow-up, not a side effect of this change. The `note` naming the chosen pid is returned and printed to **stderr** — every verb's first stdout line is a machine-parsed token.
  ⚠️ `reap-idle-headless.py`'s other `ambiguous-pid` refusals are **pid-recycle guards** (the registry entry changed during the guards, the pid's start time moved). They stay fail-closed and untouched; they are not this class.

## v0.313.0

- chore: **the `idle-reaper` launchd entry point is removed.** Its tick now runs inside `claude-wrapper` as an in-service handler (v0.25.0), so `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py`, its two test modules and `launchd/com.bborbe.idle-reaper.plist` are deleted rather than left for `make update` to restore a working tick from. `scripts/fleet-keeper.py` is **retained**: `claude-wrapper`'s in-service fleet keeper execs it as its predicate (`FleetKeeperCheckScript` in `pkg/lapse_exec.go`), so deleting it would break the handler at the next `make update`. `scripts/session-exit-record.py` is also retained — its Go replacement fires only on a child exit and has not been observed firing once, and deleting the only fallback for a handler that has never run is a one-way door. The marketplace clone under `~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/` is generated by `make update` and is not this change's population. Change set: `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py`, `scripts/tests/test_idle_reaper_tick.py`, `scripts/tests/test_busy_parked_card.py`, `launchd/com.bborbe.idle-reaper.plist`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.312.0

- chore: **retire the fleet-pusher launchd agents.** The 900 s wake tick and its 300 s watchdog now run as interval jobs inside the `claude-wrapper` daemon, so `scripts/fleet-pusher-tick.sh`, `scripts/fleet-pusher-watch.sh`, their two plists and `scripts/tests/test_fleet_pusher.py` are removed. The behaviour is ported with specs in `bborbe/claude-wrapper` (the pid → tty → pane join, the 13-verdict screen guard, the tick and the watchdog); `docs/fleet-pusher.md` is rewritten for the in-process jobs and carries the rollback. Change set: `scripts/fleet-pusher-tick.sh`, `scripts/fleet-pusher-watch.sh`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-pusher.plist`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-pusher-watch.plist`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_pusher.py`, `docs/fleet-pusher.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.311.2

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py` imports under Python 3.9 again, so the sweep-gate wake path can read a subject's state.** The script used PEP 604 annotations (`float | None`), which are evaluated at function-definition time and raise under 3.9 — and the in-process sweep-gate job invokes `manager-wake.py` with `/usr/bin/python3` (3.9.6 on this Mac), which then runs the liveness read with `sys.executable`, so every read failed and `pane-wake-guard.py` refused the wake as `skip:state-unreadable`. Measured 2026-11-14 on the wake emitter's first live ticks (09:31Z and 10:35Z), both `skip:state-unreadable` while the same read returned `OK` under a 3.15 interpreter. `from __future__ import annotations` makes the annotations lazy, so the script imports and runs unchanged under both. `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.311.1

- fix: **a LEAKED `pending_turns` no longer holds a parked headless worker past every bound.** The wrapper increments the counter when it accepts a turn and decrements it when the turn ends; a turn that ends without the decrement leaves a record reading `state: idle`, `question: null`, `pending_turns: 1` that nothing ever clears, and `guard()` refused on `pending_turns != 0` unconditionally. Measured 2026-11-14: session `ece87199` carried `pending_turns: 1` with `last_event_age_seconds: 49751` (13.8 h) against the wrapper's own 16-minute `childHold()`, resident 14 h 42 m against a 15-minute bound, while the four genuinely pending wrappers on the same machine read 1/3/24/28 s. `_leaked_pending_turn()` is the **mirror** of `_stale_busy()`, not a widening of it: `_stale_busy()` discounts a stale registry `status` and therefore *requires* `pending_turns == 0`, which is exactly why a leaked counter was the one input it could never see. The new predicate discounts the counter itself, and frees a session only when `last_event_age_seconds` is past `PENDING_TURN_LEAK_SECONDS` (16 min) **and** no open tool marker sits inside `TOOL_MARKER_WINDOW` — a child silent inside one long tool call keeps its refusal. Both surfaces are wired: `guard()` and `main()`'s pre-signal re-read, because a re-read left refusing would silently undo the first decision. Fails closed on every reading it cannot take (a missing or non-integer `pending_turns`, an unageable age, a non-`idle` wrapper state, a parked question — that limb stays on its own park clock). The refusal token is unchanged: a live counter still reports `wrapper-busy` with `wrapper pending_turns <n>, expected 0`. Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.311.0

- fix: **the transfer bundle's name carries the repo, not just the PR number.** `DEFAULT_REPOS` sweeps two repos into one `REBASE_BUNDLE_DIR`, and PR numbers collide across them — 521 exists in both — so `pr-<n>.bundle` let one repo's bundle overwrite the other's between the sweep writing it and the operator pasting the line, handing over the wrong commit with no error anywhere. The path is now `<owner>-<repo>-pr-<n>.bundle`, and the Mac's copy takes its name from the bundle's own basename so the two cannot drift. Also documented: a range bundle carries `origin/master..<branch>`, so the operator's checkout must have an `origin/master` at least as new as the pod's — a Mac that has not fetched since the pod did is refused by `git fetch`. Change set: `scripts/rebase-agent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py`, `docs/rebase-agent.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the push line a cluster run emits is now runnable on the operator's Mac, because the rebased commit travels to it as a bundle instead of living in a scratch ref the pod takes with it.** Measured 2026-11-14 from a scheduled run's `record:` — `git -C /workspace/Documents/workspaces/claude-supervisor push --force-with-lease=… origin refs/rebase-agent/pr/521:refs/heads/…` — the `-C` path is the pod's clone and `refs/rebase-agent/pr/521` exists only in that pod's object store, so both halves fail on a Mac. The pod now writes a **range bundle** (`origin/master..refs/rebase-agent/pr/<n>`) to a transfer volume, and the line is one paste of three `&&`-chained commands: `kubectlWRAPPER cp <reader-pod>:<bundle> /tmp/…`, `git -C <operator-path> fetch … +<ref>:<ref>`, then the lease-pinned push. ⚠️ **The line's `-C` path is `REBASE_OPERATOR_WORKSPACES` plus the repo name, never `cfg["path"]`** — that one is the pod's clone, `expanduser`d against a `HOME` the deployment sets to `/workspace`, so reusing it would emit the pod's path again and re-create the defect the channel exists to remove. A literal `/Users/...` in the source was the first cut and is worse: it resolves on exactly one machine. ⚠️ **The fetch is forced** because the Mac's scratch ref survives between lines, so a re-sweep fetches a newer commit onto an older ref. Every `PUSH` row carries `transfer: {kind, target}` and every non-PUSH row carries `transfer: null`, so a reader can tell "no transfer" from a build that never learned the field. Four deployment facts are required — `REBASE_BUNDLE_DIR`, `REBASE_KUBECTL`, `REBASE_READER_POD`, `REBASE_OPERATOR_WORKSPACES` — and any one unset **refuses the row before the rebase runs**, rather than emitting a line that fails only after it has been pasted. Change set: `scripts/rebase-agent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py`, `docs/rebase-agent.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.310.1

- fix: **the bare-`gopls` fixture targets `/usr/bin/gnuyes` when present, so it survives the `ubuntu-latest` roll to ubuntu-26.04.** `test_a_bare_gopls_child_does_not_refuse` symlinked `gopls` → `/usr/bin/yes` and required `pgrep -P` to find the child. On ubuntu-26.04 `/usr/bin/yes` is the uutils multi-call binary, which refuses a foreign argv[0] (`Security violation: Requested utility \`gopls\` does not match executable name`) and exits before `pgrep` can see it, so the fixture built no shape and the assertion failed — on CI only. Measured: the same commit passed on ubuntu-24.04 and failed on ubuntu-26.04, and the prescribed `& wait` remedy changed nothing because `yes` itself dies. `gnuyes` is GNU `yes` (ignores argv[0], spins forever) and is present on 26.04, absent on 24.04, so preferring it with a `yes` fallback keeps both images green and leaves `assertTrue(pids, …)` intact. Change set: `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.310.0

- feat: **each `PUSH` row's push line is posted to the fleet attention board, so a cluster run delivers to the operator instead of to a log the pod takes with it.** `write_state()` and the `record:` line survive the pod only as long as someone reads `kubectl logs`; the goal this serves asks that the push lines *reach* the operator, and a run whose lines nobody sees has moved the problem rather than solved it. The write is guarded by `ATTENTION_STORE_URL` (plus `ATTENTION_STORE_TOKEN` for any non-loopback store — an unauthenticated remote store is readable by anything that reaches it) and deliberately defaults to **nothing** rather than to `localhost:18080`, because this script also runs on the Mac and a default would post every sweep to whatever happens to listen there. ⚠️ **The declaration is the load-bearing half, not the transport.** `liveness_ref` is `owner:`, not `heartbeat:` or `session:`: the store's duplicate suppression only collapses a re-push while it reads the producer as live, and it resolves both of those against the **store host's** filesystem and session registry, which a pod appears in neither of — so every 15-minute sweep would add another card. `owner:` probes nothing and returns live unconditionally, so the card must carry `expires_at` (1 h) as its only removal; once the PR is rebased or merged the re-push stops and the card clears itself. `answer_mechanism: ack` because nothing routes back to the pod, `producer_kind: cron`, `provenance_class: explicit`, `dedup_key: rebase-agent:<repo>#<n>`. ⚠️ **A board failure is never a rebase failure:** the post sits outside the per-PR `try`, records `board_error` on the row, and leaves the verdict alone. Change set: `scripts/rebase-agent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py` (11 new cases), `docs/rebase-agent.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.309.2

- fix: **the manager liveness record carries a WORK SIGNAL beside its round cadence, so `--state` stops certifying a manager that has not worked and stops reporting lapsed one that has.** `observed_period()` is built from `--arm` beats, so the record measured ROUND cadence while every consumer read it as "is this manager working": it granted `OK` off a gate tick with no round (a gate can poll every 15 minutes for 20 hours), and read `STALE` on a manager doing real non-round work — measured 2026-11-13, one manager read `OK age=73220s … gate-driven` after an operator `/ask`, an approval, a three-round gate and a headless spawn, while another read `STALE` mid-work. `--state`'s detail now carries `work=<seconds>s`, the age of the manager's own transcript resolved from the session id `--arm` records in `<slug>.session` (the file Claude Code appends to on every turn, so it moves on ANY work, round or not; `work=none` when no session marker or transcript). `OK` now means a round OR work inside the limit; a new `IDLE` state means a live gate is watching a manager with neither — armed and woken, producing nothing, and **not** lapsed, so nothing restarts it; `STALE` stays no-round-no-work-no-gate. The false `OK` was the gate tick certifying a slot that produces nothing; the false `STALE` restarted a working manager. Every consumer states the new reading in its own text: `manager-liveness.py`'s docstring and `--check`, `answer-path-check.py` (IDLE refuses a headless spawn), `crashed-managers.py` (IDLE is a state, not a probe failure), `pane-wake-guard.py` (IDLE falls through to the screen verdict), `owner.py` (IDLE still OWNS its subject), `topic-keepalive.py` and `commands/topic-drive.md` (IDLE → `jump`, never `restart`), `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` (IDLE must arm, or the round that ends it is skipped), and the three `agents/*-sweep-reader.md` tables. A case per direction — a false `OK` and a false `STALE` — pins both against silent regression. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/answer-path-check.py`, `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py`, `scripts/owner.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/steps/round-liveness.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_liveness_step.py`, `scripts/tests/test_owner.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.309.1

- fix: the incident ledger's deadline escalation no longer posts a board card. `escalate` stamps `escalated_by` and prints the escalation line into the round's output (`scripts/incident-ledger.py`; `commands/steps/drive.md`). The card carried `--option ack` as its only control — an acknowledgement rendered as a question — and measured 2026-11-13 it was the board's largest misroute class: 401 manager-kind `ack`-only items on the Fleet Manager producer, 16 flagged `only ack is not actionable` in 2026W44. `card_id` now belongs to the blocker card alone; escalation no longer writes it.

## v0.309.0

- feat: add a `check-task-link` gate that fails a PR whose body carries no `Task: <task_identifier>` line

## v0.308.1

- fix: **`is_helper` recognises a bare `gopls`, so a headless session held open by nothing but a parked language server is reaped instead of refused `watcher-alive` at every age.** Claude Code spawns `gopls` as the session's LSP child and holds it for the session's lifetime; nothing closes it on idle, and `is_helper` did not list it, so the child read as live work forever. Measured 2026-11-13: of 15 long-idle `sdk-cli` sessions, 5 were anchored and every one carried `uv` + `claude-wrapper --mcp-stdio` + **`gopls`** as its children, with `gopls` the only non-helper among them, the oldest idle 545 min — and `grep -nE 'gopls|shutdown|terminate' scripts/reap-idle-headless.py` returned nothing at v0.304.1. A live sweep the same day found six `gopls`-bearing sessions: four `idle`, one `busy` (`6a70d5c2`), one `shell` under `entrypoint=cli`. ⚠️ **The discriminator is `os.path.basename(parts[0]) == "gopls"` AND `len(parts) == 1`, and the length test is the load-bearing half.** The LSP child is spawned bare — measured on every live `gopls` — while a worker that backgrounds `gopls check ./...` carries an argument and must keep reading as a watcher, or its session would be reaped mid-work, the exact mistake the function's bias exists to prevent. The executable anchor keeps a `bash -c 'gopls …'` wrapper a watcher too. ⚠️ **Scope is wider than headless, and that is deliberate:** `close-idle-tab.py` shares `reap.watchers()`, so a tab worker idle with only a `gopls` child now closes instead of refusing `watcher-alive` — the same reasoning applies to an idle tab, and `tab_gate`'s own guards are unchanged. ⚠️ **Reaping does not orphan the server:** the close signals the session pid alone (`os.kill(pid, SIGTERM)`, no process-group kill), but parent death closes the child's stdin, and a direct experiment measured `gopls` exiting on stdin EOF in ~0.5 s. That is measured, not pinned by a test — no suite here spawns a real `gopls`. Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.308.0

- feat: a goal round now files the task an undriven SC/DoD box needs and routes it by its door — new row 4d (`commands/steps/goal-plan-undriven.md`), decision in `scripts/undriven-plan.py`. Two-way rows the step filed are approved `--by manager` and open through the ordinary `To open` path below the worker target; one-way rows ride the round's one approve card with blast radius and rollback; wide-reach rows get a `### Decision:` block and a talk-through card that asks for no decision; `re-scope` proposals are never filed. At most one file per goal per tick. Every other `phase: todo` row stays the operator's.

## v0.307.0

- feat: **the rebase agent's run record survives the pod, and carries what started the run.** The agent is moving off the operator's Mac into a nuke prod CronJob, where the state directory is an `emptyDir` — so `latest.json`, the only record the launchd job needed, would die with the pod and a scheduled run would leave nothing to read. Every run now prints one `record: {json}` line to stdout beside the human render, naming `generated_at`, the trigger, the state path and every row with its push line, and `--trigger` (default `manual`) is written into the payload as well. The flag is the point of the change rather than decoration: a CronJob passes `--trigger schedule`, and a hand-started run keeps the default, so "was this sweep unattended" stops being a guess. `write_state()` returns the payload it wrote and takes an optional state directory, so the record half is testable without touching `~/.local/state`. Change set: `scripts/rebase-agent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py`, `docs/rebase-agent.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.306.0

- fix: **`park-event.py register` writes the `later (on …)` line the fleet round reads, so a park made through it can actually be closed and its slot freed.** The exit mechanism was already correct — `close-worker.py` accepts a future `defer_date` and SIGTERMs a headless pid — but `agents/fleet-drive.md` classifies a clock-bound park on three facts, and the third (`grep -c 'later (on ' "<task file>"` ≥ 1) was the parking session's own prose step. A park made by a script, a resumed session, or a worker that skipped that step left a correct defer with no record: measured 2026-11-13, **29 of 37** future-dated `in_progress` rows carried no line (15 of the 22 session-shaped ones), and a row read `unverifiable` is never listed under `FREEABLE TABS`, so a live parked session's process holds its slot until it dies on its own. `write_task_key` now writes the line with the frontmatter — resolved through `vault-cli task show --output json`'s `file_path`, so the record lands in the same page as the defer it describes — placing it inside `# Progress` (creating the section when the page has none), replacing an existing line for the same event on a re-park rather than stacking a stale instant, and skipping a past `--recheck` because a due row is not a parked one. `--action` names what replaces the live watcher when it is not the fleet round, and `advance_members` moves a peer's record with its clock. The new `backfill --vault <name> [--apply]` sweeps the rows parked before this existed, re-deriving the population with the same filter the measurement uses so the before/after counts describe one population. Change set: `scripts/park-event.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py`, `commands/worker-drive.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `docs/worker-session-states.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.305.1

- fix: **`rebase-agent.py` names a `--repo` outside the rebase set instead of reporting a clean queue.** `main()` filtered `DEFAULT_REPOS` by `--repo`, so an out-of-set repo emptied the list and `render()` printed `no open PR is behind master — nothing to rebase` — a clean verdict for a repo it never looked at. Measured 2026-11-13 on `bborbe/git-rest#81` and `bborbe/agent-task-executor#161`, both `CONFLICTING` and both reported clean. The out-of-set name is now printed together with the set it was measured against, `--json` carries `repos_examined` and `repos_not_in_set`, and an out-of-set-only run exits 2 — "not examined" is never "clean". Change set: `scripts/rebase-agent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.305.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:open` starts a goal or topic manager's loop itself, instead of handing the operator two commands to paste.** The GOAL and TOPIC branches spawned the manager and then printed an instruction, so every manager open cost a manual paste and left a pane whose idle prompt was indistinguishable from a failed start; the WORKER path in the same file has always typed its own command into the pane it just spawned, and the rule that forbids driving other sessions by `send-text` already covers this case by construction ("only ever send this into a pane THIS command just spawned, while it is still idle"). § 2B item 4 now sends `/vault-cli:work-on-goal` and then `/supervisor:goal-loop` (the first writes the durable `claude_session_id`, so it must land first), and § 2C item 4 sends `/supervisor:goal-loop`, both in Step 3.2's shape — text, then `\r` as a separate step. ⚠️ **Readiness is `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py`'s verdict, never a fixed sleep and never a bare glyph grep**: it is the repo's one definition of "this pane can take a keystroke", and a bare `❯` test is wrong in both directions — it reads a mid-turn composer as ready (the composer is empty while the TUI is processing, and a submitted Enter is discarded) and it reads the dim next-prompt suggestion 27 of 39 sampled panes carried as typed text that is not. The wait after `work-on-goal` is the long one (300 s) because it runs a full planning chain; a timeout is reported and the second command is not sent. ⚠️ **Both waits are fail-closed and both submits are verified** — on exhaustion the block exits instead of typing into a pane the guard refused, and after each `\r` the guard must read `skip:spinner` (a submitted command) rather than an idle composer (a swallowed Enter). The loop uses a POSIX counter, not `seq`, which stock macOS does not ship. `scripts/check-open-manager-start.py` pins the whole shape in `precommit`: the superseded instruction is a literal denial, and each branch must carry its `send-text`, its separate `$'\r'`, the guard-based wait and the fail-closed exit. `scripts/budget-baseline.json`'s `commands/open.md` entry is raised by hand (+3 360 bytes) rather than tightened — the growth is this feature. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/check-open-manager-start.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.304.1

- fix: **the wake guard now fails closed when a subject resolves but its state cannot be read.** `read_subject_state()` returned `None` on a non-zero exit, an unparsable line, or the 20 s timeout, and `None` meant "no subject" — so the guard fell through to the screen verdict, which for a sleeping pane is `wake`. Measured 2026-11-13: with the subject armed `SLEEPING until 12:03:40` and `--state` reading `SLEEPING (2674s remaining)`, **three `DEFAULT_MESSAGE` wakes landed at 11:29, 11:44 and 11:59**, each a pane-typed user turn — while the same script returned `skip:sleeping` when run by hand minutes earlier. Ruled out: a stale plugin (every live session holds 0.303.2; all cached versions ≥0.302.6 carry the fix), the subject record (unchanged), the marker (present), a second producer (repo-wide search finds only `pane-wake.py`), and the wiring. ⚠️ **The two outcomes are now distinct**: `guard.STATE_UNREADABLE` when a subject resolved and the read failed — refused as `skip:state-unreadable`, because a missed wake re-arms a manager through its gate while a spurious one types into a pane whose session then closes it for good — and `None` only when no subject resolved at all, which still wakes. `manager-wake.py` always knows its subject, so its failed read is the sentinel too. `fleet-triage-verify.md` deliberately does **not** treat `skip:state-unreadable` as a valid wait, so the row stays STUCK and surfaces the broken read. Change set: `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py`, `scripts/pane-wake.py`, `scripts/manager-wake.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_wake_guard.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_wake.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_wake.py`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-verify.md`, `commands/fleet-triage.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.304.0

- feat: **the idle reaper surfaces a question-parked wrapper session that nothing closes and no card names.** A session whose wrapper record reads `state: idle` / `pending_turns: 0` / a `question` parked past `IDLE_FLOOR` is refused `busy-target` at every age — `_stale_busy()` (v0.303.2) requires no parked question, so the stale-status reap deliberately does not reach it — and nothing ever closes it. Since v0.303.2 such a record **is** a candidate, so the tick does emit an `outcome=busy-target` line for it; a refusal line in a launchd log is not an ask, which is why this population is machine-visible and never operator-visible. Measured 2026-11-13 09:08: five `sdk-cli` sessions read exactly that shape in the wrapper store while all five were registry non-idle. The operator's ruling (card `039df378a626f84a8e61fa11863517ec`) is *"keep `busy-target` absolute — fix the missing card instead, not the reaper"*, so this change **does not widen the guard**: `reap-idle-headless.py` gains `busy_parked_session_ids()` (a read that never signals) and `idle-reaper-tick.py` posts one `ack` card per session **per park**, deduped on `<session_id>:<requested_at>` in `~/.claude/state/idle-reaper-carded.json` and re-posted once per 24 h TTL while the park persists. The card names the session, its park age and that `busy-target` is why it was not reaped; nothing is closed and the pid stays alive. ⚠️ Cards stay **outside** the tick's `rows`, because `summary_line()` derives `candidates=` and `refused=` from them; `--dry-run` posts nothing and writes no marker. The read fails closed on every unreadable input (wrapper record, `requested_at`, attention store) and skips a session already carrying its own open card — `open_cards()` filters `producer_id == session_id`, so it cannot see the tick's own posts. Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py`, `scripts/tests/test_busy_parked_card.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.303.2
- fix: **a headless session stuck at a non-`idle` registry status no longer holds its spawn slot — the reaper checks the wrapper record and the tool marker before trusting the status field.** `reap-idle-headless.py`'s `busy-target` guard refused on the registry field alone, and that field is a claim written by the harness: it survives a turn that ended without a status update, so a session can sit at `busy` (or `shell`) with nothing in flight and never be reaped. ⚠️ **`candidate_session_ids()` filtered every non-`idle` record out before the guards ran**, so the guard change alone was unreachable; a record past `IDLE_FLOOR` is now a candidate, while a fresh one still costs no refusal. Measured 2026-11-13: 62 `busy-target` refusals across 41 sessions, 40 of which cleared on their own (so the guard is right for genuinely mid-turn rows), and a live read of 20 `busy` entries found exactly one past 20 minutes with no open tool marker. The new `_stale_busy()` falls through only when an idle wrapper record with `pending_turns == 0` and no parked `question` **and** no open tool marker inside `TOOL_MARKER_WINDOW` all say nothing is pending; a missing wrapper or marker reading still fails closed to `busy-target`. ⚠️ The reaper read no tool marker at all before this, so the guard reorder alone would have let a mid-call session through. Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.303.1
- fix: **the idle reaper closes an unanchored finished headless session instead of refusing `task-unresolved` forever.** Measured 2026-11-13: `outcome=task-unresolved` was the reaper's largest refusal class — 1574 `verb=reap` lines over 44 distinct session ids, of which **10 were live headless sessions idle 88 min – 22 h** holding a spawn-cap slot, unanchored by construction (`claude_session_id` and `metrics_sessions` both absent across every configured vault, no goal/topic page). The token meant "no anchor", so the guard meant to protect real work was refusing on sessions that own nothing. `reap-idle-headless.py` now splits the token's two causes: `vault-not-searched` (the session's own vault declares no `tasks_dir`, so its anchor may live where this process cannot read) keeps refusing `task-unresolved`, while an id in no page among every configured vault closes the process and prints `unanchored-closed` as its first line — the guard chain has already proven it headless, idle past `IDLE_FLOOR`, card-free, wrapper-idle and pid-identity-verified, and the transcript is kept exactly as for any other reap. ⚠️ **An empty directory list keeps refusing**: when no configured vault declares a `tasks_dir`, nothing was searched, and an absent search is not an absent anchor — without that guard an empty `vault-cli` config would read every session as unanchored and close the whole fleet in one tick. `idle-reaper-tick.py` maps the new first-line token to its own outcome and counts it as a close. Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_idle_reaper_tick.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.303.0

- feat: **`restart-worker.py` can start a fresh worker bound to its own task, so a restarted tab worker re-anchors instead of coming back in the wrong role.** `--fresh-prompt` accepted exactly one value — `/supervisor:fleet-loop`, which is the *fleet manager's* loop — so a worker restarted through it came back running the wrong tier, and the alternative was an unanchored session. The new `--fresh-prompt-task` composes `/vault-cli:work-on-task <this session's anchored task>` from the task `close-worker.py::find_task` already resolves, and adds two refusals, `no-task-anchor` and `task-handed-off`, both fired before anything is signalled. ⚠️ **The allowlist property survives the task name:** the command is a constant (`TASK_FRESH_PROMPT_COMMAND`) and only the task name varies, escaped for the double-quoted `bash -lc` context by `bash_dq_escape` — no caller reaches the shell with a command of its own choosing. `--allow-busy-lapse` now accepts either fresh-prompt flag, because a stalled tab worker is `busy`/`shell` by definition and the lift is the only way to restart one at all. The same escaping is applied to the session `title` on both resume branches — it comes from the registry `name` and was embedded raw, so a name carrying `"` or `$(…)` could break the `bash -lc` string exactly as a task name could. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.10

- fix: **the fleet digest's first line is composed in code, so the `snapshot written:` label can no longer be written twice.** Measured 2026-11-05: a round's first line read `snapshot written: snapshot written: 2026-11-05T17:34:30Z - 43 sessions`. The sweep prompt told its reader to quote `fleet-snapshot.py`'s own already-labelled stdout line into a template slot that carried the label itself — two instructions in one prompt, and the agent followed the wrong one. `fleet-snapshot.py` now composes the whole line (`compose_header`), unwrapping a repeated label from whatever value it is handed, and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` pastes that file's line 1 verbatim and composes only from line 2 onward — the prompt is 11 bytes shorter for it, so `scripts/budget-baseline.json` tightened rather than moved. The `DIGEST … · N sessions · snapshot written:` shape `manager-receipt.py` reads a digest by is unchanged, and the consumer-side unwrap in `fleet-digest.py`'s `parse_snapshot` stays — two independent guards are the point. Change set: `scripts/fleet-snapshot.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_snapshot.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.9

- fix: **the keep-alive leg's four decision-park readings reach disk, so a round's decision numbers are auditable after the hand-back is gone.** `decision-park-resume.py` gains `--persist-beside <this round's .digest>`: it writes `<dir>/<round>.decision` — same directory, same sanitized round filename — carrying `round=`, `written_at=` and the four readings, rendered by **one** function (`summary_line()`) that also produces the printed line, so the artifact and the hand-back cannot disagree. Measured 2026-11-12: `grep -rl decision-candidates ~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/` returned **0 files** while the keepalive payloads existed, so a reading that reached the manager in round 44's hand-back (`decision-candidates=35 · decision-resumed-a=0 · decision-resumed-d=0 · decision-unread=12`) was recoverable from a transcript and nowhere else. ⚠️ **A failed write is now a verdict, not silence** — exit **3**, `decision-persist: FAILED — <path>: <error>` on stderr, the readings still printed, so a read-only or full state dir cannot quietly put the numbers back into a message only; the hand-back carries `decision-persist=written|FAILED` and `check-handback-keys.py` requires it. The script refuses a path that is not a `.digest` rather than deriving a sibling of something else, and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`'s prune clause gains `*.decision` so the new store is bounded like its siblings. `docs/handback-contract.md` gains § Which readings survive the round, naming which of the leg's readings are payload-persisted and which stay message-only. ⚠️ **`--persist-beside` is required on the decide path**, so a round that forgot it is refused (exit 2) rather than silently reverting the defect; the file carries `status=ok|unread`, and a failed read keeps its `decision-unread=1` sentinel instead of collapsing into the all-zero line a healthy empty round prints. ⚠️ **Not a digest key** — the four readings do not enter `fleet-legs.py`'s `LEGS` section, which is a separate contract change. Change set: `scripts/decision-park-resume.py`, `scripts/tests/test_decision_park_resume.py`, `commands/steps/keepalive-decision-parks.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.8

- fix: **the permission tier's classifier budget is now a measured multiple of one real spawn, and a timeout is pinned by a regression test instead of being rediscovered as a false routing failure.** `server/permission-tier.mjs` spawned `scripts/permission-tier.py` with a fixed 5 s ceiling. Measured 2026-11-09 on a loaded Mac the classifier took 1.78 s per call and the fixture run makes 40+ calls, so one spawn crossed the budget: the wrapper failed closed to `operator` (correct) and `server/permission-tier.test.mjs` reported `+ 'operator' - 'manager'` — a routing difference that does not exist — while the real fact, that the classifier never answered, was invisible. The budget is now `BUDGET_MULTIPLE` × one calibrated spawn, floored at 10 s and capped at 120 s, so a slow machine gets a proportional ceiling instead of a number chosen on an idle one; `permissionTierTimeoutMs()` exposes the formula, and two tests pin it plus the timeout failure mode (the `reason` names `ETIMEDOUT` and the `form` stays `unverifiable`, so the result cannot be read as a tier verdict). Change set: `server/permission-tier.mjs`, `server/permission-tier.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.7

- fix: **the park-nudge leg no longer drafts a park for a manager-tier session, which closed the pane that is a goal manager's only wake path.** `scripts/park-nudge.py` selected on `watching_min` alone, so it drafted for every session over the 15-minute bound — and the park it asks for is a WORKER mechanism: `park-event.py register` binds an event key to an **anchored task** and writes a `defer_date`, and a manager has no anchored task, so the request is inapplicable to it rather than merely unwise. Following it (register → `defer_date` → `session-self-exit.py`) closes the pane, and a goal manager's whole wake path is the detached sweep gate typing into that pane — the arm text says it: *"a goal manager whose gate cannot reach it never runs again."* Measured 2026-11-12: fleet round 44 drafted four park-nudges against managers, each declined from its own reading, and **every one was healthy** — `--state` read `OK` at 28%, 52% and 55% of its own bound, plus one armed at `age=0s`. ⚠️ **`OK` is the normal state of a healthy armed manager, so this is a TIER test and not a `--state` test** — a guard that skipped only `SLEEPING` / `STOPPED` would still have nudged the common case. The new `manager_tier()` tests tier FIRST and reads `--state` SECOND, as a backstop: (1) the session id `manager-liveness.py --arm` recorded for it, which is now written to `<slug>.session` beside the cadence and is the one POSITIVE statement of manager tier on disk — every other candidate signal is an absence, shared with the operator's own tabs; (2) the manager subject its name resolves to, which covers a manager that armed before the marker existed; (3) `manager-liveness.py`'s `classify()` for any row that still reaches the draft path, which catches a pre-split `<base>.cadence` record the slug set cannot match. A worker past its bound is still nudged — an unarmed candidate subject reads `UNARMED`, never `OK`. The summary gains `<k> manager-skipped` and `--json` gains a `manager_rows` list naming each skipped session and which signal caught it, because a zero from a round that saw no manager must not read the same as a gate that fired. A `manager-liveness.py` that cannot be imported exits 2 rather than running without the gate: without it a manager is nudged and the output cannot be told from a worker's. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.6

- fix: **a wrapper-backed worker parked on a question is now reaped once its park exceeds the 15-minute bound, instead of being held forever.** `reap-idle-headless.py`'s `wrapper-busy` guard refused any wrapper record carrying a `question` at every age, so the 121 `wrapper-busy` refusals in the launchd `idle-reaper.log` were sessions the reaper could see and would never close. A parked question is now aged by its **park clock** — `question.requested_at` — rather than the registry's `statusUpdatedAt`: the registry clock says when the session last went idle, which can be hours before the question was asked, and it runs before `wrapper-busy`, so a session idle 2 h whose question was asked 5 min ago would have been closed 5 minutes into a fresh park. The registry clock is therefore bypassed for a parked wrapper session. Within the bound the same shape still refuses `wrapper-busy`; an unageable `requested_at` still fails closed; `gate-held` is untouched and still refuses at every age (operator decision `d09d23f5bdd857734714dbdc6a1d5f07` — closing a card-holding session loses a question a person is about to answer, and the answer leg marks such sessions unresumable). Change set: `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.5

- fix: **a deliberate stand-down now removes its subject from the auto-reopen set at every tier, not only from the manager loop.** `/supervisor:stop` writes `<branch>--<slug>.stopped`, and `manager-liveness.py --state` has always reported `STOPPED` — but the auto-open path never read it: `scripts/open-queue.py` knew only the task tier's `stopped:` page field, so **any flagged task under a stopped goal or topic was still selected and opened**, and `scripts/adopt-orphans.py` handed such a subject's stranded workers to a new owner and re-opened the work by another door. Measured 2026-11-12 on the goal `Replace Nginx Ingress – Migrate to Gateway API + Google LBs`, whose marker was present and rewritten by later stops while its flagged tasks kept opening. The marker read is now ONE resolver, `manager-liveness.is_subject_stopped(subject, branch)` — a second copy would drift from `stop()`'s writer, and the drift would be silent — imported by both consumers. `open-queue.collect()` gains `topics_dir` / `goals_dir` channels (defaulting to the `23 Topics` and `24 Goals` beside `--tasks-dir`) and resolves a row through `managed_by` → its `goals:` → the topics whose body `## Goals` lists that goal, plus a topic listing the task directly. The goal half is the vault's goal PAGES, tested **first** exactly as `fleet_board.vault_index()` does: a title that is both a goal page and a task file (~5 live, e.g. `builds are not tied to the mac`) otherwise lands in the task table, the goal→topic link is lost, and the suppression silently stops working. The `topics:` task frontmatter stays out of scope, because the fleet's own readers use the body. The guard sits immediately after the task-tier `stopped` read, by the same ordering rule: the operator's act outranks every DERIVED guard, and its reason is `subject stood down` so the two tiers stay distinguishable. `adopt-orphans.py` skips before the `claim()` call and reports it — `skipped: manager <sid8> — subject "<subject>" stood down (<marker>)` — because a silent skip is indistinguishable from a leg that found nothing. ⚠️ **The keepalive restart path needed no change and is unchanged:** it already honours the marker transitively (`topic-keepalive.py` → `owner.py` → `manager-liveness.py --state` → `leave`), and a regression probe now pins that so the new reads cannot break it. Probes go through `collect()` → `select(live=0, target=10)` with injected capacity, never the bare CLI, whose `selected` is truncated by the live fleet's worker count and would be blind on a capped fleet. ⚠️ **`/supervisor:open --flagged` is unchanged and stays so:** that batch is the operator naming rows to open, and it already ignores the task tier's `stopped:` field for the same reason it skips `prior_wait()`. This change removes the subject's work from the **auto**-open leg and from adoption, not from an explicit operator batch. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/adopt-orphans.py`, `scripts/tests/test_standdown_auto_reopen.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_open_queue.py`, `scripts/tests/test_adopt_orphans.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.4

- fix: **a manager tab opened or resumed by a HEADLESS leg no longer registers as `sdk-cli`.** `wezterm cli spawn` forwards the CALLER's environment into the new pane (measured 2026-11-12: a spawn from a `cli` session reads `cli` back inside the pane, and the mux server carries no such variable), so a manager resumed by a headless leg came up with a real WezTerm pane and a headless entrypoint. Two consequences, both measured on five live goal managers at once: the session census charted them `invalid / headless` — it flags a manager whose mode is headless, because a manager cannot be headless — and `closer-gate-lint.py` read the same variable as its paneless test, so those managers' `Shift+Tab` and `/reload-plugins` closers were refused as unactionable asks. All five recipe copies now carry `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli` as a positive `env` assignment beside the writer path — `commands/open.md` ×3, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md` — set rather than added to the `unset` list, so the result does not depend on Claude Code deriving the entrypoint itself. ⚠️ The four docs sites that asserted the opposite mechanism (the pane inherits wezterm's *mux server* environment) are corrected: that claim is what made the four-variable `unset` list look sufficient, and it is false. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a `board_missing` row now hands over the log card id, so a mirror card can be answered end to end.** `answer_handover()` returned `""` whenever `store_item_id` was empty, and `normalize_store_item()` is the only writer of that field — so **every** `board_missing` row (they all come from `load_events()`) rendered no `answer:` line. The mirror routing shipped in `attention-answer.py`'s 404 arm (`mirror_gate`) was therefore unreachable: no operator-facing surface ever printed a mirror card's id. Measured 2026-11-11, and the bot review of the routing PR (`#810`) called the path unreachable independently. A `question` mirror now renders `answer (relay-only): … answer <log id>`, and the literal `relay-only` marker separates it from a store row's handover — a mirror answer is **relayed** to the producing session and never recorded, which is why `attention-answer.py:539-540` prints `(no store record)`.
  ⚠️ **A `permission` mirror gets no command at all.** `attention-answer.py:517` refuses one as permission laundering, so a handover there would be an action handed to the operator and then refused. The row instead names `answer_permission` and where its id comes from — `pending_permissions` / `GET /api/1.0/sessions` (`question.request_id`) — and prints **no id of its own**: `docs/permission-allowlist.md:242-243` says never take it from the feed, whose ids are store/log ids and answer `409 no pending request`, which reads exactly like "already answered". Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.3

- fix: **park-nudge gates on the watching duration, not the bucket.** `scripts/park-nudge.py` selected candidates with `if row.get("bucket") != "watching": continue`, so a session parked on a clock/`defer_date` with no background shell read `idle` and was never nudged — however long its `🟡 WAITING` episode had run. Measured 2026-11-12: **9 of 9** sessions over the 15-minute bound sat in `idle` (`watching_min` 22–817 min) and the live reading emitted `0 nudged` while those same rows were over the threshold. An `idle` row is now admitted whenever its `watching_min` is measurable, and the duration check decides; `gated` (an operator wait), `working`, `light` and `unclassified` stay excluded. `stats["watching"]` still counts the watching bucket only, so the summary keeps reconciling with the reading's `counts.watching`. Re-run on the live reading: `7 nudged` (was 0), including the 841-minute row. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.2

- fix: **the answer-path probe now refuses a spawner whose wrapper answer route has not been shown to work, instead of trusting an armed loop.** Stage 1 recorded the reaches and reported them; this flips the verdict onto them. `reach_allowed_age` is `2 × period + 180`, with the period read from the `period=<N>s` field `manager-liveness.py --state` already prints — never re-derived from the cadence file, which would be a second implementation of a fact that script owns. `reach_verdict` refuses on an absent, unreadable, untimestamped or expired wrapper reach, each with a quotable reason.
  ⚠️ **The `mcp` leg is reported, never gated.** The origin case was measured on the wrapper route, and a manager that answers parks fine there is not the failure this refuses — gating on `mcp` would refuse it for a route it may never use, and at flip time would have refused every manager whose `mcp` reach had not yet arrived.

## v0.302.1

- fix: **the answer-path refusal no longer tells a manager to open an agent row interactive.** `commands/open.md:211` offered one remedy for every role — *open the row `interactive`, or arm this session's manager loop and re-run* — and for a `role: agent` row the first is the outcome the fleet forbids (`docs/role-mode-matrix.md` Rule 2) and the cheaper one, so a refused spawner took it. Measured 2026-11-12: **24 of the 90** `role: agent` new-worker spawns opened as tabs in 24 h, every one `mode_source=argument`. The remedy is now role-aware, and its one home is the new `docs/role-mode-matrix.md` § Answer-path remedy: `role: agent` gets *arm this session's manager loop and re-run*, or a recorded `refused` for the tick when no loop can be armed; `human` / `manager` keep the interactive route. `commands/open.md:211` cites that section rather than restating it. The plugin's JS `ANSWER_PATH_REMEDY` constant gains the same split (`ANSWER_PATH_REMEDY_AGENT`, which the drill surface's refusal now uses) so the drill cannot drift, and `server/answer-path.test.mjs` pins both shapes. ⚠️ The bound supervisor is the Go `claude-wrapper` daemon, whose spawn path carries no answer-path guard — that refusal is a separate row, not this change. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `docs/role-mode-matrix.md`, `server/answer-path.mjs`, `server/answer-path.test.mjs`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.302.0

- feat: **one flag-provenance predicate for tasks, goals and topics.** An agent-set or absent `flag_set_by` now opens nothing at any tier. Before this, a **task** refused an agent-set flag (`flagged-rows.refusals()`) while a **goal or topic** accepted any provenance (`topic-spawn.flagged()`, `topic-keepalive`), and the round's own open leg read the flag with no provenance at all (`open-queue.is_flagged()`) — so an agent could flag its own goal and get a manager opened for it, silently, with the round printing `OPEN` and never `REFUSED`. `scripts/flag_provenance.py` now owns the decision and every open path calls it; `check-resume-flag-clause.py` follows the constant's re-export so the `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5 selector stays in sync. **Second half — unflagging now stands the manager down:** nothing read a flag going *off*, so an operator who cleared the flag kept the manager they were retiring. `topic-keepalive.py` writes a `<slug>.flagged` marker while a subject is admitted and, on the first later round that finds the page no longer admitted, prints the `manager-liveness.py --stop` stand-down and keeps the marker, so a round that skips the stop retries and the retry ends once the loop reads `STOPPED` — so the sweep classifies the row `stood-down` and no later round resumes it. Both topic-round verdict tables (`commands/topic-drive.md`, `agents/topic-drive.md`) carry the `stand-down` row, so a round following its own contract runs the stop rather than leaving the verdict unactioned. ⚠️ The rule is a **transition**, never the absolute state: measured 2026-11-12 in `private-personal`, of **84** goal pages carrying a `claude_session_id`, **64 carry no flag at all**, so an "unflagged + session id" predicate would have stood down 83 of 84 live managers. Operator ruling 2026-11-10, attention card `f8cd8b41`. Change set: `scripts/flag_provenance.py` (new), `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/flagged-subjects.py`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/check-resume-flag-clause.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flag_opens_callers.py` (new), the affected test modules, the `commands/`/`agents/`/`docs/` prose, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.301.0

- feat: **`wait-reprobe.py` has a round caller, so a session parked on a merged PR is re-driven on that evidence.** The join shipped in v0.284.0 with no caller anywhere in `agents/`, `commands/` or `scripts/` — `CLAUDE.md:202` called it *"the round's join"* while nothing invoked it, so no round ever produced its `REPROBED` block. `drive-retained.py render` now prints the block after `RETAINED`/`PARKED`, from one probe pass, and a `declared · resumed` row carries a `→ re-drive` marker — `expired` rows do not, because `declared-wait.py` has already ruled them not a wait, and the live store is mostly expired. The marker is the code-side handle the drive leg's prose `NUDGES` step consumes; without it, "re-drive the resumed rows" is a rule an agent is asked to remember rather than a fact the script states. A refused join renders `(refused: …)` rather than dropping the section. Measured 2026-11-12: `21 declared: 1 resumed · 1 still blocked · 19 unprobeable`, the resumed row being `bborbe/sun#133`. `agents/fleet-drive.md` now pastes the third block, revives a `→ re-drive` row (the only route by which a `parked-on-watcher` row is ever nudged), and records it as a `revive` entry so the ledger suppresses a repeat. Change set: `scripts/wait-reprobe.py`, `scripts/drive-retained.py`, `scripts/tests/test_wait_reprobe.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.300.1

- docs: **`fleet-surface.md` now states the decision-park resume rule.** The keep-alive leg's § 2b resumes a closed headless worker whose recorded wait has cleared, and the rule was written only in the step file and the agent body — `grep -c decision-park docs/fleet-surface.md` returned 0, so the doc a manager reads first did not mention the leg at all. The new section records the two wait kinds and their reads (approval later than the last transcript message; a non-empty `blocked_by` all `completed` with the latest `completed_date` later than that message), why ordering rather than presence is what makes the wait a consumable event (measured 2026-11-12: a row resumed at 23:39:17Z took a turn at 23:50:26Z and left the next round's candidates), that an empty `blocked_by` and a `hold` dependency are not cleared, that `unread` is a third answer, that the four decision counts live on the hand-back and in no payload file, and that the close belongs to `reap-idle-headless.py` on its own launchd tick rather than to this leg. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.300.0

- feat: **the fleet round now runs its SC1 and SC2 detectors.** `scripts/round-detectors.py` answers, for every session the sweep classified, whether a parked `pick` / `review:` / `you run:` / `approve:` closer resolves to a live card in the attention store (`NO-CARD` when it does not), and whether a `🔴 BLOCKED` closer naming no owner has a manager to route to (`ROUTING`). It composes the shipped scripts — `closer-gate-lint.py`'s single transcript scan and `owner_named()`, `operator-ask-card.py`'s session-keyed `verdict()`, `blocked-row-routing.py`'s `routing()` — and re-derives none of them. ⚠️ **One call per round, and the manager chain is built once.** A real `escalation-manager` resolution measured **387 s** on 2026-11-12 (`vault_index` 64 s, `colour_census` 37 s, `build_task_index` 87 s under load), so resolving per row would make a round take tens of minutes; `escalation-manager.py` gained `resolve_many()` (one grouping build, N lookups) and the chain is only built when a row needs routing. ⚠️ **The producer contradiction is resolved in the same change:** `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 8b renders the `DETECTORS` block into the digest, and `agents/fleet-drive.md` now COPIES a `ROUTING` record instead of running `escalation-manager.py` per blocked row — `commands/steps/drive.md` says so. A blocked row absent from `ROUTING` already names an owner and carries no `manager`. Measured 2026-11-12 on the live fleet: 17 checked → 14 carded, 2 `NO-CARD`, 1 `ROUTING`. Change set: `scripts/round-detectors.py`, `scripts/escalation-manager.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_detectors.py`, `scripts/tests/test_escalation_manager.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.299.0

- fix: **the tracked set refreshes on divergence, so a declaring row can no longer sit outside every sweep.** `manager-predispatch.py --compare-tracked` gains `--heal`: on the one shape where the caller's set is a provable strict subset of the gate's (`caller-scan-suspect` — gate-only names present, caller-only within the page's declaration) it writes the union, prints `REFRESHED tracked set <N> → <M> (+K): <names>` naming every row it added, and exits 0. **Every other shape stays report-only** — `caller-overshoot` and `both-sides-moved` can each mean the caller is right and the snapshot is a refresh behind, so a write there could delete a row that exists, and `caller-format` still refuses the round. Every `--heal` run also prints `TRACKED missing <N> · extra <M>` and, when the set is whole, `✅ TRACKED complete — <N> names, missing 0, extra 0`. Measured 2026-11-12 on the live vault: a goal manager's tracked file was last written at `14:09:48` while its gate snapshot refreshed at `16:29:12`, so 130 names stood against 136 and six declaring rows — including the regression-repair template defect behind 11 held rows — were invisible to every sweep for three hours while each check read the file and found it internally consistent. `commands/steps/goal-resolve.md` already required the declaration read to refresh on every firing and `--compare-tracked` already detected the gap on every firing; nothing wrote, because a prose step is not a producer. The write is now a code path that fires exactly when there is something to write and **never per firing**, so a quiet tick moves no mtime. The step's router trigger also stops reading as "manager start": row 1 of `commands/goal-drive.md` is `every tick (declaration read refreshes per firing)`, byte-identical to the step file's `When:`. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `commands/steps/goal-resolve.md`, `commands/goal-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.298.4

- fix: **`--tokens` measures the COMPLETED previous round, not the round it runs inside.** The current turn's transcript is written as the turn progresses, so a window ending "now" cannot see the round's own spend — a gated tick printed `spent: 0` however long it took (measured 2026-11-12). `--start` now carries `previous_round_started_at`, `--tokens` windows `previous_round_started_at → round_started_at`, and the first tick prints `n/a (baseline)`, never `0`. The spend-per-change line moves to the head (`commands/steps/round-readings.md` step 1b), before the sweep's snapshot, so `--count` reads the previous round's moved rows against that round's own `.prev` — if the order slips, numerator and denominator describe different rounds. One-tick lag, which SC3a permits. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.298.3

- fix: **the goal tier's sweep leg is gated on the frozen-tree predicate, and a gated tick's table always prints.** `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md` carried no predicate evaluation and no `--gate` — its only `manager-receipt` call was `--record --step sweep` — so a goal-tier round dispatched `supervisor:goal-sweep-reader` on **every** tick, frozen or not: the change that shipped the gate wired the readings leg and the *topic* sweep only, so its own SC2 could not pass on the tier it names. The step now mirrors `topic-sweep.md`: `manager-predispatch.py --predicate` at the leg's head, `--gate --step sweep --tier goal` and no dispatch when frozen, delegation unchanged otherwise, with the snapshot still taken so the tail's `--count` diff reads `0` rather than the previous sweep's movement. ⚠️ **The render half was broken too.** `manager-receipt.py --render-cached` dropped `lines[0]` unconditionally, which is the stale *marker* on the topic payload but the **top border** on the goal payload (`--write-payload` writes the box itself) — so every gated goal tick printed a box with no top edge. It now drops only what precedes the first `┌`. And a tick with no payload to replay prints the marker plus a full box built from the tracked set — the five-column goal frame (`Topic / Goal / Task | Session | Status | Phase | Met`, subject row first, rows indented) or the topic frame, per `--tier` — naming the absence, where it used to exit 1 and print nothing: a subject that had never swept printed no table at all, the failure `Manager Session` § Sweep output records from the first implementation. Change set: `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.298.2

- feat: **the cheap wake cadence ships as two launchd agents, so it survives a reboot and its death is reported.** The Fleet Pusher's cheap layer (`pane-wake.py` in send mode, no model turn, every 15 min) lived in `/tmp` and then in `~/.claude/scripts/` — unshipped, unsupervised, and gone on the next reboot or plugin upgrade. It now ships as `com.bborbe.fleet-pusher` (`StartInterval` 900) plus `com.bborbe.fleet-pusher-watch` (`StartInterval` 300), the second existing because **a tick cannot report its own death**: if the tick stopped, nothing inside it runs. Two defects found only by installing the stopgap are carried into the shipped version and pinned by tests — the launchd PATH had no `wezterm`, so every tick exited **0** and woke nothing for ~35 minutes while `launchctl list` read healthy, and the watchdog parsed the heartbeat with BSD `date -j -f "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"`, whose `%Z` rejects a literal trailing `Z`, and posted a false "unparseable" card for an eight-minute-old stamp. Also corrected here: the stopgap's comment claimed `pane-wake.py` "exits non-zero whenever any session is skipped" — it does not; skips are rc-neutral and the tick discriminates on JSON presence instead. Review round 2 closed a false-green of the same class the cadence exists to remove: a failed `session-liveness.py` read was discarded and parsed as `[]`, so a broken read rendered as "0 live session(s)", the tick exited 0 with a fresh heartbeat and the watchdog stayed silent; the tick now separates a failed read from an empty fleet (`TERMINAL:` + exit 1), and the pane-wake success test counts parsed JSON rows instead of matching a stray `{`. The watchdog also fails loudly when its own `attention-ask.py` post fails, and its header now states what it does *not* detect — a tick that runs and fails, which surfaces as a non-zero `launchctl` exit and a `TERMINAL:` log line. Review round 3 found the watchdog could not do its only job at all: `attention-ask.py` defaults `--producer-id` to `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, launchd provides none, and the post is **refused** — a dead cadence detected and reported to nobody. It now passes a fixed `--producer-id fleet-pusher-watch`, fails loudly when its own post fails, and stays quiet for 1800 s after a boot so a reboot is not reported as a dead cadence (residual: a long sleep is still not covered). Change set: `scripts/fleet-pusher-tick.sh`, `scripts/fleet-pusher-watch.sh`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-pusher.plist`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-pusher-watch.plist`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_pusher.py`, `docs/fleet-pusher.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a closer that names a pull request in words but carries no number is refused, so the wait can be re-probed.** `closer-gate-lint.py` gains a third, independent arm — `pr_without_number()` — beside the bare-`PR #N` one shipped in v0.288.0. That arm refuses a *number* the probe cannot resolve; this one refuses a slot that never gets as far as a number, which the number-shaped matchers cannot see: `` `bborbe/claude-supervisor` PR for branch `fix/x` reaching green `` names a repository and a branch and still yields `observables() == []`, so the round's re-probe pays a call to learn nothing. ⚠️ **The boundary is the probe's own patterns, mirrored** — `PR_OBSERVABLE` reproduces `observable-reprobe.py`'s `_BARE` / `_QUALIFIED` / `_URL` / `_HASH`, so a slot carrying any of them passes; in particular a bare `#973` with no `PR` word stays allowed, because widening to it is a different change and a false block on a compliant closer is worse than the gap. ⚠️ **A repo and a branch are not a PR reference** — the recorded shape carries both, so an arm that looked for an `owner/repo` span would pass exactly the declaration this exists to refuse. Measured 2026-11-12 over 329 live declarations: 50 PR-bearing, **24** of them observable-empty, and the newly-refused set equals that set exactly — 0 over-blocks.
